# ENVIRONMENT.md — environment variables that affect this repo

Scope: everything **this repo adds or repurposes**, plus the upstream variables and the non-ggml
runtime variables (HIP/ROCm) that materially affect how this delivery behaves.  Defaults are taken from
the `rdna-boosts` tree at the current release (`release.json`; every default stated here is the shipped
one, and a row that changed after the V2.0 cut names the `rNN` that changed it), and the table is generated
from the code, not from memory (see [Maintaining this file](#maintaining-this-file)).

## How to read this

| class | meaning |
|---|---|
| **Kill-switch** | the feature is **ON by default** because it passed its gate; the variable exists to disable it for A/B, bisection or a workaround. Setting it to `0` restores the old behaviour. |
| **Opt-in** | OFF by default; the feature is not gated or not for production. |
| **Tuning** | takes a number/string; the default is the validated value. |
| **Diagnostic** | no effect on the default path — it only logs, dumps, or gates a debug helper. Never set in production. |
| **Numerics selector** | does not enable/disable a feature: it picks between two *numerically different* implementations of the same op. A change moves every layer by ~1 ulp, i.e. it moves greedy text and MTP acceptance. A/B and bisection only. |

Two idioms are used in the code and they are **not** interchangeable:

* *presence-only*: `getenv("X") != nullptr` — **any** value (including `0`) enables it.
* *value-gated*: `getenv("X") != nullptr && atoi(...)` — `1` enables, `0` leaves it off.

The tables below say which one each variable uses, because guessing wrong is a classic time sink.

Every behaviour-affecting variable is also resolved **once** (into a function-local static or value) on
first use, so setting one after the process has started has no effect.  This is deliberate: `getenv()`
takes a lock and rescans the environment block on Windows, and several hot paths (per matmul, per
device allocation, per graph input, per batch) would otherwise read it thousands of times.  It also
avoids caching a `const char *` returned by `getenv()` across calls, which is unsafe on Windows because
the MSVC CRT reuses its buffer for the next `getenv()`.

**Since r217 this is enforced, not aspirational (issue #273 item 4).**  A run-time `getenv` counter
(an `LD_PRELOAD` interposer) found four reads that were genuinely per-op rather than per-process:
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_TYPES` (per matmul, 7 657-22 913 calls per run), `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_STATS` (7 681-15 552),
`GGML_STAGE_TRACE` (per tensor, 144-240) and `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_CHUNK_DEBUG` (per FA launch, 125-431).
All are latched now, and **no line in the delivery reads the same name twice** (129 did, in the
`getenv("X") ? atoi(getenv("X")) : D` idiom).  A new variable is read like this:

```cpp
static const bool on = [] { const char * e = getenv("GGML_CUDA_X"); return e == nullptr || atoi(e) != 0; }();
```

(or a file-local accessor when several functions need the same switch).  The audit found a second reason
to latch rather than re-read: the ROCm 7.14 `rocprofiler-register` calls `setenv()` during early init and
can reallocate the environment under a concurrent `getenv()` (ROCm issue #10196), which is why
`src/models/qwen4exp.cpp` gate-compiles qsa3 instead of gating it on an env variable.  A `static` inside a
**template** function is one copy per instantiation, which is the only multi-read that remains.

## The short answer: what you actually need

**Nothing.** Every feature the delivery ships is default-on, and this is deliberate policy (see
`AGENTS.md`, "Default-on policy"). You do **not** need to set anything for the recommended server or
`llama-cli` invocation. The variables below exist to *disable* things, to bisect a regression, or to
tune a value that has a validated default.

The only ones worth knowing by heart:

| variable | default | why you would touch it |
|---|---|---|
| `GGML_COMPUTE_BUFFER_MARGIN_PCT` | `10` (HIP only) | `0` disables the compute-buffer slack — the fastest way to reproduce the old TODO #42 abort |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` | unset = auto | `0` disables the MoE expert cache entirely (CPU expert path) |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB` | **on** | `0` disables the movable-boundary slab allocator (back to per-allocation `cudaMalloc`; reintroduces the TODO #42 server abort).  Since **r37** the slab is additionally **armed only for models with host-resident experts** (`-ncmoe`/`-cmoe` > 0); a dense model never creates one.  Since **r47** the arm is independent of `--fit` (issue #136): if the cache is enabled the slab is armed |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB` | `1152 + 128 x n_gpu` (1280 / 1408 / 1536 MiB; r206) | VRAM left outside the slab after it reclaims the unused reserve. **Raising it buys steady-state margin at the cost of arena**; `2048` aborts the 16k path inside hipBLASLt |
| `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE` | `auto` on Linux/HIP (r165) | force `hybrid` \| `internal` \| `nccl` \| `ce` \| `q8ce` for multi-GPU A/B (`q8ce` = the copy-engine chain with a **lossy** 8-bit wire) |
| `LLAMA_DROP_COMPUTE_BUFFERS` | **on for every tool** | `0` keeps the wide-prefill layout (smaller arena). Under the slab a later wide prefill reclaims it via a boundary move, so a server may drop too |
| `GGML_CUDA_OP_TIMING` | off | per-op GPU timing/profiling |

---

## 1. Compute buffers and the MoE arena

### 1.1 `GGML_COMPUTE_BUFFER_MARGIN_PCT` — **tuning**, default `10`, HIP/ROCm only

Pads every **compute** buffer allocation by that percentage (`ggml-alloc.c`, via the buffer type's
`get_compute_margin_pct`).  The graph allocator sizes the compute buffer from a *measure* graph, but a
runtime graph can carry a different live-tensor set (host-expert staging, MTP taps) and need a little
more — measured +3.3 %.  Growing it is a free-then-allocate-larger, so it needs a contiguous block
**bigger than the one just released**, which fails once the free VRAM belongs to the MoE arena.  Taking
the slack up front moves the cost to before the arena is sized.

* `0` disables it — and **reproduces the original abort**, so it is the natural A/B control.
* Cost, measured on 2× R9700 (`-sm tensor -ncmoe 48`): 1275 MiB of arena residency (63.8 % → 61.5 %),
  ≈127 MiB per percentage point.
* Only the RDNA/ROCm path opts in (`#if defined(GGML_USE_HIP)`); every other backend's allocation sizes
  are unchanged.  Read once per process.
* **Unchanged in r37:** the margin still applies to dense models (about 10 % of a now-small layout).  Only
  the **chunk** is slab-gated (`GGML_COMPUTE_BUFFER_CHUNK_MIB`); together with the r37 host-expert slab gate
  this is what restores a dense model's pre-slab compute-buffer sizes (issue #120).
* Compute-only: model weights, the KV cache and the arena are untouched.

### 1.2 MoE expert-cache arena

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` | unset = **auto** | tuning | sizes each device from its free VRAM. `0` disables the cache **completely** (CPU expert path) — prefer this over a tiny arena, which stands the cache-aware MoE fusions down and is ~8× slower than the cache-less path.  On an **integrated GPU** (shared host/device pool) **auto is refused** (r91): the cache is declined with one WARN and the run takes the host-expert path, because AUTO + the slab would map host RAM (see `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_UNIFIED`). |
| `--moe-cache-mib` (CLI, upstream `llama_moe_cache`) | unset = off | **works, not recommended** | the **upstream** cache engine, not this block's.  It was unusable from block 09 to r98 (the scheduler never invoked `sched->callback_copy`, so its slot map stayed `-1` and the cached layer's `MUL_MAT_ID` indexed the expert bank one expert below it: wrong values on gfx1201, an HSA fault on gfx1151); fixed in r99 (TODO #56).  It is correct now but slower than `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` here, because it synchronises the split backend once per cached layer per pass (35B-A3B `-ncmoe 40`: decode 50.1 -> 37.8 t/s), so use the delivery's own arena.  Not affected by `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_*` or `GGML_CUDA_SLAB`/`GGML_SCHED_STAGE`. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_IDENTITY_PREF` | `1` | kill-switch | **whole-table identity placement** (r117, block 09).  A table is on the decode band's identity fast path only at its FULL slot count, and a uniform slot count that lands one slot short (each table's arena is rounded up to the slab arena unit) drops *every* table off it — worth ~40 % of decode (46.7 -> 65.0 t/s measured).  On: when at least 96 % of the layers can have their full slot count, those layers get full residency and the coldest few are dropped to 0 slots (they run through the scheduler's host copy) instead of spreading the shortfall.  `0` restores the pre-r117 uniform placement (A/B and bisect only).  `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` still bounds the arena — the placement never exceeds the budget. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_UNIFIED` | unset = refused | **opt-in, APU only** | r91.  On a device whose "VRAM" is the same physical pool as host RAM (`cudaDeviceProp_t::integrated`), the expert arena is **off by default** and this variable is the only way back in: it requires an explicit `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` **and** an explicit `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_RESERVE_MIB`, so the slab is bounded by construction (`mapped = free - reserve`) instead of mapping the machine.  Ignored on a discrete GPU. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_RESERVE_MIB` | `1024` | tuning | VRAM held back from the arena for the compute side / KV cache.  The old server workaround raised this to `8192`; **that is no longer needed** (the r20 slack + the fail-soft yield cover it). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIN_MIB` | `0` | tuning | auto-sizing floor; below it the cache declines itself instead of building a useless arena.  Decided **early** (before the context exists, i.e. before any graph) since r24 -- a late `g_enabled = false` would plan the MTP draft and the target with different kernels.  If a config still trips it late, it logs an ERROR and keeps the small arena (correct, slower). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIN_RES_PCT` | `18` when auto | tuning | minimum residency worth keeping.  Live since r24 (it was silently inert while the early floor never ran). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_AUX_RESERVE_MIB` | unset = derived (r86) | tuning | **the draft CONTEXT reserve** since r86 (KV + narrow-2 compute + slack; the draft weights are already resident because the draft model is loaded before the target context).  Taken verbatim per device; unset/0 falls back to the derived value from `draft-reserve.cpp`.  Before r86 it was the whole draft footprint. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_SLOTS` | `0` | tuning | explicit slot count hint (0 = derive from the budget). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_PERIOD` | `32` | tuning | promotion/rebalance period, in tokens. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_FILL` | `1` | kill-switch | fill-on-miss. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_TOUCH` | `2` | tuning | `admit=touch`: uses since last resident before a fill is allowed (a first-touch expert is served from the pool's in-place cold read instead of being admitted).  **Measured 2026-11-07, two live-demote cells** (Flash-Next + MTP, `-c 204800 -ub 6144`, `-ncmoe 48`, `-lme 36` and 44, 3 + 3 interleaved reps, `-lv 4`): `3` damps churn exactly as intended (demotions **-17.3 %/-18.3 %**, sink uses **-27 %**) and buys nothing measurable -- decode **+1.3 to +2.2 %** at 93.8 % coverage (inside this box's spread), prefill flat -- while giving up **0.78/0.81 pt** of arena hit rate and serving **+35 %** more misses cold.  **Keep the default 2**; the knob is a churn damper, not a lever.  `archive/work/host-expert-parity/RESULTS-2026-11-07-touch.md`. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_PROVISIONAL` | `1` | kill-switch | provisional eviction during a pass. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_PREFILL_SEED` | `1` | kill-switch | seed the cache during prefill. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_PREFILL_SEED_N` | `0` | tuning | how many prefill seeds (0 = default). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_SEED` | **`eager`** | **opt-in (measured arm)** | r270, `TODO` #318: how much of the sizing fill (the arena seed AND the host-pool prewarm) happens up front.  **The catalogue entry, with the numbers, is in §20**: the fill is the prefetch, so `auto`/`lazy` move the same bytes into the request, and on a covering pool they cost the device remap. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_PREFILL_IN_PLACE` | `1` | **kill-switch** | **an identity (fully resident) expert table is served IN PLACE in the prefill band** (r258, block 12, `TODO` #133).  Above the decode/verify band the scheduler used to stage the whole table into a ring slot (pinned master) or gather it into the split input (host pool) on **every ubatch** — for a 100 %-resident arena that is the whole table read back out of VRAM: measured 63 648 MiB re-copied for a 5246-token prompt on three RDNA4 GPUs, serialised against the compute, which left 3-GPU prefill **slower than 1-GPU**.  On: the op reads the arena directly (`moe_cache_get_table`'s identity fast path, at any width), the scheduler skips the staging/gather for that input, and the prefill MoE fusions that read the expert table directly (MMB GLU / the fused MMQ gate+up / the MMQ swiglu→down fold) stand down for such a table so the per-op chain can redirect.  **Since r266 it also covers the ARENA tier's prefill band** (`TODO` #317): a partly-resident table is served in place too, by admitting its used-and-missing experts (empty slot -> the table's landing slots -> the coldest resident THE PASS DOES NOT ROUTE, ranked by the decode heat prior) and publishing the slot-remapped routing, so no resident is copied out.  A table that cannot fit (`|used| > slots`) is declined WHOLE and stages as before, which is why a wide `-ub 4096` prefill on a 78 %-resident arena is exactly inert.  `0` now selects the r265 staging path for BOTH tiers.  Worth +6.3 % prompt eval / +5 % decode on 122B Q8_0 `-lme 90` and +28.5 % on Q5_K_XL `-lme 75` (250-token prompt), identity of text, MTP acceptance and prefill logits, and no two-slot decoder disturbance (`benchmarks/2026-11-12-prefill-in-place-arena.md`).  **This is NOT the r40 in-place prefill read coming back** — that one was a device-side per-expert policy with a device map and a complement to fetch (see §1.2's r40 note); this one has no complement, no policy and no map, it is a base-pointer swap on a table whose arena IS the table.  Worth **+171 %** prefill on the pinned tier (1489.6 -> 4033.9 t/s) and **+13.5 %** on the default pool mirror (3588.8 -> 4072.4), out-of-band on a non-identity arena (2275.6 vs 2266.4, 1 GPU at 69.1 % residency); byte-identical greedy text, prefill KLD 0.000000 and identical MTP acceptance across the switch.  `0` restores the staged/gathered path (A/B and bisect only; the two arms read the same bytes, so a logit difference between them is a bug).  Records: `benchmarks/2026-11-10-prefill-in-place.md`. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_SIZE_AT_PRIME` | `1` | kill-switch | **size the arena at the warmup, not the first real decode, and stop the seed at the prefill reclaim boundary** (r75, blocks 09/10/11; developed as the unpublished r74 beta).  The warmup only *primes* each host table, so the deferred sizing used to wait for the first user decode and pay the whole arena allocation + H2D seed on the first generated token.  On (default): the priming pass sizes, `slab_extend_all` is skipped there (it over-mapped the reserve and a later graph capture OOM'd), and the arena is sized to the **full** region while the seed stops at `narrow_floor + ring + wide` -- the band a wide prefill immediately reclaims (the coldest, heat-ordered-last tables stay unseeded and fill lazily during decode).  Measured (maintainer's GSQ command, 13.8K prompt): arena 15666.6 MiB at load, 97/144 tables seeded, first-prefill eviction 6150 MiB (the band), re-arm restores the arena, decode 75.0 t/s.  `0` restores the deferred sizing. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP` | `1` | kill-switch | **persistent cross-run expert heat map** (r60, block 09).  On: the cache tallies a long-horizon per-expert use count (device-side on the prefill band, host-side on the eager decode band) and writes it to a sidecar next to the model; the next run seeds the arena with the hottest experts at sizing and allocates the hottest **layers** first (so the work-pool boundary reclaims the coldest).  Measured on `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=4096`: **+34 % decode over a 48-token turn** (38.15 -> 51.30 t/s, hit 0.767 -> 0.940, fills 11748 -> 2976) and **+10.3 % over 256 tokens** (48.53 -> 53.55 t/s, fills 19452 -> 6912); prefill unchanged.  It is a placement prior only (the expert bytes are unchanged), so the output is bit-identical.  `0` restores the exact pre-change behaviour.  `load` (aliases `ro`, `read-only`) loads and seeds from the map but never writes it.  A **read-only heat-map file** is also loaded and frozen: the file's mode is the freeze signal, and it does not fall back to a cache directory. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP_DIR` | unset | tuning | directory for the sidecar when the model directory is not writable.  Otherwise the fallback order is `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/llama-moe-heatmap`, `$HOME/.cache/llama-moe-heatmap`, `/tmp`. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP_DEBOUNCE_S` | `60` | tuning | minimum seconds between heat-map writes (the server flushes at turn end; process exit always flushes). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP_MAX_FAILS` | `3` | tuning | consecutive write failures before persistence is disabled for the session (non-fatal throughout). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP_DECODE_SAMPLE` | `64` | tuning | **sampled decode-band heat tally for a fully-resident (identity) table** (r110, block 09, TODO #64.2).  Every other table's decode routing is tallied *exactly* on the host (the eager path already reads it), but a fully-resident table takes the identity fast path, which never reads the routing back at all -- so its decode traffic contributed nothing to the heat prior.  This tallies it from the device entry, one op in `N`, **weighted by `N`** so the counts stay on the same scale as the exact tallies.  `0` disables it.  Cost: one atomic tick per decode op and, for a sampled op, one small kernel launch; the tally mirror is pre-allocated at sizing so nothing is allocated on the captured decode path.  Measurement-side effect only (the prior changes which experts are resident, never the bytes served). |
| `MOE_EXPERT_TRACE` | unset = off | **diagnostic** | **offline routing trace** (r110, block 09, TODO #61 P1): writes the raw routing of every `mul_mat_id` to this path, in a text format that `scripts/moe-trace-replay.py` replays to score cache policies (the shipped F-LRU, static top-f, a per-layer capacity quota, pass-start pre-admission) against a real workload.  DECODE records come from the scheduler hook, where the ids are already on the host (no readback, no sync); PREFILL records come from the device entry, because the staged prefill path never brings them to the host -- that costs one readback per prefill op (a prefill ubatch is never CUDA-graph captured, so it is safe).  A fully-resident (identity) table's decode band is not traced: it cannot miss.  It is a *diagnostic*, never default-on, and the replayer's absolute hit rate is a model of the cache, not the cache -- judge policies by their delta. |
| `MOE_EXPERT_TRACE_MIB` | `1024` | tuning | cap on the trace file, in MiB (r110).  Recording stops at the cap with one warning; the records already written are kept.  A 296-token prompt + 128 decode tokens on a 40-layer MoE is ~2 MiB. |
| `GGML_MOE_CACHE_MAX_TOK` | device MMVQ band (16 on RDNA4; 8 on RDNA3, NVIDIA) | kill-switch | caps the expert-cache **decode/verify band** (routed `MUL_MAT_ID` tokens the cache serves from the arena).  It defaults to the routed-expert MMVQ kernel's own band on the device since r31; `8` restores the pre-r31 band (band widening in `archive/work/moe-cache-band16/`).  A non-numeric value silently yields 1. |
| `GGML_MOE_DEVREMAP` | `1` | kill-switch | **sync-free decode-band device remap** (r203, PR #266, block 09; **tensor-split and covering-pool scope r221, TODO #269**).  The eager hook synchronizes the backend once per routed op to read the routing back (`ggml_backend_synchronize(input_backend)` + the ids readback's own sync), runs the residency policy and uploads the slot remap.  On: the scheduler offers the split input to the backend first and a kernel maps the device routing through a per-table expert -> slot map (`slots + e` = cold, read in place from the pinned master; `slots + pool slot` for a host pool that COVERS the table) straight into the table's `remap_dev`; the policy consumes that routing one call late and re-uploads the map only after a residency change.  Each expert is read from the same bytes as before, so the output is unchanged (byte-identical greedy text, identical MTP acceptance, prefill KLD 0.000000).  Worth **+8.4 % TG** on 35B-A3B `-ncmoe 40` (1 x R9700) and +14.1 % / +9.2 % at 4 / 8 slots (r203), **+9.9 %** on the same model with the default `-lme auto` **pool mirror**, and -- because the eager path it replaces synchronizes the WHOLE meta backend -- **+16.1 % / +28.4 %** under `-sm tensor` at 2 / 3 devices (r221; 64.0 -> 74.3 and 59.4 -> 76.3 t/s at `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=8000`), plus +5.0..6.0 % on `llama-batched-bench -npl 1/4/8` and +5.3 % under `--spec-type draft-mtp`.  Scope **by construction**: a cold source for EVERY expert (a pinned master, or a host pool that covers the tensor -- a sub-complement pool has no map it can express and declines), one unsplit table (`n_slices == 0`), no identity table, the decode/verify band only (`ne[2] <= band`), and after the eager path has sized the cache.  `0` restores the eager path (A/B and bisect).  Invariants: the map is rebuilt from `slot_dirty` on every slot mutation -- a stale map reads the wrong arena slot, and the cold encoding also depends on the slot COUNT; and a covering pool's expert -> slot map is **write-once after sizing** (the demand path never evicts a resident, and `pool_rerank_from_heat_locked` returns before any bookkeeping for a covering pool), which is what lets the one-call-late map be safe without a synchronize.  See §1.2's "device remap" note below. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_COLD` | `1` | kill-switch | **`--host-experts pool` reads a pool-resident miss in place** (r203, PR #266, block 09).  A pool-backed table has a pageable master, so the in-place cold read stood down and every decode miss was an arena fill even when the expert was already in the pinned pool.  On: such a miss is read cold from its pool slot through the pool's device alias (cold id `n_res + pool slot`, `moe_cache_get_cold` returns the pool base) -- the same bytes the fill would have copied.  Measured (pool arm, 1 x R9700, 35B-A3B): fills 21747 -> 15270 with 8445 misses served in place, **+4.8 % TG** (57.6 -> 60.4 t/s), text identical.  Safe because the demand path never evicts a pool resident, a retiring slot (`slot_state == 2`) is excluded, and the eager path it rides synchronizes the backend before the hook.  `0` restores the fill-every-miss arm. |
| `GGML_MOE_GATHER_ONCE` | off | diagnostic | gather each expert once per pass. |

> **Heat map (r60; unified naming r75).**  The per-expert heat map is **on by default** whenever the
> cache is active on host-expert tables (`MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` auto/positive and a `-ncmoe`/`-cmoe`
> model).  Its sidecar is a **single unified index per model**, `<base>.moe-heatmap`, where `<base>` is
> the model filename with the `.gguf` extension and any llama.cpp split marker (`-NNNNN-of-NNNNN`)
> stripped -- so every shard of a sharded GGUF shares one file (`Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-...-00001-of-00009.gguf`
> -> `Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-....moe-heatmap`), and an unsharded `model.gguf` -> `model.moe-heatmap`.  It is
> geometry-keyed, atomic (`tmp`+`rename`), debounced 60 s and non-fatal on failure; if the model
> directory is read-only it falls back to `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP_DIR` / the XDG cache.  It is
> a **placement prior only**: the expert bytes are unchanged, so a run's output is bit-identical and
> `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=0` reproduces the pre-change behaviour exactly.  The retired per-shard
> `<gguf>.moe-heatmap` naming is **never read or written**; a stale file in that form next to the model
> is ignored with a warning (delete it -- the map relearns from a cold start).  The in-run
> `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_PREFILL_SEED` remains as a second (weaker) seed source; the heat map is applied
> first.  **Freezing a map (r61):** set `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`, or simply make the heat-map file
> read-only (`chmod 400 <base>.moe-heatmap`).  A read-only file is loaded and used to seed, is never
> overwritten, and is not relocated.  See `patches/README.md` (block 09, r60/r61/r75) and
> `archive/work/moe-heatmap-persistence/`.  **Two bands, two counters (r114, TODO #88):** the map keeps
> a **combined** counter (prefill + decode) and a **decode-only** one, because the two consumers want
> different rankings.  The **arena** serves the decode/verify band, so its seed (`apply_heat_seed_locked`)
> and the heat-ordered layer placement rank by the decode counter; the **host pool** stages whole tables
> *for a prefill*, so its bulk layout (`pool_rank_experts_locked` / `pool_rerank_from_heat_locked`) keeps
> the combined one.  Before the split, one counter held both and a prefill ubatch (which routes ~`n_tok`
> experts) outweighed the decode by tens of times, so the arena was seeded from a nearly uniform
> distribution: measured on a real trace, the stored prior's top-112/table covered **80.3 %** of the
> decode mass where the same map restricted to its decode component covers **95.3 %** (the oracle's).
> On the GPU (35B-A3B Q4_K_M, 2 GPU tensor, `-ncmoe 40`, `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=4096` = 43.8 % residency,
> the *same* map in both arms): `h` **0.8747 → 0.8862** (284-token prompt) and **0.8698 → 0.8948**
> (5256-token prompt).  The sidecar format is **MHT2** (both arrays); a **MHT1** file still loads and is
> used for both counters, i.e. exactly the pre-r114 behaviour, so an existing map keeps working and simply
> re-learns the split as it is rewritten.  (`MHT2` is two counters per expert, so a map doubles in size.)
> **Host-pool warm order (r110):** the `--host-experts pool`
> pinned tier used to be warmed in expert-id order, because the eager `prefill_count` tally never runs
> under the staged prefill.  It now takes its order from the device prefill tally, folded in once at the
> first decode-band pass (`pool_rank_from_prefill_locked`); the prewarm log line says which source it
> used (`ranked by the prompt tally` / `ranked by expert id (no tally)`).  With `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=0`
> there is no tally and the historical id order remains.

> **REMOVED IN r40, narrowly RE-INTRODUCED IN r203 — read this before touching either.**  The 2026-10-10
> decision (released r40) deleted the *device-remap family* from the code base: `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_DEVMAP`,
> `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_DEVPOLICY[_SPLIT]`, `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_KSLOT`, `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_DEV_EAGER`,
> `MOE_HOST_POOL_POLICY`, `g_devmap`/`g_devpolicy`/`g_kslot`/`g_dev_eager`, the device mirrors
> (`slot_dev`/`count_dev`/`ghost_dev`/`last_dev`/`slot_prov_dev`/`policy_dev_t`), the device **policy**
> kernel, the KSLOT in-kernel slot lookup, deferred promotion, the device prefill tally / lazy seed, the
> cache-aware in-place prefill read, and the `moe_cache_promote`/`moe_cache_inplace` hooks (2684 deletions,
> `archive/work/moe-verify-fusions/FINDINGS-devmap-removal.md`).  That machinery is **not** coming back: it
> was a multi-session time sink, it made prefill slower, its decode advantage was unproven, and the
> divergence it was blamed for turned out to be the per-start hipBLASLt solution pick (issue #67, §44), not
> the map.  **Do not re-add any of it.**
>
> r203 adds a **deliberately minimal** device remap instead (`GGML_MOE_DEVREMAP`, PR #266), and the
> difference is the whole point: the *policy stays on the host* (the eager `access_locked` still decides
> every admission/eviction from the previous call's routing), and only the **slot-id lookup** moves to a
> kernel that writes `remap_dev` -- one small kernel, one per-table expert->slot map, one mapped-pinned lag
> buffer per table, no device policy state, no device mirrors, no KSLOT, no promotion, no prefill path.  It
> is confined to the decode/verify band and exists because the measurement is now positive and repeatable
> where r40's was not: **+8.4 % TG** (`-ncmoe 40`, 1 x R9700), prefill untouched (band-only), byte-identical
> text and identical MTP acceptance.  **r221 (TODO #269) finished the scope**: the Meta backend forwards the
> hook to each simple device, so a `-sm tensor` split gets its own per-device map (the eager path's meta
> synchronize is what makes the win grow with the device count: +9.9 % / +16.1 % / +28.4 % at 1 / 2 / 3
> devices), and a host pool that COVERS its tensor (the default `-lme auto` mirror on a mapped load) is a
> legitimate cold source, so the cold id is `slots + pool slot` there.  A sub-complement pool still declines
> -- a device-side map cannot express a miss whose expert is neither in the arena nor in the pool.  If you
> extend it, keep it host-policy-only and re-run the four arms in `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §82.

> The arena is the **lowest-priority** VRAM consumer: without a slab, any device allocation that runs short
> frees the largest table (then the whole arena) and retries.  With the slab on (the default, see §1.3) that
> path is *skipped* — freeing a table cannot return bytes to the driver once the slab owns them — and the
> allocation instead fails with a message naming `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_RESERVE_MIB`.  There is no variable for the
> yield behaviour, and it should stay that way.  See `ARENA-UB-TENSION.md` §13/§14.

### 1.2.1 Host-expert pool (`--host-experts pool`, blocks 09/12) — **no pinned master**

The pool is an **option** for systems that cannot hold the weights resident; the default `--host-experts
pinned` (and `auto`) keeps the usual behaviour (a full pinned `ROCm_Host` master) and allocates **no**
pool.  r35 shipped the pool as a bounded bounce tier *next to* that master; **r111 completes it**: in POOL
mode there is no master at all.  **POOL mode is only for the case where the host expert set does NOT fit in
RAM** — when it does fit, `pinned` is faster and cheaper on every axis, and POOL mode will be slower.

| tier | what |
|---|---|
| L1 | the VRAM expert-cache arena (the movable-boundary slab) |
| L2 | the bounded pinned host pool — the **only** non-swappable tier (`MOE_HOST_POOL_MIB`/`FRAC`), one per host tensor, process-wide budget |
| L3 | the read-only **model mapping** → page cache → the GGUF on disk |

The host expert tensors are placed in the model's read-only mapping (a dedicated per-device host buffer
type, `ROCm_HostMap`, which still carries its device so the scheduler keeps the `MUL_MAT_ID` on the owning
GPU).  **No device copy and no kernel reads that mapping**: every host read is served from a pool slot
(a `memcpy` out of the page cache, or a `pread` with `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1`), and when the pool declines
through a per-table pinned bounce ring.  The in-place cold read and the device-side gather therefore stand
down in this mode (they need a device-mapped master, issue #116) — a miss becomes a pool fill instead.

Measured (35B-A3B UD-Q4_K_M, `-ncmoe 40` = 18 662 MiB of host experts, 1x R9700): `RssShmem` **18.6 GiB
(`pinned`) → 6.8 GiB (FRAC 25)**, the difference sitting in reclaimable page cache; FRAC 50 pins 10.2 GiB
and FRAC 100 reproduces the old footprint, so **the pinning is the fraction knob**.  Under a 12 GiB or
8 GiB cgroup (`MemoryMax`, no swap) the `pinned` arm is OOM-killed and the `pool` arm runs at ~93 t/s —
that is the point of the mode: a host expert set larger than RAM streams from disk instead of failing.
Cost: one extra host memcpy per staged byte on the host-expert *prefill* staging path when the fills are
buffered (decode parity; `llama-cli` prefill −1…−6 %, a single-ubatch `pp512` on the 2-GPU tensor config
−34 %).  A non-mapped load (`--load-mode none/dio`) has no mapping to place the tensors in and keeps the
pinned master (warned); use the default mmap load mode.  r111.

**r113 — the pool is a LAYOUT, not a churning cache, and it is ranked by the persisted heat prior.**
`MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC` is a percentage of the **model's host expert set** (`-ncmoe`/`-cmoe` bytes), *not* of
system RAM, and it is inherently capped there (FRAC 100 = each table's pool is exactly its expert count,
i.e. the whole set pinned once — a pool can never be larger than the model needs).  Two consequences drive
the r113 change:

1. **What the pool HOLDS decides the disk traffic; the eviction policy barely matters.**  Measured offline
   on a real 10.5K-prompt routing trace with the model's own persisted `.moe-heatmap`
   (`scripts/moe-trace-replay.py --pool-frac 80 --pool-init sidecar-complement:<sidecar>`; arena 369 and pool
   409 slots per table, the field config's real geometry): pool content ranked by expert id (the pre-r113
   behaviour) = 3571 MiB of L3 (disk) reads over 451 decode tokens; the same pool ranked by the persisted
   heat prior as the arena's *complement* = **237 MiB**; an oracle prior = 237 MiB.  Every
   eviction/hand-over policy (no-evict, retire-on-promote, evict-LRU-and-admit, swap with a weak candidate,
   swap with the incoming's own slot) lands within **0–246 MiB** of that — i.e. noise next to the content
   effect, while each costs host↔device copies.  So the pool is laid out in bulk — as the arena's
   complement, ranked by the heat prior — at sizing (after the heat/prefill seeds), and again at the first
   decode-band pass (after the prompt's routing is folded into the heat vector).  The **demand path never
   evicts**: a resident expert is the source, a miss takes an EMPTY slot, otherwise it falls through to the
   per-table pinned bounce.  The background eviction-prefetch worker is gone with that (see
   `MOE_HOST_POOL_BGFETCH`).
2. **A pool that does not fit is worse than a small one.**  `MOE_HOST_POOL_RAM_PCT` (r113, default **85**
   since r114)
   caps the *whole* pinned pool at that percentage of `MemAvailable`, measured once at the first pool build.
   Without it, `frac %` of a large host set can exceed what the box can pin, and the failure is silent: each
   table's `cudaHostAlloc` fails, that pool is marked `failed`, and its reads take the bounce path (the
   disk).  The cap trims per table (with one WARN naming both knobs) and keeps the one-slot floor.

Measured on the gfx1201 field config (Qwen3.8-Flash-Next **IQ4_NL**, 2× R9700, `-ncmoe 48` = 64 800 MiB of
host experts, MTP `--spec-draft-n-max 7`, `--cache-ram 16384`, 70K-token prompt, 128-token decode, all on
the same build): the pool's hit rate went **0.7999 → 0.9183** and its evictions **4600 → 0**, and against the
`pinned` reference (836 t/s prefill / 57.5 t/s decode) the pool arm went from 581 / 30.6 (**DIO=0**) and
a prefill that could not finish in 27 minutes (**DIO=1**, 39 t/s) to **733 / 53.7** and **287 / 23.1**.  The
remaining `DIO=1` gap is the fraction of each table no pool tier holds (FRAC 80 leaves 20 %), which a prefill
ubatch re-reads per ubatch; it disappears under `DIO=0` (page cache) or FRAC 100.  Purity is unchanged: the
pool only chooses which byte-identical copy fills a slot (greedy text byte-identical old vs new, and
`MOE_HOST_POOL_VERIFY=1` is clean).

**r114 — the fill mode is chosen per box, and the pool-backed prefill stages in one launch.**  Two of the
three `TODO #87` follow-ups landed: `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO` became **`auto`** (see the table row) and the
pool-backed prefill staging fills every tier-resident expert with one kernel instead of one
`cudaMemcpyAsync` per expert.  Measured, 2x R9700 / 35B-A3B Q4_K_M `-ncmoe 40 --host-experts pool`,
70K-token prompt, `-ub 2048`, `-ncmoe 40`:

| arm | prefill t/s | decode t/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| pool, one-launch staging, `DIO` auto → buffered | **2103 / 2099** | 46.6 / 46.4 |
| pool, `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_BATCH=0` (the per-copy arm) | 1564 | 46.2 |
| pool, `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1` forced | 2088 | **22.7** |
| `pinned` (reference) | 2099 | 49.3 |

So the pool now stages prefill within 0.3 % of `pinned` and decodes within 6 %, and the `pinned`-only tax the
mode used to carry on a RAM-rich box (287/23.1 t/s at the shipped `DIO=1`) is gone.  Text is byte-identical
across every arm (`pinned` / pool / `DIO` 0 and 1 / staging on and off) and `MOE_HOST_POOL_VERIFY=1` is
clean — the pool only changes which byte-identical copy fills a slot.

**r112 — the defaults are tuned for the case the mode exists for.**  `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC` defaults to
**80 %** and, since **r114**, `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO` is **`auto`**: buffered when the host expert set fits
beside the pool in `MemAvailable`, O_DIRECT when it does not (so a box with the RAM to spare no longer pays
the disk tier's price by default, and a memory-tight box still never lets the page cache compete with the
pinned pool).  A memory-tight box cannot afford two copies of the same weights (pinned pool *and* page
cache), and a page cache that cannot hold the set only
buys swap thrash; 80 % keeps the working set hot and lets the disk serve the remainder.  Measured on the
field case (gfx1100, 24 GB VRAM, **30 GiB RAM**, 35B-A3B **Q8_0** = 37.8 GB, `-ncmoe 40` = 31.9 GiB of host
experts, 2000-token runs): pool 26.0 GiB pinned, **78.3 t/s decode**, 0.3 GiB swap — versus 48.6 t/s for
r111's 25 % buffered default and 2-3 t/s (16 GiB of swap) for `pinned` on the same box.  The knee is 80 %:
90 % measured 71.8 t/s *and* started swapping.  The cost is the first prefill: with nothing cached it stages
the whole set through O_DIRECT (~10 s on that box, i.e. a short prompt shows ~0.6 "prompt t/s"; a 1400-token
prompt measures 49 t/s).  On a box where the set fits in RAM, stay on `--host-experts pinned`.

**r234 — the prefill expert transport, per arm and per tier (`TODO` #272).**  Three transports exist for a
host-resident expert table's prefill, and which one a config gets is decided by the switches below, not by a
rule.  Measured on **1 x R9700 (gpu0, a Gen5 x8 slot)** with `Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GSQ-RCO-IQ3_S`, a
52 512-token prompt, `-n 1`, greedy, **balanced ABBA (A B B A) and 4 runs per arm** — quote the spread, not a
single run (see the last paragraph):

| tier | arm | switches | 1-GPU prefill |
|---|---|---|---|
| **pool (mirror)** | device gather (**default**) | — | `-ub 2048` **1066**; 5120 1564; 6144 1673; 8192 1791; 16384 1987 |
| pool | per-expert copy-engine staging | `GGML_SCHED_DEVGATHER=0 MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_BATCH=0` | 891; 1837-1849 (see the caveat below); 1721-2028; 1829-2133; 1996-2029 |
| pool | one-launch staging kernel | `GGML_SCHED_DEVGATHER=0` | the worst of the three at every width measured (-10 to -20 %) |
| **pinned** | staging kernel (**default**) | — | `-ub 6144` **1793** (phase-balanced) |
| pinned | no cache staging: one bulk H2D per table | `GGML_MOE_CACHE_STAGE=0` | **2023** (+12.8 %) |
| pinned | device gather | `GGML_SCHED_DEVGATHER=1` | 1493 (-16 %) |

**CAVEAT (added the same day, from the next session's re-measurement):** the pool tier's width band is **not
reproducible**.  With a palindrome-order protocol (each point sampled twice, symmetric in the sequence) the
gather led at every width measured — 4096 `1260.6/1261.4` vs `1121.5/1122.9` (+12.3 %), 4608
`1322.4/1321.7` vs `1225.1/1216.6` (+8.3 %), 5120 `1389.5/1384.2` vs `1331.3/1302.8` (+5.3 %) — i.e. the
`1837-1849` above (a previous session, same width) did not reproduce: the copy-engine arm moved ~29 % between
sessions while the gather moved ~11 %.  So there is **no width threshold to encode** and the gather stays the
pool tier's default at every width.  Full record: `WORKLOG.md` 261107.012 and
[`wip/expert-transfer-engine/`](wip/expert-transfer-engine/README.md).

So, on one GPU: the **gather is the stable default and wins at small ubatches** (a 20 % edge at `-ub 2048`,
where the routed set is a small fraction of the table); **from `-ub` 5120 up the copy-engine arm was ahead by
10-18 % in the r234 session** (not reproduced — see the caveat) and at 16384 the two tie; the **one-launch kernel should not be selected on either tier** (it
monopolises the CUs and starves the compute kernels — the r114 rationale was a *pageable* master paying a
bounce memcpy per copy, which a pinned master and a pool alias do not pay).  The archived "the gather wins by
8192" does **not** reproduce (the copy-engine arm was ahead in 4 of 4 runs at 8192).  **The defaults were
deliberately not changed** (`archive/work/host-expert-transport/RESOLUTION.md` §3): the band needs a width gate
whose lower edge (between 2048 and 5120) is unmeasured, it couples two switches, and every copy-engine arm is
**bimodal**.

**The engine was almost merged, and the box says no (2026-11-07).**  Making the per-expert copy engine the
only staged engine (deleting `moe_cache_stage_kernel`) measured **+15 %** on a pinned master at 1 GPU in one
run-to-run phase and **-8 %** in the other, byte-identically: the **engine ranking reverses with a box-level
phase** whose cost is proportional to the number of DMA operations a transport issues (gather, a kernel read:
±0.1 %; one-launch kernel: 6.5 %; one bulk upload per table: 17 %; 512 copies per table: 32 %).  That phase
is **ROCm/SDMA dispatch latency** — a trace shows the slow phase at 67 % GPU-busy against 85 % with the copy
engine *less* busy, and `HSA_ENABLE_SDMA=0` removes it (stable, but at the slow level) — so it is not fixable
in the delivery and not attributable to the lazy-mode/PLE page cache (`auto`, `off` and `mlock` oscillate
identically).  The merge was withheld; the shipped engines are the low-DMA-count ones (the one-launch kernel
on the pinned tier, the gather on the pool tier) and `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_BATCH=0` remains the A/B arm that
wins where SDMA is behaving.  Records: `WORKLOG.md` 261107.012, `INVARIANTS.md` (r234, second addendum),
`archive/work/host-expert-transport/README.md` §6c.

**Judge a transport with `A B B A`.**  Identical consecutive runs of one unchanged arm on this box alternate
between two levels with period 2 — measured **1873 / 2177 / 1865 / 2180 t/s**, amplitude 16 % on the pinned
bulk arm and 17-32 % on the pool copy-engine arm, while the gather moves ±0.1 %.  Cause unidentified (not
paging, not `MemAvailable`, not the arena/slot geometry, not THP accounting).  A plain `A B A B` therefore
reads as a ~6 % win for whichever arm sits in the even positions, and a block-ordered set of reps drifts
monotonically by up to 16 %.

| variable / flag | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--host-experts pool` | `pinned` | option | select the bounded host pool and **drop the pinned master** (r111).  `pinned`/`auto` = full pinned master, no pool; `mmap` was removed in r33. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_MIB` | `auto` | tuning | process-wide pool budget: unset/`auto` = `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC` % of the MoE host expert bytes (`moe_host_expert_bytes`), split over the host tensors in proportion to their size; `N` = explicit MiB; `0` = disable the pool (with `--host-experts pool` the master is then still dropped and every miss takes the pageable-free bounce path). |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC` | **`80`** (r112; `25` in r111) | tuning | the auto percentage (1–100) of the **model's host expert set** (`moe_host_expert_bytes`), per table, capped at that table's expert count and by `MOE_HOST_POOL_RAM_PCT`.  **It is the NOT-FITTING band's knob: in the mirror band it is not read at all** (r256) — when `-lme auto` fits the whole set under `MOE_HOST_LIMIT_FRAC`, the pool mirrors the set (every table at its full slot count, `h=1.0000`) and FRAC 30/50/95 measure identically (1233.6 / 1240.9 / 1234.5 t/s, `benchmarks/2026-11-10-devgather-coverage.md` §2); the same applies to the deprecated `--host-experts pool` spelling, which is `-lme auto`.  Coverage is the pool's decode lever (122B-A10B, `-ncmoe 48`, 1 x R9700: 17.76 t/s at 80 %, 20.59 at 100 %, against `pinned`'s 22.34), **but 100 is deliberately NOT the default** (r207): the RAM ceiling does not yet reserve the model's own pageable footprint, so on a multi-device split 100 % pins enough to starve the page cache and measured **9 % slower than 95 %** (4 GiB less pinned, 42 GiB less disk per run, 3 x R9700), it over-committed a 91 GiB box once (a stalled prepopulate), and the 1-GPU srv cell measured 100 % *worse* than 80 % (624.5 vs 646.8 prefill).  Raise it deliberately, per split, when the ceiling reserves the model. |
| `MOE_HOST_LIMIT_FRAC` | **`80`** | tuning | r210: the `-lme auto` ceiling, as a percentage of **`MemAvailable`** — *not* of the host expert bytes (that is `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC`, a different question).  `auto` spends the whole host expert set on the pool when the set fits this ceiling (the pool is then a MIRROR of the set: `h=1.0000`, zero evictions, no page-cache re-read); above it, `auto` keeps the historical `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC` layout, i.e. the not-fitting band where the page cache / `O_DIRECT` is the source and nothing is hard-pinned.  Lower it to make a box cap the pinned tier earlier; it is the one number that decides "fits, so mirror" vs "does not fit, so bounded".  **r211 trap**: in the not-fitting band this knob IS the pool budget (`auto_cap` = this % of `MemAvailable`), and it **overrides `MOE_HOST_POOL_MIB`** -- so the old way of forcing the band, `MOE_HOST_LIMIT_FRAC=1`, now sizes an 812.9 MiB pool (11 slots/table) and every arm measured with it is a tiny-pool arm.  Use `-lme 64` (the whole set as a pool) or `-lme 8` (a sub-complement budget) for those A/Bs. |
| `MOE_HOST_TIER` | unset | diagnostic | r210: `pool` / `tensor` force the host-expert tier for an A/B — `pool` = the pinned per-expert pool (the shipped mechanism, `-lme`'s default), `tensor` = the whole-tensor `hipHostMalloc` masters (what `--host-experts pinned` uses).  Unset = the policy chooses. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_COLD_MULTIDEV` | **`1`** (r210) | kill-switch | a pool-resident expert is read **in place** by a device kernel at any device count, not only on one GPU.  The historical restriction (`device_count == 1 && src_off == 0`) was a geometry accident, not a property of the pool: `base_dev` is already the cross-device fill source, and a tensor split's slice offset is folded into the cold base exactly as it is for a pinned master.  Set `0` to restore the single-device/single-slice restriction (the A/B arm).  Measured: `-lme 8` 48.9 → 54.5 t/s decode, the not-fitting band 51.8 → 57.0, at 2 devices, byte-identical text. It is also the predicate the r221 decode remap's pool cold source rides, so `0` turns that off with it (the remap then declines to the eager path).  |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_RAM_PCT` | **`85`** (r114; `80` in r113) | kill-switch / tuning | **r273: the ceiling applies to a DERIVED budget only.**  The budget's PROVENANCE decides, and the loader reports it (`explicit_abs` on the budget setter):
  * **explicit absolute** — `-lme N` (and `MOE_HOST_POOL_MIB=N`) — is **honoured in full and never scaled**: the operator typed a number, and the CLI refuses it only **at or above 100 % of `MemAvailable`** at parse time (r273: it used to refuse above 90 % while rounding the allowance to whole GiB, so `-lme 75` was rejected with a message claiming 80 was allowed — both the threshold and the arithmetic were wrong).  Between 85 % and 100 % it is accepted with a WARNING naming the exact figure and percentage.  A second, silent, proportional shave over that number was the memory-percentage system overruling a deliberate reservation, and it cost the one thing the number was chosen for: `-lme 70` against a 54 750 MiB host expert set on a 91 GiB box became **52 183.6 MiB = 95.3 %** with no WARN, which is the band where the arena exchange is engaged and (`-sm tensor`) can never demote.  A refused `cudaHostAlloc` on an honoured budget is loud and self-reporting (one WARN, then the bounce ring), which is the right failure mode for a number the operator chose;
  * **derived** — the bytes `-lme auto` resolves to, `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC`, `MOE_HOST_POOL_COV_PCT`, and a non-CLI consumer that enables the pool with no byte budget — keeps the ceiling, which is what it was written for: a percentage of the model's host expert set says nothing about whether this box can PIN it.
  The pool logs its **RESOLVED** layout once, at WARN, when the first table is built — coverage %, mirror/near-mirror/complement, exchange engaged or inert, and the budget CLASS that produced it (naming the scale when the ceiling bound) — because the loader's placement line can only state the request; it runs before any pool exists.  Ceiling on the **whole** pinned pool, as a percentage of `MemAvailable` measured once at the first pool build.  Raising it 80 -> 85 is a maintainer decision: the ceiling binds **only** on a memory-tight box (on the 184 GiB primary box it is 147 GiB against a 14.9 GiB pool, i.e. inert), and there it only decides how much of the set gets trimmed.  The reference point is the same box's FRAC sweep (r112): FRAC 80 pinned **26.0 GiB** with 0.3 GiB of swap, FRAC 90 pinned 26.4 GiB and was 9 % slower — 85 % of that box's `MemAvailable` (~29 GiB) allows ~24.6 GiB, i.e. a trim that stays below the pinned total it already tolerated, so the ceiling is on the safe side of a measurement.  **It has not been re-measured on the tight box with the r114 binary**; if a soak is wanted, run the field command there with `MOE_HOST_POOL_RAM_PCT=80` vs `85` and watch `VmSwap`, because the interaction with `MOE_HOST_POOL_FRAC` is the untested part.  Bounded (1–100); `0` disables the ceiling (the pre-r113 behaviour, which can silently fail every `cudaHostAlloc` for a table and drop it to the bounce path).  The clamp trims per table and WARNs once, naming both knobs; the one-slot floor per table is kept, so the pinned total can exceed the ceiling by ~`n_tables x host_bytes`. **r207: the ceiling is a DISTRIBUTION, not a tail shave.**  It used to cut the *registration-order tail* to the one-slot floor (122B, `-ncmoe 48`, `RAM_PCT=70`: 26 of 144 tables at ONE slot, 10.94 t/s); it now compares the process-wide plan with the ceiling once and scales **every** table's slot count by that factor (the one-slot floor only where the ceiling cannot cover a slot).  Measured: `RAM_PCT=70` **10.94 -> 17.44 t/s** (+73 % with `MOE_HOST_POOL_SIZING=1`: 18.92) at identical pinned bytes, prefill 343 -> 598; `RAM_PCT=85` **19.52 -> 20.97**.  Nothing changes where the ceiling does not bind.  **Windows (r209)**: the figure is `ullAvailPhys` from `GlobalMemoryStatusEx` (free plus standby, the `MemAvailable` counterpart); before r209 it was unreadable, so the ceiling did not apply.  **Windows, r223/r227/r233**: the pinned ceiling there is NOT `MemAvailable`.  WDDM charges pinned host pages against the adapter's **shared (aperture)** segment, and the published model for that is roughly `total RAM / 2 / visible GPUs` (ROCM-33542 / rocm-systems#14578, ROCm/TheRock#9339: ~13.4 GiB on a 27.6 GiB box with one adapter, ~6.5 GiB with two), so a plan above it fails the tail pools and then every bounce entry.  **That model is refuted as a predictor by the issue #274 reporter's own measurements (r227)**: shared usage stops at **9.32-9.35 GiB** on a 47 GiB box *and* on the same box after it was upgraded to 64 GiB, with one visible adapter and ~60 GiB of commit headroom, i.e. it neither scales with RAM nor with the formula.  **r233 found the missing variable: it is the loaded HIP runtime, not Windows.**  On issue #278's box `amdhip64_7.dll` came from the Adrenalin driver in `C:\Windows\System32` (10.0.3661.0); with the ROCm/TheRock 10.1 runtime (10.0.3684.0) dropped beside the same r229 binary the pinned tier grew to **16 004 / 40 078 MiB** with **141/141** host tensors and **0** failed fills, where the driver runtime latched at ~9.3 GiB.  The delivery now prints the loaded HIP runtime (module path + `hipRuntimeGetVersion`) at startup and WARNs when it is the system-directory driver copy; `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN=0` closes the tier on a box that cannot pin.  No ceiling is derived from the figure here on purpose: the first refused `cudaHostAlloc` latches the pinned tier off with one WARN naming the platform cause, and r227 makes the exit census report the **pinned tier's realized total** plus, on Windows, the adapter's aperture `CommitLimit`/resident (`ggml_hip_win_shared_mem_info`, the same `D3DKMTQueryStatistics` call the WDDM free-memory path uses).  Lower this value (or `-lme`) on Windows, install the ROCm/TheRock runtime beside the executable, or make one GPU visible. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_SIZING` | `0` | opt-in | **r207 (block 12 amendment):** `1` = per-table pool slots from each table's routing **concentration** (`cov_t` = the slots a table needs to hold `MOE_HOST_POOL_COV_PCT` of its own prior mass) fitted to the same total byte budget, instead of a flat `FRAC`.  A per-table heat *mass* is degenerate (a top-k router gives every table the same routed mass); concentration is not.  Measured at identical pinned bytes: **+7.5 %** decode at `FRAC` 80 and **+16.4 %** at `FRAC` 60, for **-13/-15 % prefill** (the pool is ranked for decode and a prefill ubatch routes nearly uniformly).  `0` restores the uniform rule. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_COV_PCT` | `90` | tuning | the mass fraction `cov_t` targets when `MOE_HOST_POOL_SIZING=1`. |
| `LLAMA_HOST_MASTER_PINNED` | unset | A/B only | force the pinned master even with `--host-experts pool` — the control arm for a measured comparison. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_POLICY` | — | **removed (r40)** | the pooled-table device-policy selector.  The device-policy fill (and the whole device-remap family) was deleted 2026-10-10; pooled tables always fill from the pinned pool (a master fallback only exists in `pinned` mode).  The variable is ignored. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_ALLOC` | **`thp`** (r215; `portable` in r214) | kill-switch / A/B | the pool's **memory type**, i.e. how the pinned mirror is allocated and released.  `thp` (default, r215) = an anonymous `mmap` + `MADV_HUGEPAGE` (2 MiB pages) region pinned with `cudaHostRegister(Portable\|Mapped)` **after** the mirror has been filled, released with `cudaHostUnregister` + `munmap`.  The old pair (`portable` = `cudaHostAlloc(Portable\|Mapped)`, the r214 shape) pins page by page, so a 48 GiB mirror costs **5.4 s** of pure pinning and pins a zero page for every 4 KiB page the fill then overwrites; `thp` makes that ~1 ms plus a pin folded into the fill (measured, Flash-Next IQ3_S, `-ncmoe 48 -lm mmap`, cold page cache, 1 device: ready 26.3 s -> 15.2 s, `none` 16.7 s).  `plain` = `cudaMallocHost`, `mapped` = `cudaHostAllocMapped` are the other historical arms.  **Set `portable` to restore the r214 behaviour.**  `cudaHostUnregister` ALONE does not give the pages back on this stack (the pin is tied to the VMA -- `archive/work/host-expert-eager-push/` P4 §5, 2.0 of 256 MiB re-readable), hence the `munmap`; verified: `AnonHugePages` 48.97 GiB while running -> 0 after teardown, `MemAvailable` back to baseline.  Recycling a pool slot is index bookkeeping inside that one allocation, so it never pins or unpins: 144 registers / 144 unregisters over a run with 35 559 slot fills. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_BULK` | **`1`** (r215) | kill-switch / A/B | **r273: the mirror fill is O_DIRECT whatever `--load-mode` says** (see `MOE_HOST_POOL_BULK_DIO`).  `1` = a pool that covers a whole host tensor (a **mirror**, `slots == n_experts`) is filled with **one sequential read of the tensor** straight from the GGUF, because its memory is one contiguous `n_experts * host_bytes` allocation and the GGUF expert layout is expert-major at the same stride (slot `e` == file offset `file_offs + e*host_bytes`).  `0` = the per-slot fill (one `host_bytes` `memcpy`/`pread` per ranked slot), the r214 behaviour and the A/B arm.  The mirror's fill ORDER is content-free (every expert is resident either way); the heat prior still decides the layout whenever the budget is smaller than the tensor.  Measured (`-ncmoe 48 -lm mmap`, 48 GiB mirror, cold page cache): **10.8 s -> 7.4 s** (4.5 -> 6.5 GB/s, the rate the direct `--load-mode none` load gets), and it stops faulting a second 48 GiB into the page cache that cannot be kept beside the pinned mirror.  Not applicable to a partial pool. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_BULK_DIO` | **`1`** (r273) | kill-switch / A/B | the transport of the one-shot **placement** read (the mirror fill).  `1` = **O_DIRECT, always**, whatever `--load-mode` names, because the placement read is not a cache miss: the pinned pool is not the read buffer, so a `pread` still materialises the whole set in the **page cache** first and only then copies it into the pool.  Measured (35B-A3B Q8_0, `-ncmoe 40 -lme 40` = a 32 640 MiB mirror, cold cache): page-cache high-water **+3.9 GiB** on the direct tier against **+33.7 GiB** buffered (the whole set, twice over), for 6.8 s against 4.9 s of fill time.  On a box whose pool + set + PLE does not fit that second copy is the difference between running and thrashing, and it cannot be reclaimed afterwards: the expert tensors ARE the pool-backed mapping, and `posix_fadvise(DONTNEED)` skips mapped pages.  r262 broke this by making the fill mode follow `--load-mode`; the transport knob is a DEMAND-read policy.  `0` = the buffered arm (the A/B), and a platform/geometry that refuses O_DIRECT falls back to it mid-stream with one WARN rather than failing the fill. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_PREWARM` | `1` | kill-switch | one-shot slot fill when a pool is built; **r273: it stops when the box is out of headroom** — a pool whose placement would leave less than `MOE_HOST_POOL_PREWARM_RESERVE_MIB` of `MemAvailable` is DEFERRED (its slots exist, the experts arrive on demand), because pinned memory can never be given back.  Otherwise: one-shot slot fill when a pool is built, ranked by the persisted heat prior (then the prefill tally, then expert id — r113).  The layout is re-derived as the arena's complement once the arena is seeded, so the prewarm's order is only a warm start. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_PREWARM_RESERVE_MIB` | **`1024`** (r273) | tuning | the `MemAvailable` floor the prewarm keeps for the rest of the process: a pool whose placement would leave less than this is DEFERRED and grows on demand (`MOE_EXPERT_SEED=auto`'s argument applied to the one resource the policy cannot create).  `0` disables the gate.  This is what makes `eager` mean "fill up to what the box can hold" instead of "fill the whole budget and let the box swap". |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_BGFETCH` | — | **removed (r113)** | the low-priority background eviction-prefetch worker.  The demand path no longer moves an arena victim into the pool (measured: the hand-over cost 2x decode for no L3 benefit, because the pool's CONTENT is what decides the disk traffic), so there is no eviction to prefetch.  The variable is ignored. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO` | **`auto`** (r114; `1` in r112, `0` in r111) | tuning / A/B | **(r227) the disk tier exists on Windows too.**  It was a stub returning -1 there until r227; it is now `CreateFileA` + positional `ReadFile` + `_aligned_malloc`, with `FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING` for the DIO mode and `FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED` always.  In the **memory-tight band** (`pool + host expert set > MemAvailable`) Windows takes the direct tier even when `--load-mode mmap` asked for the page cache, because a mapped-view fill there is a demand fault per page: the reporter measured ~250 MiB/s of disk traffic, ~65 k pages-input/s and the GPU computing ~2 % of a 4,180-token prefill, with the whole expert set re-faulted per pass; `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=0` forces the page cache back.  Elsewhere the r212 mirror of `--load-mode` is unchanged.  **(r219, issue #274) the disk tier is refused with a WARN on a platform that has no pool file I/O** (`MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO_FAULT`, or a build without the helpers), so it can never be half-applied; the same platform rule makes the pool's fill, the bounce ring and the demand fill fall back to the model mapping instead of failing.  The disk tier was never a correctness requirement: the pool's master IS the read-only model mapping, so every fill has a mapping source.  **r212: `auto` MIRRORS `--load-mode`.**  The pool fills the way the model was loaded: a mapped load (`mmap`, `mmap+mlock`, `auto`->mmap) fills through the **page cache** (a `memcpy` out of the model mapping), and `--load-mode dio` gets O_DIRECT -- except that a pool only exists on a **mapped** load at all (its master IS the mapping; with `dio` the plan says "NOT APPLIED: a pool needs a mapped load"), so in practice auto = buffered, and O_DIRECT fills are an explicit request.  `--load-mode` IS the operator's caching decision, and silently bypassing it was both a surprise and slower: measured on a 113 GB Flash-Next at `-ncmoe 48 -lme 60` (2 devices), the old MemAvailable heuristic chose O_DIRECT because `pool + host set > MemAvailable`, and the population -- the only read that cannot be avoided -- became **97 692 storage reads = 95.20 GiB at 2 785 MiB/s (35.8 s)**; the same run buffered is **97 692 mapping memcpys = 95.20 GiB at 4 062 MiB/s (24.8 s)**, identical bytes, zero drive traffic, and the pages stay for a re-arm/stand-down/second load.  The mirror still states the trade-off at the moment it is taken: when MemAvailable cannot hold the pool AND the host expert set, the decision line says so and names `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1` as the memory-tight arm (that is the arm r112 tuned for a box whose set overflows: 18-19 vs 3.2-3.3 t/s).  The old heuristic survives only for a consumer that never reports a load mode (non-CLI), and `0`/`1` still force either arm for an A/B.  Buffered vs forced O_DIRECT is value-identical: `gate-prefill-logits.sh --extra "-ncmoe 40" --ab "MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1"` measures mean KLD **-0.000000 / 100.000 %**.  **r231 (56 GiB rig, `radix`): the fill mode is the biggest *prefill* lever below a mirror, and the answer depends on the tier.** At `-lme 44` (93.8 %) with the demote on, `DIO=1` measures **1 329 t/s first-pass 50k prefill against 735 buffered (+81 %)**, decode unchanged (75.9 vs 76.2), for 2.1x the bytes (32 vs 15 GiB per pass) -- and its band-transition tail is 1.6 s against 22.1 s.  Without the demote the same pair is 1 699 vs 1 191 (+43 %) at -16 % decode.  At `-lme auto` (97.9 %) buffered wins: 75.6 decode against 67.7, and 2.1 GiB of reads against 11.2.  So: **a sub-complement budget should set `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1`** (or `--load-mode dio`), a near-mirror budget should not.  **r232 supersedes that for the buffered arm**: it coalesces now (see `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_SPAN`), and on the same rig at `-lme 12` the buffered arm measures **821-875 t/s against 631-663 for `DIO=1` with its span** -- while decoding **61 t/s against 26** -- so `auto` = buffered is the fastest mode at a sub-complement budget and the direct tier is not a default to reach for |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_SLICE_READ` | **`1`** | kill-switch | **r261 (`TODO` #132): a `-sm tensor` fill reads only THIS device's slice of the host expert, not the whole expert.**  Each device owns a slice of every host expert, and for `gate`/`up` it is *contiguous* inside the expert (`host_pitch == 0`, so this device's bytes are `src_off .. src_off + expert_bytes`) while the consumer copies exactly that run; the fill used to read all `host_bytes`, so every cold expert was read at **double size**.  `1` reads `expert_bytes` at `file_off + expert*host_bytes + src_off` into the pinned entry at `src_off` (so `copy_slot_from_src`'s `base + src_off` stays correct); a **strided** slice (`host_pitch > 0`, an axis-0 `down_exps` split) or a **fused** table (`n_slices > 0`) keeps the whole-expert read, because its slice is not one run and one sequential read beats `rows` strided ones.  Measured (122B-A10B Q4_K_XL `-ncmoe 48 -lme 54`, `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1`, `-n 1000`, same binary): 2-GPU x8 pair **18.98 -> 20.12 t/s decode (+6.0 %), 844.9 -> 917.8 prefill (+8.6 %), 193 221 -> 173 354 MiB disk (-10 %)**; 3 GPUs **48.96 -> 49.33 decode, 1126.9 -> 1406.8 prefill (+24.8 %), -16 % disk**; **inert at 1 GPU** (one device owns the whole expert, so both arms read the same bytes).  Purity: greedy text byte-identical across the arms *and* against the pre-change binary, and `gate-prefill-logits.sh --record --ab "MOE_HOST_POOL_SLICE_READ=0"` (35B-A3B Q8_0, 3 devices, `-ncmoe 48`) reads mean KLD **-0.000000 / 100.000 %**.  `0` is the old whole-expert read (A/B and bisect).  It changes nothing about *which* expert is read or where it lands: the bytes are a subset of the same expert.  Record: `benchmarks/2026-11-11-host-pool-slice-read.md` |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO_FAULT` | unset | **diagnostic** | (issue #274, r219; covers Windows since r227) makes the pool's file I/O report "not available on this platform" on **any** platform, i.e. `pool_open_file` returns -1 and the demand fill/bounce/pool all fall back to the model mapping.  With it set, `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1` is refused (buffered fills through the model mapping) and `gate-host-pool.sh`'s `nodisk` arm exercises the fallback.  Never set it in a real run: it exists so the fallback path is reachable from a Linux gate (there is no Windows runner) instead of only from a reporter's log. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN_FAULT` | unset | **diagnostic** | (issue #278, r229; root cause named in r233) emulates the platform's **latched pinned refusal** -- what a Windows box does when the loaded HIP runtime refuses a `cudaHostAlloc` against the adapter's shared-(aperture)-segment budget (measured there: nothing more can be pinned past ~9.3-9.5 GiB of shared usage, whatever the RAM; `ROCm/TheRock#9339`).  **r233: that ceiling belongs to the driver-shipped HIP runtime in `System32`, not to Windows** -- the same binary with the ROCm/TheRock `amdhip64_7.dll` beside it pins 16-40 GiB (issue #278); the diagnostic remains the way to reach the degraded state on a Linux gate.  The first refusal latches the pinned tier off for the run (`g_pinned_off`) and is not retried, because on that platform it can leave a process-wide HIP error behind.  Forms: `1` refuses every pinned allocation from the start (no pool is ever built, no ring ever grows -- the "the tier never came up" shape); `late` lets the load build its pools and size the arena and then refuses the FIRST bounce-ring grow after that, which is the field's sequence (the pool works, a later fill needs one new ring entry, the driver says no) and the shape that proves an ALREADY-BUILT pool keeps serving (the census prints `N expert fills were served from a host pool built BEFORE the refusal`); `0`/unset is off.  Never set it in a real run: it exists so `gate-host-pool.sh`'s `pinlate` arm can reach the Windows degraded state from a Linux gate, and so a reporter can reproduce the shape on demand. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN` | **`1`** | kill-switch | (r233, issue #278) the pinned host tier's kill switch: `0` closes it for the whole run on **any** platform -- no host pool is built, the bounce ring and the coalesced span ring never allocate, and every fill that needed them takes the pageable model-mapping fallback or declines (correct, slower).  `1`/unset is the shipped behaviour.  It exists for a Windows box whose loaded HIP runtime is the display driver's System32 copy: that runtime refuses a pinned allocation at ~9.3 GiB of adapter shared (aperture) usage even on a 64 GiB machine and can spin in its own wait loop afterwards (issue #278), while the TheRock runtime beside the binary pins 16-40 GiB and runs clean -- the startup WARN names the loaded runtime, and this switch lets the driver-runtime box still run the host-expert path without touching the tier.  `gate-host-pool.sh`'s `pin0` arm asserts the text stays byte-identical and the census names the switch. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_FAULT` | unset | **diagnostic** | (issue #274, r219; extended r223) makes `expert_host_src()` fail for a **pageable** master (the pool/bounce path; a pinned master is untouched), i.e. "no source can be resolved for this expert".  It is how `gate-host-pool.sh` proves the invariant that a failed fill ROLLS BACK and declines the op instead of serving the slot: with it set the run must still produce byte-identical text, with `FAILED fills: N` in the census, `the slot is NOT resident` per table, and **`takeover=0`**.  Forms: `1` (or any value below 2) faults every source from the start (the r219 arm); `N>1` starts at the N-th call process-wide (reproduces a report, but the landing point moves with the box's arena/pool sizing, so its text is not gateable); `late` faults from each table's **second** call on, i.e. "fills that had already succeeded, then a failing call" -- the mid-run shape @briansp2020 reported, deterministic per table; `0`/unset is off.  Never set it in a real run (it is the forced-failure arm of that gate). |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_BATCH` | `1` | kill-switch | `1` = a pool-backed prefill staging fills every expert that lives in either VRAM/host tier with **one kernel launch** (the per-expert slot map carries both the arena slot and the pool slot), which is what closes the prefill gap to `pinned`; `0` restores the pre-r114 arm (one `cudaMemcpyAsync` per expert) for an A/B.  Measured, 2x R9700 / 35B-A3B Q4_K_M `-ncmoe 40 --host-experts pool`, 70K prompt, `-ub 2048`: **2103 t/s** with `1` vs **1564 t/s** with `0` (+34 %), identical text.  **That number is r114-era and does not reproduce on today's tree**: with the pool alias readable in place there is no host bounce left for the one-launch kernel to amortise, so `0` (the per-expert copy engine) is now **equal or faster** — 1 x R9700, mirror pool, Flash-Next, `-ub` 5120 `1837-1849` vs `1564` for the gather, and `-ub 2048` `891` vs `1066`.  See the transport note above; the r234 measurement is the one to use. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_RANK` | **`decode`** | kill-switch | **r224 (block 12):** which persisted counter ranks the pool's content.  `decode` (default) ranks the arena's complement by the **decode** counter — the one the arena itself seeds from — and `combined` (the r113-r223 behaviour) ranks by the prefill+decode counter; `floor` keeps the prefill-hot `MOE_HOST_POOL_RANK_FLOOR` % ahead of the decode ranking so the pool's staging duty is preserved.  Single-stream at `-lme 36`, `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1`: `combined` 24.3 -> shipping 26.2 t/s.  **`combined` is also the documented workaround for a 4-user batch**: the ranking's sign is workload-dependent (it costs prefill throughput in a prefill-dominated `-npl 4` mix), so a deployment whose traffic is parallel slots should set `combined` and keep the demote. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_RANK_FLOOR` | `50` | tuning | the prefill-hot percentage kept ahead of the decode ranking, for `MOE_HOST_POOL_RANK=floor` only.  Ignored by `decode`/`combined`. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK` | **`1`** | kill-switch | **r224 (block 12), the eviction demote:** `1` = an admitted miss copies its arena victim into a free pool slot *before* the incoming fill overwrites it, so the pool learns from evictions instead of discarding a recently-hot expert.  This is one extra D2H per admission (9.6 us per 679 762 B at `-lme 36`, ~2/3 hidden behind compute) bought against a disk read (~0.3 ms on this box, ~5 ms on a 125 MB/s device); it pays because **6-8 %** of demoted slots are used again against a ~3 % break-even here.  `0` restores the pre-r224 "layout, no write-back" behaviour.  Declines (never lies) when there is no slot, when the geometry cannot express the copy (a `-sm tensor` axis-0 strided slice), or when the copy fails — a failed demote drops the victim exactly as before and never publishes the slot.  **r273: the exchange requires the demote to be EXPRESSIBLE** (`host_pool_t::demote_ok`, decided once from the table geometry at pool build).  A `-sm tensor` split gives each device a SLICE of every expert (`expert_bytes != host_bytes`), so the one-contiguous-copy demote can never be written into the pool's whole-expert image; before this fix the exchange was still "engaged", which (a) reserved ~4 % of the slots for a demote that always declined and (b) **retired the pool's copy of every expert it promoted to VRAM with nothing to refill the vacancy** — measured on the 2-device tensor split: 766 758 geometry declines out of 766 758 attempts, **792 321 retirements against 52 704 slots, 701 706 refills, h=0.3473, 0 evictions**, and every host read resolved to the page cache/disk (decode at half the pinned-master rate).  With the guard the same cell reports `0 demoted, 0 geometry, 0 promoter slots retired` and h=0.8294 (the coverage gap, not churn).  **r228 (block 12):** the exchange is **disengaged on a NEAR-MIRROR pool** (coverage gap < 4 % of the set) and a **prefill-band fill never honours the headroom**.  Measured on a 59 GiB field box at `-lme auto` (97.7 %, a 12-expert gap per 512-expert table, 1 x R9700): `SINK=0` first-pass 50k prefill 1 837 t/s / 74.5 gen against `SINK=1` **867 / 74.2** -- the reserve held free the slots the complement needed, so every prefill fill was refused and the missing experts were re-read through the bounce ring on every ubatch (the gather's "neither tier" volume 10.1 k -> 46 k experts).  With the fix that cell is byte-for-byte the `SINK=0` path (census `sink near-mirror tables ... 144`, 0 demoted, 0 headroom fills) and the demote's wins are untouched: `-lme 44` (93.7 %) **+19 %** gen / +48 % prompt, `-lme 36` +8-9 %, mirror inert.  A deployment whose tier is within ~4 % of its host expert set therefore gets no demote, by design. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK_SPARE` | `-1` (auto) | tuning | absolute override for the exchange headroom `K` (slots per table kept free for a demote).  `-1` = derive it from `_SPARE_PCT`. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK_SPARE_PCT` | `4` | tuning | the derived headroom, as a % of a table's **slots** (floor `_SPARE_MIN`, cap slots/8).  Measured at `-lme 36`: a table has ~393 slots, so 4 % = 15 -> 27.5 t/s; 3 % under-shoots to 11 slots -> 26.8.  **r228:** `K` is additionally capped by the table's **coverage gap** (`n_experts - slots`), because a reserve larger than the gap starves the complement it is meant to protect; and a prefill fill ignores it entirely (see `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK`). |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK_SPARE_MIN` | `2` | tuning | floor for the derived headroom, in slots. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_SPAN` | **`1`** | kill-switch | **r224 (block 12):** `1` = the prefill read of a run of ≥ 2 consecutive experts that are in neither tier is ONE aligned `O_DIRECT` read per chunk into a pinned span buffer instead of one read per expert.  A ~0.65 MiB read is latency-bound at depth 1 (~2.2 GiB/s on this box's NVMe, against 4.2 GiB/s for a 16 MiB read), and a prefill stages the whole host set per ubatch: measured prefill **+81 % at `-lme` 12, +57 % at 18, +14 % at 36**, generation unchanged, text byte-identical, 0 fallbacks.  `0` = one read per expert (the pre-r224 arm; A/B and bisection only). |  **r232 (issue #281): the coalesced read runs in BOTH fill modes.**  It reads the FILE either way -- O_DIRECT when the disk tier is on, a plain buffered `pread` through the OS page cache otherwise -- so a buffered `--load-mode mmap` run (the field default since r212) now coalesces instead of reading every "neither tier" expert out of the model MAPPING one ~0.65 MiB demand fault at a time.  Measured on the 56 GiB rig (`soar`, Flash-Next GSQ-RCO-IQ3_S + MTP, `-c 204800 -ub 6144`, cold page cache, 50k prefill x2): at `-lme 12` (26 % coverage) **821-875 t/s against 513-545** and 95 GiB of storage reads against 220-328 GiB; at `-lme 36` (77 %) **1307-1446 against 985-1235**; **inert at a near-mirror pool** (1889 vs 1890 -- no runs of >= 2 to coalesce); decode unchanged.  A buffered `pread` reads the same page-cache pages the mapping does, so this is a granularity change, not a fill-source change -- and it is what a Windows box needs: a mapped fill there is a demand fault per 4 KiB page (measured 715 MiB/s), while the file read gets the Cache Manager's readahead.  `0` remains the kill switch and is the A/B arm.
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_SPAN_EXPERTS` | `16` | tuning | experts per coalesced read (~10.9 MiB on the field config); clamped 1-64.  The shortest run worth a span (2 experts) is a **code constant**, not a knob: it is load-bearing (measured at `-lme 36`: 1 -> 362, 2 -> 379, 4 -> 331 t/s). |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_ORDER` | **`offset`** (file order) | kill-switch | **r231 (block 12), extended to the load-time PREWARM in r270 (`TODO` #318):** the order the **pool's bulk fills** are issued in — the band-transition re-layout (`pool_rerank_from_heat_locked`) *and* the load-time prewarm (`pool_prepopulate_locked`).  `offset` (default) = ascending expert id, which is ascending file offset, because one table's experts are contiguous in the GGUF; `rank` = the pre-r231 order (the pool's heat order), i.e. a random walk over the file.  The fill *set* is identical either way -- the slot an expert lands in is arbitrary (`pool_fill_empty_locked` takes the first empty slot) -- so this is pure ordering.  **r231, `-lme 44` (`SINK=1`, buffered fills), old/new interleaved**: the **band-transition tail** (the part of a 50k first-pass prefill spent re-laying the pool out after the last ubatch, before the first token) goes **13.5-18.6 s -> 9.4-9.7 s**, the prefill 769/780 -> 874/779 t/s, and the fill count 8 315 -> 7 352; text byte-identical (`sha=cf7f8b23f404`, five arms).  **r270, the PREWARM on `soar`** (Flash-Next GSQ-IQ3_XXS, `-ncmoe 47`, `-lme 32` = 419 of 512 slots/table, cold page cache, two runs per arm): the prewarm is **12 546 -> 7 774 ms** (3.67 -> 6.25 GB/s effective) for the same 42.7 GiB and the same set, **TTFT 19.10-20.39 -> 15.54-16.13 s (-20 %)**, whole run 30.7 -> 26.6-27.5 s (-13 %), decode unchanged, text byte-identical; the prompt eval pays +0.3 s of it back (1.17 s against 0.88 s -- the file-order arm leaves the colder pages in the cache and the staging misses ~2 GiB more).  It is **not** the whole tail: a 0.65 MiB fill is 0.7-1.6 ms on this NVMe, and the rest is page-cache/reclaim pressure under a memory cap (`MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO=1` makes it WORSE here: 21.3 s of storage reads at 2.1 GB/s, a depth-1 O_DIRECT read is latency-bound) |


### 1.3 The movable-boundary slab (block 06) — `GGML_CUDA_SLAB`, default **on**

**This is the delivery's central allocator.**  ONE slab per device is reserved and mapped **exactly once**
(`cuMemAddressReserve` + one `cuMemMap`), then split by a movable **boundary**: the work pool (the compute
buffer) below it, the MoE arena above it.  Growing the work region is a *boundary move inside the
already-mapped slab* — the lowest arena chunks change owner and the tables living there are evicted — so
**HIP is not called at runtime** and the work region's base VA never moves (a growing layout keeps every
tensor address).  It replaces the per-allocation VMM pool (`GGML_CUDA_COMPUTE_VMM`, **removed**) and the
"chunk the compute buffer so the arena can yield" plan: nothing is ever unmapped, so the ROCm sub-range
`hipMemUnmap` limitation stops mattering.

**r47 (issue #136):** the slab is **armed whenever the MoE expert cache is enabled, independent of
`--fit`**.  `moe_cache_preflight` runs an idempotent env parse before its cache gate, so `--fit off` (no
fit probe load, and therefore no backend init before the preflight) no longer leaves `g_slab_armed` false
with the cache on a raw `cudaMalloc` arena.  `GGML_CUDA_SLAB=0` is the only up-front opt-out; if the slab
is armed but cannot be **created** (no VMM, bad chunk geometry, or too little free VRAM for the reserve)
the run logs the reason and **streams** the experts from the host, and a one-time WARN in the
cache-enable path makes the (now bug-only) "cache on, slab not armed" state loud.  The fail-soft arena
yield now guards on the global `ggml_cuda_slab_enabled()` (not the failing device's slab) and the
stand-down is device-scoped.

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
