| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB` | **on** | kill-switch | `0` disables the slab.  The plain `cudaMalloc` path returns, together with the pre-slab failure mode (a later wide prefill aborting — the TODO #42 crash).  Since **r47** the slab is armed independent of `--fit`; a slab that is armed but cannot be created logs the reason and the cache streams from the host (issue #136). |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_CHUNK_MIB` | `64` | tuning | the boundary-move granularity (must be a power of two).  Arena *allocations* use the finer VMM granularity (2 MiB), so this does not waste arena. |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_RESERVE_MIB` | `headroom + the draft CONTEXT estimate` (`GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB` + kv + compute + slack, floor `1024`; r86 -- it was `max(8192, 25 % of the device)` before r86, a flat guess that made the slab decline under partial offload) | tuning | left OUTSIDE the slab for the weights / KV / draft / workspaces.  This is a **hard** constraint: ROCm will not unmap a sub-range of the slab's single mapping, so a value too small ENDS THE RUN (measured: 4096 -> a failed hipBLASLt workspace, exit 134).  An explicit value is taken **verbatim** (no `+ headroom`), which makes it the only knob that reaches the slab's **narrow-2 shortfall branch**: it leaves `value - headroom` as the unmapped VA spare, so a value below `headroom + the draft's compute region` makes the draft's compute pin *evict arena tables* instead of taking the spare (`GGML_CUDA_SLAB_NARROW2_PREFER_MALLOC` does not exist in the delivery -- measured inert, `archive/work/host-expert-parity/RESULTS-2026-11-07-narrow2.md`).  The evicted tables are re-armed and re-filled on the next sizing pass, so the cost is one re-fill of the evicted bytes (~15-20 ms), but the reserve must still hold the draft's KV or the load fails. |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_MIN_ARENA_MIB` | `2048` | tuning | hard cache floor: the slab is created only if it fits `estimated work buffer + this`.  Otherwise it declines, logs an error and the cache STREAMS from the host (`MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=0` semantics). |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB` | `1152 + 128 x n_gpu` (1280 / 1408 / 1536 MiB; r206 -- it was `640 + 128 x n_gpu`, 768 / 896 / 1024, r86-r205; `6144` under `-sm layer` with host experts, r36) | tuning | how much stays free outside the slab when `slab_extend` reclaims the reserve the model did not need.  A **MiB value, not a boolean** (the only knob for this; `0` disables the reclaim).  **Not slack**: it must cover every allocation made after that point, including ones we cannot redirect (see below).  Since r36 the MoE cache asks for at least **6144** when every cache table is unsplit and the tables span more than one device (`ggml_cuda_slab_headroom_at_least_mib`); the 255k `-sm layer -ts 59,41` run OOMed at 4096 and passes at 6144.  An explicit value still wins.  **3072 was trialled and rejected (2026-10-10):** the H2D ring moved inside the slab so it no longer spends ~1 GiB here, and 3072 measured **+8.5 % decode step rate** on the field config (arena residency 61.7 -> 64.9 %) — but on the §2.2 wide-prefill config (`--fit off`, IQ4_XS, `-ngl 99 -sm tensor -ncmoe 48 -ub 6144 -c 204800`) 4096 is clean (312 t/s, 0 corruption) while 3072 dies in `hipblaslt.cpp:164`.  **Since r206 the default is `1152 + 128 x n_gpu`** (1280 / 1408 / 1536 MiB), raised from `640 + 128 x n_gpu` (768 / 896 / 1024) because the 640 base was sized on the field GSQ / `-ub 4096` shape, whose widest outside transient is ~300 MiB per device, and the `-c 262144` host-expert runme is a harder one: r194 needed **1536** there to stop `hipBLASLt out of memory` on the first prefill, i.e. the shipped default was one workspace short on a documented configuration.  A default that needs the operator to know that shape is the wrong default.  **Measured cost of the bump** (Flash-Next GSQ-IQ3_XXS, 1 GPU, `-ncmoe 48`, `-c 32768`, q8_0 KV, 5 246-token prompt, `-n 2000`, interleaved arms, `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0`): old 768 -> arena 24 691.7 MiB / `h` 0.9603 / 47.95 t/s, new 1280 -> **24 576.4 MiB / 0.9602 / 47.98 t/s**, i.e. **-115 MiB of arena, -0.01 pt of expert hit rate, decode flat**, with byte-identical text.  The bump is **margin, not a demonstrated fix**: the r194 cliff is order-dependent and this box did not reproduce it on any prompt tried at r206 (5 246, 25 763 and 51 475 tokens at `-c 262144`, with 768 and with 1280, all clean and all text-identical).  An explicit `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB` is still taken **verbatim** (no per-GPU addition), so the old 768 is one env var away.  **Measured 2026-10-17 on the field GSQ config (1 GPU, `-ub 4096 -c 131072`, `--fit off`, MTP):** 4096 -> arena 15 666.6 MiB / hit 0.9056, 3072 -> 16 705.7 / 0.9147, 2048 -> 17 744.8 / 0.9223, and on a 16 384-token prompt 2048 gives 0.9380 vs 0.9165 at 4096 with identical text — i.e. **~1 pt of hit rate per GiB of headroom converted to arena**, on a config whose widest ubatch is 4096.  It is a per-config tuning value: the 3072 rejection above was `-ub 6144 -c 204800`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_POOL_MIN_FREE_MIB` | `512` when the cache holds host-expert tables, else `0` | tuning / kill-switch | r36.  The workspace pool's **first** attempt refuses like a real OOM when it would leave less than this much VRAM free, so its existing flush + retry runs instead.  The retry has no floor, so it never aborts.  `0` disables the floor. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MIN_FREE_MIB` | unset / `0` = off (r135) | **opt-in budget** | a device-wide free-VRAM floor at the single allocation choke point (`ggml_cuda_device_malloc`): an allocation that would leave less than this much free is refused exactly like a driver OOM, so every fail-soft path above it (arena shrink/release, pool flush, FA staging stand-down) still runs and the run ends with a message instead of consuming the last bytes.  This is the knob for a card that is genuinely too small for the requested `-c` / `-ngl`: on Windows/WDDM an over-committing allocation is **not** refused, it is paged to shared memory and a later GPU access faults the device (`unspecified launch failure`, or a bugcheck inside `dxgkrnl`, issue #219) and the whole session is lost.  Set it to the largest value that still runs on that box (e.g. `1024` on a 16 GiB card with a desktop), not to the card size.  **Inert while the movable-boundary slab is armed** (the slab parks nearly all free VRAM by design), so an MoE `-ncmoe` run is unchanged.  On Linux the same over-budget config does not fault: it aborts with `ROCm error: out of memory` -- and the r135 note that it can instead run "~30x slower while the driver evicts resident weight pages" is **wrong as a mechanism** (TODO #103, closed in r167): HIP refuses an allocation that does not fit, never demotes a resident working set (measured: a 30 GiB resident set reads at 505-513 GB/s with 1.7 GiB free), and what a squeezed driver actually costs is **page-migration traffic for the *other* client's buffers** (0-48 % of prefill in the arms that could produce any, tracking GTT migration volume).  The floor starts either outcome earlier and says why.  Pair it with `GGML_CUDA_LOW_FREE_MIB` below, which is the default-on diagnostic for the same state. |
| `GGML_CUDA_LOW_FREE_MIB` | `512` (r167) | **diagnostic**, kill-switch | warn **once per device** when a device allocation leaves less free VRAM than this (`0` disables; `GGML_CUDA_LOW_FREE_MIB=0` is the A/B arm).  Nothing is refused and no behaviour changes: it is the number a "it is slow" / "it aborted" report is missing.  The run's free-VRAM **low-water mark** is now sampled at every >= 16 MiB allocation on *every* config -- until r167 it was tracked only while the movable-boundary slab was active and printed only under `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_STATS` -- and a refused allocation reports free/total plus that mark.  At a few hundred MiB of headroom the next growth can fail with `out of memory`; on Windows/WDDM the over-committing allocation is paged instead and a later access can fault (issue #219); and a driver that must migrate another client's buffers out of VRAM costs the run PCIe time (TODO #103).  **Skipped while the movable-boundary slab is armed** (the slab parks nearly all free VRAM by design).  Measured cost: none -- a 15.8k-token 27B prefill is neutral versus the r166 binary in an interleaved A/B. |
| `GGML_CUDA_WDDM_FREE` | **on** (r209, block 09) | **kill switch** (Windows only) | `cudaMemGetInfo` returns the smaller of HIP's free figure and the adapter's **WDDM** figure — `D3DKMTQueryStatistics` over the non-aperture segments (`CommitLimit - BytesResident`), i.e. every process on the card — so the slab, the expert-cache sizing, `--fit` and `GGML_CUDA_MIN_FREE_MIB` see the memory another process holds (on Windows `hipMemGetInfo` counts only this process, and past that point WDDM pages to system RAM instead of failing: issue #216).  `0` keeps HIP's figures.  It makes the *reading* honest; it does not make an overcommit fail, so `TODO` #216's accepted limitation stands.  Inert off Windows (no code, no read).  Read once per process. |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_RING_MIB` | `6144` | tuning | MiB cap on the H2D staging-ring region the slab carves as a **work sub-region** (r39).  The auto size is `slots x (largest host table + 512)`, and that estimate comes from `llama_model::max_host_weight_tensor_bytes()`, which can be a MERGED host tensor (every layer/expert in one -- measured 27 GiB on Qwen3.8-Flash-Next where the largest staged slice is 270 MiB), so this cap keeps the region proportional to a real table.  The ring lives between the narrow work floor and the wide work view, never in the arena, and is reclaimed on the prefill->decode drop.  `0` puts the ring back outside the slab (the raw `cudaMalloc` + G4 path).  The field tables (270 MiB) fit in `2560` and give nearly the same prefill as `6144`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_OPTIONAL_ALLOC_MAX_FREE_PCT` | `50` | tuning / kill-switch | caps an **optional** raw allocation at this percentage of the device's *free* VRAM.  Since r39 the op-offload H2D staging ring lives **inside the slab** (see `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_RING_MIB` above), so this now effectively guards the **FA prefill staging arena** only (`GGML_CUDA_SLAB_RING_MIB=0` restores the ring to this path).  A refusal is graceful: the ring falls back to the serial path, the FA staging to the native K/V read (one-time warning) -- and on `fingon` that refusal is *faster* than allowing it (2502.2 vs 2315.1 t/s prefill), so this is an adaptive win, not a limitation.  `0` restores the pre-change unbounded behaviour.  **Inert unless the slab is active on the device**, so a non-host-expert / non-ROCm run is unchanged; it is a cap, never a reservation.  `GGML_SCHED_STAGE_MAX_MB` remains the hard ring override. |
| `GGML_COMPUTE_BUFFER_CHUNK_MIB` | `256` | tuning | the compute buffer is allocated in whole chunks + one spare (`(ceil(need/C)+1)*C`), so growth inside the chunk is free.  `0` falls back to `GGML_COMPUTE_BUFFER_MARGIN_PCT`.  Since **r37** the chunk applies **only when the slab is in use**: a dense model (no slab) falls back to the percentage margin, which removes the +739 MiB of issue #120.  **Since r139 it is per device and requires a *live* slab** (`ggml_cuda_slab_active`), not merely an armed one: the allocation that creates the slab is sized before the answer exists, and a slab that was armed but declined must not keep chunking its streaming compute buffer.  On issue #214's 12 GiB gfx1031 config a 510 MiB layout was rounded to 768 MiB on the declined path; it now stays at the 561 MiB margin (`0` + `GGML_COMPUTE_BUFFER_MARGIN_PCT=0` gives 510).  A live slab uses the chunk from the first allocation after it exists. |
| `GGML_CUDA_POOL_CACHE_MB` | unset / `0` = unlimited (r46, block 06) | **opt-in tuning** | cap on the bytes of FREED buffers the **legacy** `ggml_cuda_pool_leg` keeps cached.  A long prefill's temporaries grow with the KV length and each smaller predecessor stays cached, so at ~256K the idle VRAM starves allocations outside the pool.  Before a new device allocation, once the cached bytes exceed this MiB value the pool frees them (never during graph capture).  Unset/0/negative/garbage = today's behaviour.  Only the legacy pool is touched (HIP uses it; CUDA's VMM pool is unaffected), and the release is a whole-cache flush, not largest-first eviction. |
| `GGML_CUDA_POOL_WIDE` | **on** (r86) | kill-switch | while the wide (prefill) band is armed, the legacy workspace pool serves its **large** temporaries from the slab's wide-phase auxiliary region instead of caching them outside the slab (`set_wide_routing` + `pool_wide_pin`).  `0` keeps every pool allocation outside the slab.  A `POOL_WIDE_LEAK` tripwire fires at the drop if a pool block is still live. |
| `GGML_CUDA_POOL_WIDE_MIN_MIB` | `1` (r86) | tuning | the smallest pool allocation redirected into the wide band; the band rounds to the slab unit (2 MiB here), so the many tiny prefill scratch blocks stay in the pool. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_TRACE` | off | diagnostic | trace the graph capture state machine (`capturing graph`, `capture deferred`, `deferred-capture warmup`), and at exit report how every `graph_compute` call was served: `graph_compute calls: direct=… replay=… capture=… properties_changed=…` (r144).  A capture must never be attempted while `SLAB_STATS ... armed=1` (r86 refuses it).  **Read the census, not the capture lines**: a captured graph that is never replayed still logs captures.  A `direct` count equal to the call count with a matching `properties_changed` means the replay identity is churning, and then `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` costs nothing — which is how you tell the two apart.  Since r149 the decode census is `direct` ~= 0, `replay` ~= the call count and `properties_changed` in the tens: that is what "the replay works" looks like, and `capture` is the number of **distinct keys** (one graph per layer per width), so it is expected to be in the hundreds. |
| `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_STATS` | off | diagnostic | per-device slab accounting at the arm/drop and destructor.  Since r86 it prints `free=`/`free_min=` **for the selected device** (BETA 1 repeated device 0), `outside_max` / `outside_failed` (the largest allocation the cushion refused) and `pool_wide` / `wide[n_pop hole inval rebase aux_refused]`; `hole=0` and `outside_failed=0` are the acceptance signals.  Since r168 `wide[aux_refused=N]` is the number of wide-aux growth requests **refused in that wide pass** (the region is decided once per pass, see `patches/README.md` block 10) and `SLAB_WIDE_AUX dev=... req=... want=... target=... armed=...` is printed per growth decision; the one-per-pass `SLAB_WIDE_AUX ... refused for this wide pass` line is a plain WARN and does not need this variable. |

**Why the cards sit near-full.**  `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB` is what is free *at the moment the slab is
extended*, and the steady state is that minus everything allocated afterwards — measured ~3.7 GiB of it: the
MTP draft buffer (762 MiB), the FA-QSA workspace (762 MiB), hipBLASLt's Tensile code objects and internal
workspace, and the workspace-pool high-water mark.  So ~200-350 MiB free is normal, and raising the headroom
raises it one-for-one (8192 -> ~4.3 GiB free) at the cost of an equal amount of arena.

> **At the VRAM cliff, raise the headroom instead of dropping hipBLASLt (r194; the default covers it since r206).**  Measured on Flash-Next
> (qwen4exp, `-ncmoe 48`, q8_0 KV, `-c 262144`, block 15's attention pool, one R9700, 1 slot): the then-default
> 768 MiB left `hipBLASLt out of memory` on the **first prefill** (`hipblaslt.cpp:169`) while the server stayed
> healthy and the request failed with no reply.  `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB=1536` served 3/3 requests clean
> with the arena at 19.12 GiB instead of 19.88, and `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0` also worked (up to -15 % decode).
> The same config is fine at `-c 131072` with the default.  So this is a budget fact of running a 262144-token
> context on a 32 GiB card, not a pool bug: every MiB of headroom is arena you chose not to take.  **r206 raised
> the default to 1280 MiB on one GPU** for this reason (the shape no longer needs the env var), without
> reproducing the order-dependent cliff itself -- see the row above.

> **The MTP draft's compute region is committed VRAM, not a reservation (r214, `TODO` #143).**  The slab pins the draft
> context's compute buffer in its **narrow-2** region at the top of its reserved VA, and since r214 that region is
> **mapped for its whole extent** -- 0.75 GiB per device at `-c 32768` up to 1.75 GiB at `-c 262144` on a Flash-Next
> `-c 204800`-class config (the measured ladder is in `INVARIANTS.md` r214).  Before r214 the region's top could be
> left as reserved-but-absent VA; the run then *faulted* instead of reporting a budget problem, so any comparison of
> "VRAM used" across r213 and r214 will show **more** used for the same arena -- up to ~1.4 GiB per device.  That is the
> draft's own buffer becoming present, not a new cost: the reserve already accounted for it, and the total is no larger
> than the straddle path's (measured 32.82 / 33.11 GB used against 32.89 / 33.24 GB).  What it does mean is that the
> per-device margin at a deep context is real -- on this box ~1.0 GiB free on the tighter card at `-c 204800` -- so
> `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB` and the arena budget both still apply, and a config that used to "fit" only because
> the draft's buffer was unmapped now needs the headroom it was always supposed to have.

> **Do not disable hipBLASLt to buy headroom.**  The ROCm BLAS stack allocates its own Tensile code objects
> and workspace behind the application's back, and that class of allocation CANNOT be routed into the slab.
> Measured with `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0` at `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_HEADROOM_MIB=2048`: the output was CORRUPT
> (`////`), and at 4096 a 16k request returned one token then EOS.  It is not a supported configuration.

---

## 2. Scheduler and H2D staging (block 06)

The whole-shard H2D staging path for host-resident MoE expert tables.  Defaults below are the validated
configuration.

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_SCHED_STAGE` | on | kill-switch (value) | master switch for the staging path. |
| `GGML_SCHED_STAGE_MIN_TOKENS` | `64` | tuning | width gate floor.  Below it the copy loop's gather branch serves the input instead. |
| `GGML_SCHED_STAGE_SLOTS` | unset | tuning | ring slot count. |
| `GGML_SCHED_STAGE_MAX_MB` | unset | tuning | hard ring budget cap; the slab-gated free-VRAM cap `GGML_CUDA_OPTIONAL_ALLOC_MAX_FREE_PCT` (§1.3) also applies to a growth. |
| `GGML_SCHED_STAGE_TABLE_REF_MB` | `0` | tuning | `0` = width-only gate (r18 disabled the stale r7 table-size scaling; a positive value restores it for A/B). |
| `GGML_SCHED_EVENTS` | on | kill-switch | set `0` to drop the cross-device event ordering. This was the pre-r12 workaround for the `-sm layer` + host-expert garbage-prefill bug; it is **fixed** in block 06, so leave it on. |
| `GGML_SCHED_DEVGATHER` | **unset = pool-backed tables only** (r207) | tuning / A/B | **three-valued (block 12):** **unset** = a `--host-experts pool` table's routed `MUL_MAT_ID` is served by the device gather in the **prefill band** (`n_tokens > moe_cache_band`) and everything else keeps whole-table staging; **`0`** = kill switch, the pre-r207 behaviour; **`1`/`force`** = the historical A/B arm (gather below the staging width gate and for large tables, either host-expert mode) — deliberately NOT the default, because it costs a `pinned` master **~47 % prefill at two devices**.  Why the pool-only default: a pool-backed table's whole-table staging is the slow path at every width (its source is the pageable mapping plus the pool's host alias, and it degrades as coverage rises) — 122B-A10B on 3 x R9700, 5258-token prompt, `-ub 2048`: pool FRAC 95 **1631.3/1507.7 t/s** prefill with the gather, **16.9** with it off, against `pinned`'s 634-638; decode unchanged (67.2-67.7).  The decode band keeps the arena slot-remap read on purpose (the gather there costs an order of magnitude: 1.2-4.1 t/s).  Prefill logits are **bit-identical** to the staging path (KLD -0.000000 / top-p 100 %).  **r234, 1 GPU:** on a pool tier the default gather is the stable transport (and +20 % at `-ub 2048`), while from `-ub` 5120 up the copy-engine arm is 10-18 % ahead; on a **pinned** master forcing the gather costs **16 %** (1493 vs 1793 t/s at `-ub 6144`).  See the transport note above. |
| `GGML_SCHED_MOE_DUP_SPLIT` | **on** (r96, block 09) | kill-switch | a second `MUL_MAT_ID` consumer of the expert weights the current split already stages (qwen4exp's full-row MTP export tail next to its gathered logits tail) starts a split of its own, so each consumer is staged for its own routing.  Required for correctness as well as throughput: in one merged split the second consumer reads the expert-cache arena slots staged for the first consumer's routing, which produced non-greedy-pure text with 0.00000 draft acceptance on `-sm layer` + `-ncmoe` + MTP and cost a full table copy per prompt chunk on `-sm tensor`.  `0` restores the merged split (A/B and bisection only).  Detail: `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §43. |
| `GGML_SCHED_SYNC_GRAPH_INPUTS` | on | kill-switch | extra graph-input sync: a split input that is (a view of) a user graph input takes the **synchronous** copy instead of the async H2D (r35, issue #87 — the recurrent-state copy is always consumed through such views).  `0` restores the r26 behaviour (A/B and bisection only). |
| `GGML_SCHED_ASYNC_LARGE_HOST_INPUT` | **unset = a host split input above 1 MiB is copied synchronously** (r235, block 09) | kill-switch (A/B and bisection only) | `1` restores the **asynchronous** copy for a host-resident split input **above `GGML_BACKEND_ASYNC_SNAPSHOT_MAX` (1 MiB)** that is not a weight.  Above that limit no backend snapshots the source (the CUDA pinned ring stops there, and `ggml-cuda.cu` defines its ring limit from the same constant), so the copy engine reads the caller's live pointer when the stream reaches the copy — and the one that bites is a CPU split's output written **in place**: the token embeddings when the table is on the CPU (`token_embd` in `CPU_Mapped`, `model.input_embed` / `node_1`, **41,943,040 B** per 2048-token ubatch), which the next ubatch's CPU split rewrites while a copy queued behind earlier work is still pending — a corrupted prefill from layer 0 that stays fluent.  Upstream ggml copies every host input synchronously; this is that guarantee for the large ones **only** — inputs up to 1 MiB keep the ring snapshot (r90) and a host **weight** keeps the async copy at any size (a weight is not rewritten in place; where the delivery does rewrite one, its own staging ring owns the ordering).  **Measured cost of the default: 1 GPU `pp2048` −0.17 %, `-ub 512` −0.30 %, 3 GPU `-sm layer` +0.08 %** (same binary, kill-switch A/B, inside the arm spread), prefill logits and greedy text unchanged. |
| `GGML_SCHED_DEBUG` | unset | diagnostic | scheduler debug output. |
| `GGML_SCHED_DEBUG_REALLOC` | unset | diagnostic | log buffer reallocations. |
| `GGML_META_SPLIT_COPY` | unset | tuning | meta-backend split copy handling. |
| `GGML_META_SEG_ASYNC` | **on** (r101, block 10) | kill-switch | the meta backend's **row-aligned segmented `set_tensor_async`** splice: a segmented (`n_segments > 1` or `nr > 1`) host-expert table upload is issued as one async `set_2d_async` per (segment, repeat, device) plus an async `set_async` for the MMQ tail pad, instead of one synchronous setter call.  Mid-row ranges stay synchronous by design (the loader's chunked upload owns a staging ring that may be reused on return).  `0` restores the fully synchronous segmented setter (A/B and bisection only; measured neutral on the gemma4 miss path — the win there is the arena sizing, not this). |
| `GGML_META_SS_VERIFY` | off | diagnostic | `1` recomputes every outermost meta split-state cache hit and aborts on a stale entry (r25 added the 8-version + compacted cache; this verifies it). |
| `GGML_META_COMPUTE_HEADROOM` | `128` | diagnostic | views **per static tensor** the meta backend's *first* compute-container context holds (`128 * n_tensors` views per simple device; the 48-tensor attention KV buffer of a `-sm tensor` run therefore starts at 6144 views).  The container **grows** past it when one generation registers more (a long-context state restore registers one view per KV cache chunk per layer), so this is a first-context size, not a limit — issue #232 was the old abort (`not enough space in the context's memory pool`) when that budget was exhausted.  A smaller value only makes the container grow sooner; it never changes an output (used to reproduce/verify the growth path).  A growth is logged at DEBUG (`-lv 5`). |
| `GGML_META_SS_SCRATCH` | **on** (r100, block 09) | kill-switch | the meta split-state cache's pre-warm walk keeps its `wstack`/`wseen` in a reused per-thread `monotonic_buffer_resource` (1 MiB, released per walk) instead of allocating one heap node per visited tensor on every graph re-allocation.  Inert on the computed states (the walk visits the same tensors in the same order; `GGML_META_SS_VERIFY=1` re-derives every outer hit and agrees).  `0` restores the heap containers (A/B and bisection only). |
| `GGML_STAGE_META_REDIRECT` | off | diagnostic | meta redirection instead of the explicit d2d completion. |
| `GGML_STAGE_NO_RESTORE` | off | diagnostic | skip the redirect restore (debug only — unsafe). |
| `GGML_STAGE_GATHER_SCRATCH` | off | diagnostic | force the gather scratch path. |
| `GGML_SCHED_COPY_EVENT` | `per-copy` | kill-switch (value) | r220, block 12.  The cross-backend split-input copy is ordered on a **per-copy** event from the scheduler's per-source-backend ring, instead of the backend's shared per-device `copy_event`.  Why: a queued HIP stream wait resolves against the **latest** record of its event at execution time, so a copy still in flight re-records the shared event and re-binds the consumers already queued on it — independent copies then serialize against each other (diagnosed by @slavap for PR #268, where it cost a two-lane prefill all of its overlap).  `shared` restores the old single shared event (A/B and bisection only).  Bit-exact and throughput-neutral on this box (pp8192, 3 GPU `-sm layer`: 6311 vs 6317 t/s); the win is on a box where two cross-backend copies overlap. |
| `GGML_MOE_CACHE_STAGE` | on | kill-switch | r83.  With `-ncmoe`/`-cmoe` host experts, a whole-table staging copy is filled from the expert-cache arena for the experts already resident and from the pinned host master for the rest, so only the non-resident experts cross PCIe.  `0` restores the full whole-table upload.  It declines to the plain upload for a table the slab stood down mid-prefill, an unpinned host master or a geometry mismatch; `moe_cache_report` prints the staged arena/host split, the H2D percentage and, since r234, one WARN naming every declined table with its reason (a stood-down table was previously declined silently, on every ubatch, while its bytes were missing from the census).  **Where it pays, measured 2026-11-07 (Flash-Next GSQ-RCO IQ3_S, 52 512-token prompt, balanced ABBA, 4 runs per arm):**
* **any PAGEABLE master** (`--host-experts pool`, `-lme` in the not-fitting band) — `0` is **5-10x slower** (1 GPU, `-ub 6144`, `GGML_SCHED_DEVGATHER=0`: 1052-1556 t/s against **103-281**), because without the cache staging the fill falls to `moe_cache_upload_pageable`, which copies every byte through a pinned bounce (a host `memcpy` + a pinned H2D per expert) instead of the arena's D2D.  This is the case r83 measured (+32-54 %) and the one the default exists for.
* **a high-residency pinned master across 2 devices** (`-sm layer`, arena 47 534.9 MiB of a 47 962.5 MiB host set = **99.1 % residency**): staging is **+4.0 %** (1961 against 1885 t/s) and is the *stable* arm — with the arena covering the set, staging moves ~0 bytes over PCIe (`arena=322 875 MiB host=0.0 MiB`, H2D 0.0 %) and everything else rides the copy stream.
* **a low/moderate-residency pinned master on ONE device** (43.4 % residency, arena 20 796.2 MiB): `0` is **+12.8 %** (2023 against 1793 t/s) — the one shape where the shipped engine loses, because the one-launch fill kernel is a worse engine than one bulk copy per table (it monopolises the CUs and starves the neighbouring compute kernels) and there are not enough arena bytes to save.
So the switch is default-on for a measured reason, not habit: the failing shape is the 1-GPU pinned one, and the fix there is the *engine* (a copy-engine or hybrid fill that keeps the arena source without the kernel), not dropping the feature.  See the transport note above. |

`GGML_MOE_CACHE_INPLACE` (r36) was removed with the device-remap path at r40 and is not read by this
set; the cache-aware staging above is the only prefill residency path.

---

## 3. MTP / speculative decoding

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MTP_DRAFT_N_UBATCH` | `512` | tuning | caps the MTP draft's encoder-injection chunk.  The draft only drafts `n_max+1` tokens, so a 512-token chunk is plenty, and the ~1.6 GiB/device the wide chunk held at `-ub 8192` goes to the MoE arena.  `0` restores the target's `-ub` (no cap); a tiny cap (e.g. 4) costs prefill speed.  **It is also the draft cache's ubatch, so it sizes the MTP draft window's spare room** (`win + one batch` cells) **and the draft reserve `--fit` charges** — since r248 both read this value through `common_speculative_draft_n_ubatch()` instead of the target's `-ub`, which disagreed by 512 cells (issue #297). |
| `LLAMA_SPEC_DRAFT_N_MAX_CLAMP` | `1` = clamp on | kill-switch | clamps `--spec-draft-n-max` to 15 (the recurrent snapshot bound). `0` escapes the clamp; **purity is only promised to 7**. |
| `LLAMA_SPEC_NGRAM_MOD_BAND_CLAMP` | `1` = clamp on | kill-switch (r123, block 09) | clamps the **draftless n-gram** speculators' draft length (`--spec-ngram-mod-n-max`, default **64** with `n_min` 48; the ngram-simple/ngram-map `size_m`, default 48) to **one less than the MoE expert cache's decode/verify band** whenever the target's arena is active — 16 tokens on RDNA4, so a 15-token draft / 16-row verify; 8 on RDNA3 and NVIDIA.  A verify batch is `n_draft + 1` rows and the arena serves a routed `MUL_MAT_ID` from VRAM only up to the band: above it the op leaves the arena and the routed experts are streamed from the pinned host master on **every** verify step, which on a host-expert config costs far more than the extra accepted tokens buy (TODO #59).  `n_min` is lowered with `n_max` (ngram-mod discards a draft shorter than `n_min`, so a naked `n_max` clamp would silently disable it).  A model with no active arena — fully device-resident, or the CPU expert path (`MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=0`) — reports no band and is **never** clamped, because there the wide draft is the win.  `0` restores the uncapped draft (A/B and bisect only).  Measured (2x R9700, Flash-Next IQ4_NL `-sm tensor -ncmoe 36`, `prompts/recall.txt`, `-n 2000`): the README combo 73.9 -> 168.3 t/s; the cliff is one row wide (159.2 t/s at a 16-row verify, 55.0 at 17).  Device-resident 144.7 (MTP n3) -> 378.7 (ngram default) vs 244.6 clamped.  Record: `archive/work/ngram-mod-host-experts/EVIDENCE-2026-10-24.md`.  **Since r129 the clamp is decided on the LIVE band (TODO #96 closed):** it is applied after `llama_init_from_model`, so a host-expert config whose movable-boundary slab is armed and then **declines** (a small-VRAM card, e.g. 12 GiB at 128k where the work buffer + the arena floor + the ring hole do not fit) keeps its wide draft — that run streams with no arena, where the wide draft is the win (2x R9700: 67.6 vs 52.2 capped t/s).  The clamp no longer lowers `cparams.n_rs_batch`: that stays the **unclamped** safety bound (the widest batch a partial rollback can land in), and the slab's narrow verify reserve takes the band-limited width instead, so a live arena is sized exactly as before.  Two consequences worth knowing: an ngram speculator floors the chunked-GDN threshold at its unclamped draft width for the whole run (a prefill **tail** ubatch below it takes the sequential, bit-identical GDN path), and a decline that happens *after* the context exists (an OOM-yield arena release) still leaves the draft clamped — `0` is the escape in both cases. |
| `LLAMA_MTP_DRAFT_OP_OFFLOAD` | unset | tuning | op-offload behaviour for the draft model. |
| `LLAMA_MTP_SPARSE` / `LLAMA_MTP_SPARSE_DECODE` | unset | tuning | sparse MTP variants. |
| `LLAMA_MTP_INGEST_TAIL` | unset/`0` = every row | **opt-in tuning** (r52, block 02) | feeds the MTP draft only the last N positions of each prompt (the server reports each prompt end via `common_speculative_set_prompt_end()`); generation rows are always fed.  The draft loses look-back over the skipped prefix only, so output is unchanged, but draft acceptance drops: on a ~31k-token prompt, `4096` measured prefill **+4.8 %** and decode **-4.4 %** (acceptance 0.821 -> 0.715).  Kept opt-in because it is a speed/look-back trade, not a pure win.  Tools other than the server never report a prompt end, so there the variable has no effect.  **Since r118 the target side is skipped too** (block 02, PR #206): a prompt ubatch that lies entirely below the installed tail no longer copies its nextn hidden state to the host, and the MTP hook no longer calls `llama_get_embeddings_nextn()` for it.  That getter's `ctx->synchronize()` was the real drain: it waited for both backends to go idle after every prompt ubatch, so the host could only build the next ubatch once the GPUs had stopped.  Measured 2x R9700, Flash-Next IQ3_XXS, 31.5k-token prompt, `4096`: the tail's own prefill gain goes from **+4.5 %** to **+6.1 %** (1509.8 vs 1423.3 t/s without the tail), greedy text and draft counts (177/364) unchanged, decode flat.  Of the +1.4 % the hook half is about +0.6 % and the target readback half about +0.1 %.  **The tail shares the reuse realign (r218, issue #272):** `spec_draft_align_cache()` drops the rows a reused prefix left below the new prompt's window before the first injection, so a partially reused prompt cannot leave the draft's cache ending below the batch it ingests (a strict, non-M-RoPE target would answer HTTP 500).  On every MTP-head model on the maintainer's boxes the target is M-RoPE, where the batch check allows the jump, so this arm is unreachable there and is documented rather than measured. |
| `LLAMA_MTP_TAIL_READBACK` | **on** | kill-switch (r118, block 02) | disables **both halves** of the `LLAMA_MTP_INGEST_TAIL` prompt-tail speed-up: the MTP hook's early return (no `llama_get_embeddings_nextn()` per prompt ubatch, so no whole-path `synchronize()`) and the target's `llama_set_embeddings_nextn_from()` readback skip.  `0` restores the pre-r118 behaviour with the tail still set, for bisection or a field report on the skip.  Without `LLAMA_MTP_INGEST_TAIL` every per-sequence threshold is `0`, so no batch can qualify: every non-MTP decode, and every MTP run without the tail, is unchanged (measured -0.1 %, i.e. noise).  Record: `archive/work/mtp-tail-readback/`. |
| `LLAMA_DFLASH_INGEST_TAIL` | **on** | kill-switch (r130, block 12) | a DFlash/DFlash2 draft whose **every** layer has a sliding window ingests only the prompt rows it can attend to (the last `n_swa` positions of the prompt, reported by the server through `common_speculative_set_prompt_end()`), and skips a prompt batch left with no rows **before** the wait on the target, so the target's next ubatch stays queued.  Unlike the MTP tail this costs no look-back: the draft's mask is `p1 - p0 >= n_swa` (`LLAMA_SWA_TYPE_STANDARD`, which DFlash sets unconditionally), so a row before `prompt_end - n_swa` is masked out of every draft query that can follow, and the skipped rows were being encoded and written into the window-sized draft cache only to be dropped unread.  A draft with any full-attention layer (a mixed pattern) keeps ingesting every row; the decided value is logged at startup (`every layer has a N-position window`).  Measured on 3x gfx1201 `-sm tensor -mg 0`, 27B UD-Q4_K_XL + DFlash2-Q4_K_M, `-fa on -ctk/ctv q8_0`: prefill **+8-12 %** at 6K/26K/88K tokens (88.6K: 1271 -> 1395 t/s, no-draft 1415 t/s, i.e. the draft's share of the prefill goes 10.2 % -> 1.4 %), 35B-A3B Q4_K_M + its DFlash2 **+14.6 %** at 6.1K, greedy text and draft counts byte-identical in every arm (1/2 slots, `-ub 512`/`2048`).  `0` feeds every row (A/B and bisect only).  **A partially reused prompt cannot invalidate the draft's cache (r218, issue #272):** a turn that adds more than `n_swa` tokens puts this prompt's window past the rows a reused prefix left in the draft's cache, and `spec_draft_align_cache()` drops those rows (they are older than the window of the prompt's end, so no later row can attend to them) before the first injection.  Before r218 that gap was a `HTTP 500 decode() failed: failed to process speculative batch` for a strict (non-M-RoPE) draft, so `0` was also the interim workaround for it. |
| `LLAMA_ARG_SPEC_DRAFT_CACHE_TYPE_K` / `_V` | unset = **follow the main `-ctk` / `-ctv`** | kill-switch (r125, block 02) | env aliases for `--spec-draft-type-k` / `-v` (`-ctkd` / `-ctvd`).  **Unset means the draft context uses the main model's KV type**, so `-ctk q4_0 -ctv q4_0` (or `q8_0`, or `bf16`) takes the draft with it; set either one to pin the draft's own type, f16 included.  Upstream defaults both to **f16 unconditionally**, which is invisible VRAM: on a dense 27B MTP run at `-c 131072` the draft context's single attention layer held **512 MiB** of f16 KV against **144 MiB** at q4_0, on a 16 GiB card already out of budget (issue #216).  The K and V flags are independent, but the delivery hard-rejects a mixed pair anyway (`type_k != type_v`), so set both if you set one.  Cannot change a generated token (the draft only proposes; the target verifies) and the same-seed greedy text is byte-identical between the two draft KV types; decode is unchanged within noise on the models the delivery is tuned for (3 alternating reps each: 56.4-56.6 t/s f16 vs 56.4-56.5 q4_0 at `-c 8192 -n 400`, and 80.8-80.9 vs 80.6-80.7 t/s at `-c 102400 -n 3000` on the dense 27B; 190.4 vs 190.8 t/s on 35B-A3B `-ncmoe 40`), because a quantized draft cache simply takes the native quantized-KV FA arm (block 05).  A **fast small model under `-sm tensor`** does pay, by **1.5-3 % (mean ~2 %)**: 4B Q8_0, 3 GPU tensor, `-ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0`, MTP n3 = 237.7/236.1/240.0 t/s (f16 draft KV) vs 232.0/237.2/233.7 (q8_0), text identical.  It is not GPU work (a kernel trace of both arms is identical, -0.04 %); a quantized KV row needs an F32 source for `SET_ROWS`, so the write costs a few extra host-side node evaluations per step.  1 GPU and `-sm layer` are neutral.  `-ctkd f16 -ctvd f16` restores the f16 draft on any of these.  With `-ctk` left at f16 nothing changes. |
| `LLAMA_ARG_SPEC_DRAFT_MTP_VOCAB` | unset/`0` = off | **opt-in tuning** (r107, block 13) | env alias for `--spec-draft-mtp-vocab N`: the MTP draft head scores only the LM-head rows for token ids < N plus every control/user-defined id, and scatters the logits into a full-vocab row of `-inf`.  The target's verification is untouched, so output is unchanged by construction and only proposals are lost.  **N is payload-specific**: 98304 on Qwen3.8-27B Q8_0 with `--spec-draft-n-max 7` (four-axis `-n 3000`: R +1.3 %, P +1.7 %, C +2.4 %, K +0.8 %; acceptance 0.6193 -> 0.6194), 64000 is a *loss* there at n-max 9, and gemma4's only lossless cut is 245760/262144 (a no-op).  Costs `N/n_vocab` of the LM head in VRAM (510 MiB at 98304 on the 27B, per device under `-sm tensor`) and is **not** in the `--fit` estimate.  Purity holds inside `n_max <= 7` (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §48).  **Device count decides whether it pays** (r109 validation): the subset head is *mirrored* on every device under `-sm tensor` while the untrimmed head is *split*, so its draft matmul costs `N` columns per device versus `n_vocab / n_devices` — enable it only when **`N * n_devices < n_vocab`**.  On the 27B (vocab 248320) 98304 is +1.4 % aggregate at 2 GPU (share 124160) and **-2.2 % at 3 GPU** (share 82773, the delivery's server shape), and at 3 GPU no N pays: 64000 is below the share but loses acceptance (0.68878 -> 0.67260) for -9.4 %.  Depth 16384 is flat.  Record: `archive/work/spec-draft-mtp-vocab/VALIDATION-2026-10-22.md` |
| `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN_WIDTH_BAND` | **16** (r185, block 02) | tuning (opt-in; `0` = off) | the verify **width** that bounds the aligned draft depth on a config with **no** expert-cache arena (`band == 0`: every all-in-VRAM run, `-sm layer`, or a model with no routed expert).  `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN` bounds the aligned depth by `band / n_drafting - 1` rows per sequence, and since r176 `band` answers only for a model with host-resident experts -- so on a band-0 config a multi-slot draft was **unbounded** and four slots at `--spec-draft-n-max 7` presented ~28-32 rows, which leaves the decode kernels' small-batch families (MMVQ/MMVF -> MMQ).  The bound is now `width / n_drafting - 1`, where `width` is the arena band when one is active (unchanged) and this value otherwise.  **16 and not 8**: at the two widths the fixed-depth arms measure 16 rows ahead on 3 of 4 shapes (35B-A3B 3 GPU 432.7 vs 389.8 t/s, 2 GPU 387.2 vs 359.6; dense 27B 3 GPU 240.1 vs 211.2; qwen4exp Flash-Next GSQ-IQ3_XXS 2 GPU + a resident PLE table is the exception, 204.6 vs 220.8), so "above ~8 rows each extra draft token costs more than it gains" (PR #169's premise) does not hold: the *unbounded* width was the defect, not width 8.  Measured with 4 concurrent 300-token greedy requests, one binary, band on vs off: **35B-A3B 3 GPU 330.8 -> 434.7 t/s (+31.4 %)**, qwen4exp IQ3_XXS 2 GPU + PLE **175.5 -> 208.9 (+19.0 %)**, dense 27B 3 GPU **218.5 -> 249.6 (+14.2 %)**, and a fixed `draft-mtp` `n_max 7` arm 310.9 -> 362.8 (+16.7 %).  3 slots also gain (+16.9 %); **1 and 2 slots are inert** (16 / 2 - 1 = 7 = the default `--spec-draft-n-max`, and the 2-slot text stays byte-identical), so it is a pure multi-slot change and no single-sequence arm moves.  A host-expert config takes the arena band and is untouched (same code path, verified in the log).  `0` (or `1`) disables the bound (A/B and bisect only).  Gate: `scripts/gate-spec-width-band.sh`.  Record: `benchmarks/2026-10-31-multislot-verify-width.md`. |
| `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN` | **on** (r153, blocks 02/09) | kill-switch | aligns the drafts of the sequences that verify in the **same** step to one length, so the target batch is one ubatch instead of one per distinct draft length.  `llama_memory_hybrid::init_batch()` splits a decode with `llama_batch_allocr::split_equal()`, which takes one token per sequence set per round and **stops when the shortest set runs out**: if two slots verify different draft lengths, the longer one's tail becomes a SECOND ubatch and the target runs a full extra pass for that step.  The fixed `--spec-draft-n-max` path never produced a difference (every slot drafts `n_max`), but `draft-mtp-adaptive` holds a **per-sequence** depth, so two concurrently verifying slots routinely disagree -- i.e. this is the multi-slot server shape (`--parallel >= 2 --kv-unified`), and it is invisible to every single-sequence gate.  `draft-mtp-adaptive` gives every drafting sequence the deepest cap in the same call, bounded by the smallest HARD cap in the call (`--spec-draft-n-max`, the MTP layer cap, and the caller's per-slot `n_max`, which the server lowers as a response runs out of tokens) and by the expert cache's band.  The alignment is deliberately **inside that one drafter**: the generic `min` cut of the finished drafts (which r153 also shipped) measured -6 % on the n-gram-first chain, because it truncates the OTHER drafter's good draft, so it is gone and a chain of different drafters keeps its previous behaviour exactly (measured inert, identical draft counts; gated by the gate's mixed-drafter arm).  Measured on 27B UD-Q4_K_XL greedy `-n 400`, aggregate t/s of a concurrent `recall` + `reasoning` pair: **104.8-108.3 -> 140.1-140.6** at 2 GPU (**+30..34 %**) and **86.5-87.0 -> 133.1-133.7** at 3 GPU (**+54 %**); the host-expert shape (35B-A3B `-ncmoe 40`) **116.2 -> 149.0** (**+28 %**); the split decode steps go 58 -> 0 at each.  The equal-length arm is unchanged (-0.4..-2.1 %), and a single drafting sequence is untouched by construction (`llama-cli`, `llama-bench` and every single-sequence gate produce byte-identical text).  On a host-expert config the aligned depth is bounded by `band / n_drafting - 1` rows per sequence so the whole verify batch stays inside the expert cache's band (see §1.2 and `LLAMA_SPEC_NGRAM_MOD_BAND_CLAMP`).  Three consequences to know: the low-acceptance slot of a mixed pair verifies at its neighbour's width, so this is a **new numerics reference** for that config, and `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN=0` and `=1` differ deterministically, exactly as `--spec-draft-n-max 4` vs `5` do (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §63), so a multi-slot server upgraded across this change can produce different, equally valid text; a **single-slot** request (and every `n_seq == 1` run) is byte-identical and reproducible, but a **two-slot aligned pair is not reliably stable run to run** (measured at 3 GPUs: one unchanged binary landed in two basins, 1237 and 1232 characters with a 9 % t/s difference between them), because the alignment makes one slot's verify width depend on its neighbour's state and on the batch composition -- so judge a change by the single-slot arms and by the split count, never by a two-slot text diff; and a slot that cannot draft at all (its last token, or a full context) still splits that step, deliberately: that second ubatch carries the neighbour's genuine draft rows, and both removing it and cutting the neighbour's draft measured neutral (`TODO.md` #111, analysed and declined -- `benchmarks/2026-10-28-spec-align-111.md`), so the gate **counts and bounds** those single-row splits (`TAIL_MAX`, default 2) instead of requiring none.  `0` restores the r152 behaviour (A/B and bisect only).  Gate: `scripts/gate-spec-align.sh`.  Record: `archive/work/spec-align-234/`. |
| `LLAMA_DRAFT_SCHED2` | **on** (r65, block 09) | kill-switch | runs the draft context's second graph on a second scheduler (`llama_context::sched_alt`), so its two alternating graphs both stay allocated and are reused instead of re-split/re-allocated every cycle.  Which graph goes there is per arch (`llama_context::sched_alt_for`): MTP sends the graphs **with outputs** (its no-output ingest stays on the main scheduler), a DFlash/DFlash2 draft sends the **draft step** and identifies it by the batch kind, because its draft block has no outputs either (the lattice is read from `h_nextn`, not the logits) and `n_outputs` cannot tell it from the KV injection (r120, issue #208: reuse 0 -> 97.8 %).  Needs a per-scheduler meta view slot and a `slab_compute_plain` device-iface entry (the second scheduler's small COMPUTE buffer bypasses the slab).  On Qwen3.8-Flash-Next + shared MTP (`-sm tensor`, 2 GPUs) the draft's graph reallocations drop 1068 -> 34 and generation is about +1.5 %; kill switch `0` restores the single scheduler.  The second scheduler depends on block 00's graph-layout fingerprint (r84, issue #183): a draft graph that keeps its node count but changes its node order or output marking (another sequence's in-graph sampling chain) re-reserves instead of reusing a layout computed for a different graph. |
| `GGML_LF_DFLASH_DEV` | **on** | kill-switch | DFlash device-resident layer-input features (F1).  `0` uses the host path.  Default-on since r26; if the device buffers cannot be allocated the target warns once and stays on the host path for the rest of the context.  Since **r106** a Meta (tensor-split) layer-input buffer forces the host path regardless of this variable (the device copy is one pointer range, which the meta backend cannot express; the device path there aborted).  Since r53 F1 also covers several sequences (`--parallel >= 2`); with host-resident MoE experts (`-ncmoe`/`-cmoe`, `llama_model_moe_host_expert_dev_count(...) > 0`) several sequences stay on the host path and log a warning (the movable-boundary slab's device reads, issue #127 MoE form).  One sequence is unchanged.  **Since r163** the target's graph writes the layer inputs into the device store itself (`GGML_OP_SET_ROWS`, issue #240), so they stop being graph outputs and the pinned host rows the host path reads are only reserved when that path runs (see `GGML_LF_DFLASH_DEV_GRAPH`). |
| `GGML_LF_DFLASH_DEV_GRAPH` | **on** | kill-switch (r163, block 12) | writes the extracted layer inputs into F1's persistent device store **from inside the target graph** instead of marking them as graph outputs and copying them after the graph.  A graph output cannot be reused by the allocator, so every extracted input stayed live to the end of the target graph: 5 x `n_embd x n_ubatch` f32, **188.8 MiB** of compute buffer at `-ub 2048` on a 27B (757.26 vs 568.42 MiB with no extraction at all, issue #240).  The in-graph copy is a `SET_ROWS` per extracted layer, placed immediately after the layer that reads the input, with this batch's row offsets as a graph input (an `I32 [n_tokens]` tensor), so a graph with the same shape stays reusable across ubatches at different offsets and a captured HIP graph replays with the right rows.  The store is created **before** the first graph that writes it (the context re-reserves its compute buffers once, so the smaller layout is what the run settles on), and the wait for the draft's previous read moves before the graph.  The store is placed on the device that owns the first extracted layer; a layer input on another device (a `-sm layer` split boundary, e.g. layers 48/62 on the second card) is copied across by the scheduler.  `0` is the pre-r163 shape (inputs are graph outputs, copied after the graph) for A/B and bisect: greedy text, draft acceptance and the per-position acceptance rates are identical in both arms, and the A/B costs 132 MiB of compute buffer.  `-sm tensor` (Meta layer inputs), a CPU-offloaded first extracted layer, and an allocation failure all decline the in-graph store and keep the host path regardless of this variable, with one log line naming the reason.  An embedding (image/audio) ubatch is the one case that still fills the host rows: it has no token ids, so a draft reads its features from the host.  Those rows are reserved on demand, so a text-only run never allocates them and a multimodal one pays them for the rest of the process. |

The tuned adaptive-MTP controller is selected with **`--spec-type draft-mtp-adaptive`** (a CLI flag, not
an environment variable).  Plain `--spec-type draft-mtp` does not use it.

**The verify-width rule (r123, TODO #59).**  Every speculator's draft length is a *verify width*: the
target decodes `n_draft + 1` rows, and the MoE expert cache serves that batch from its arena only up to
its decode/verify band (`MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MAX_TOK`; see §1.2).  Above the band the routed experts are
streamed from the pinned host master on every step, so on a config with host-resident experts keep every
draft **inside** the band: `--spec-draft-n-max` is clamped to 15 by `LLAMA_SPEC_DRAFT_N_MAX_CLAMP` (16-row
verify = the RDNA4 band) and the n-gram speculators are clamped automatically (see
`LLAMA_SPEC_NGRAM_MOD_BAND_CLAMP` above).  On RDNA3/NVIDIA the band is 8, so a deeper MTP draft there logs
a warning and the user decides (the 15 bound is a correctness one, in `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §27; the width rule
is §50).  With `--parallel >= 2` the *batch* is the sum over the verifying slots, which is a second reason
`LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN` exists: two slots at depth 7 already fill an RDNA4 band, so the alignment bounds itself to
`width / n_drafting - 1` rows each rather than letting one deep slot and one shallow slot drift into a split --
`width` being the arena band above, or `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN_WIDTH_BAND` (default 16) when there is no arena.

---

## 4. Model loading and context

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_TENSOR_SPLIT_ALLOW_DEGENERATE` | unset | **kill-switch / escape hatch** (r250, block 09) | `1` skips the load-time guard that refuses a degenerate `-sm tensor` split (`TODO` #135).  The guard fires only when **all three** hold: 2+ devices, a **host-resident** expert table (`-ncmoe`/`-lme`), and a granularity-rounded per-device share of that table's split axis that leaves a device with **zero** columns -- measured on gfx1201 35B-A3B UD-Q4_K_M `-ncmoe 40`, decode **109.7 t/s at 2 devices against 20.5 at 3** (Q3_K_M 105.0/23.0; Q8_0 95.7/90.2, whose 32-element blocks leave every device a share; the same 3-device arm all-VRAM is 130.9).  It is geometry-derived (the loader's own `ne_s*(j+1)/n` rule floored to `lcm(blck_size,128)` for FFN tensors), never a model list, and it is silent otherwise: a mirror-the-whole-table fallback by the meta split is what makes the arm slow, not wrong.  An explicit `--tensor-split` zero share is treated as a placement choice and is not refused.  Set this only to *measure* such a shape. |
| `LLAMA_MMAP_HOST_EXPERTS` | — | **removed (r33)** | the legacy alias for `--host-experts`.  The pageable `--host-experts mmap` mode was dropped in r33 (issue #116): a no-XNACK GPU (gfx1201 reports `XNACK enabled: NO`) cannot read a pageable host master in a kernel, so `-ncmoe`/`-cmoe` experts are always pinned (`ROCm_Host`).  The variable is now ignored.  The bounded pinned DIO host tier that replaces the reclaimable-page-cache idea is the `archive/work/host-expert-dio-cache/` campaign. |
| `LLAMA_LOAD_RELEASE_MMAP` | **on** (block 09 in the current blocks — the r49 change now lives there; r209 added the Windows half) | kill-switch | unmaps each model-mapping range right after the tensor is copied to its device buffer, and skips the up-front whole-file prefetch, so a `-ncmoe` load does not hold the full file mapping and the pinned host expert master at the same time.  `madvise(MADV_DONTNEED)` is not enough here (the ROCm runtime re-faults those pages on the next pageable H2D copy), so the range is munmapped; the exact ranges a host-pointer tensor points into (a tied embedding is loaded host-side and device-side from the same range) are preserved.  `0` restores the pre-r49 prefetch-then-unmap-at-end behaviour.  **On Windows (r209)** the release used to do nothing: `unmap_fragment` was a no-op there, so every range copied to VRAM or to a pinned host buffer stayed resident next to its copy (reporter: Flash-Next `--fit`, working set after load 67.4 -> 21.2 GiB, free RAM 5.3 -> 48.9 GiB).  A view cannot be unmapped in part, so the range is dropped from the working set (`VirtualUnlock`, clean file pages to the standby list) instead of unmapped; it still unlocks any locked page in the range, as `munmap` does on POSIX. |  **r273:** the released range is also `posix_fadvise(POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED)`ed right after the `munmap`.  That is only possible *after* the unmap (fadvise skips mapped pages), and it is what stops a large offload from leaving the uploaded weights resident in the page cache next to the device copy — the cheap half of "do not flush the system page cache for weights that are already placed", with no change to the read transport.  Measured on a 4.3 GiB model: peak `Cached` during load **5.15 GiB without the release vs 1.99 GiB with it**. |
| `LLAMA_TENSOR_HOST_BUFT` | unset | tuning | host buffer type for overridden tensors. |
| `LLAMA_DEVICE_INPUT` | off | **opt-in** | device-side input handling. |
| `LLAMA_OUTPUT_PINNED` | **on** (r65, block 12) | kill-switch | when the output device is a meta (tensor-split) device with no host buffer type of its own, `output_reserve` uses the first simple device's pinned host type for the output buffer (logits/embeddings/MTP hidden state).  Without it those per-step D2H reads land in pageable memory and each blocks the host; the output buffer is never scheduled on, so its `.device` does not matter.  About +2.7 % MTP decode on Flash-Next (`-sm tensor`, 2 GPUs) and about +4.7 % on the dense 27B; `0` restores the CPU-buffer fallback.  The meta device's own `get_host_buffer_type` is unchanged. |
| `LLAMA_DROP_COMPUTE_BUFFERS` | **on** (every tool) | tuning / kill-switch | drop the wide-prefill compute layout at the prefill→decode transition so a wide `-ub` and a large arena coexist (`-ub 8192` cache-auto cli decode 45.6 → 78.7 t/s).  Follows `common_params::drop_compute_buffers`; set the env to `0`/`1` to override.  **A server may drop too** (this was cli-only until r22): the movable-boundary slab reclaims a later wide layout with a boundary move, which is verified on `wide1 → short → wide2` at `-ub 8192` (0 aborts, all coherent).  `0` is only needed where the compute layout CANNOT be reclaimed — i.e. with `GGML_CUDA_SLAB=0`. |
| `LLAMA_DROP_EXTRA_RESERVE_MIB` | `0` | tuning | when the drop fires, VRAM held out of the arena per device for a later compute growth.  Default `0` — the compute-buffer margin covers the small post-drop growth and the arena's layer-uniform re-size absorbs fragmentation, so a reserve only costs arena. |
| `LLAMA_LAZY_BUF_MB` / `LLAMA_LAZY_IO_THREADS` / `LLAMA_LAZY_READER_STATS` | unset | tuning / diagnostic | lazy-mode buffer size, reader thread count, reader statistics.  `--lazy-mode off` (`LLAMA_ARG_LAZY_MODE=off`) keeps the `TENSOR_READ_LAZY` tables (PLE/engram) **resident even under `--load-mode mmap`** (r122, block 00): the tensor is read into its own host buffer instead of being served from the mapping, so mmap keeps its fast mapped load for the bulk of the model.  `auto` below its 4 GiB threshold and `on` are unchanged.  `--lazy-mode mlock` (`LLAMA_ARG_LAZY_MODE=mlock`, r230) is the copy-free equivalent: the `on` data path (rows read from the mapping on demand) plus one populate + `mlock` of **every** marked table at load (no 4 GiB threshold), so the table is pinned in the page cache instead of duplicated (27,465 MiB table -> `VmLck` 26.82 GiB, `RssAnon` 1.3 GB) and the pin is released when the process exits.  It needs `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` >= the table size (`ulimit -l`); a refused `mlock` warns and leaves the table on the evictable page-cache path. |
| `LLAMA_LAZY_MLOCK_RESERVE_MIB` | **`1024`** (r273) | tuning / safety | the `MemAvailable` floor that `--lazy-mode mlock` / `mlockr` must leave behind.  Those modes populate the WHOLE marked table into the page cache and this runs at the very END of the load, when the model mapping, the host-expert pool and the arena reserve are already committed — a table larger than what is left turns the populate into a reclaim storm, and for `mlock` into unevictable memory the run can never give back (measured shape: `MemAvailable` bottoming at 1.0 GiB, VmRSS 76.1 GiB, a 13 t/s rep).  If `table_bytes + reserve > MemAvailable` the whole pin is **DECLINED with one WARN** and the tables keep the ON-DEMAND path — `lazy.has()` is already true, so rows are read from the mapping as they are used, i.e. exactly the `--lazy-mode auto` behaviour.  Declining is a policy choice, not a failure: nothing is pinned, nothing is copied, and the pages stay reclaimable.  `0` disables the check. |
| `LLAMA_PLE_DIRECT` / `LLAMA_PLE_THREADS` / `LLAMA_PLE_DEBUG` | **on** / `8` (capped by `--threads`) / unset | kill-switch / tuning / diagnostic (r243, block 11) | the qwen4exp **host PLE gather** (`llm_graph_input_ple::set_input`).  `LLAMA_PLE_DIRECT=0` puts the rows through the `emb_scratch` vector + `ggml_backend_tensor_set` again instead of dequantizing them straight into the graph input; the in-place write only happens when that input lives in a host buffer (a device/split/RPC buffer keeps the scratch path), and it is the same bytes either way.  `LLAMA_PLE_THREADS` is the fan-out **ceiling**: the gather is split over one thread per 4096 rows, so fewer than 8192 rows (decode) stays on the calling thread, and the width is the smallest of this ceiling, `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` and `cparams.n_threads_batch` (`--threads` / `-tb`), i.e. **`--threads` decides it** unless this is set lower; `=1` is the single-thread A/B arm.  Read once (static).  `LLAMA_PLE_DEBUG=1` prints one INFO line per `set_input` (so `-lv 4`): `ple gather: <rows> rows, <threads> threads, direct=<0|1>` -- use it to prove an arm is not vacuous.  Measured (2 R9700, `-sm tensor`, Flash-Next GSQ-RCO IQ3_S, 31.5k prompt): prefill **+1.31 %** on the `-ub 4096` delivery arm (2338.5 -> 2369.2 t/s) and +1.16 % at `-ub 4096` without the attendance pool, but **neutral** at `-ub 6144` (+0.06 %; 101k +0.32 %) -- the saving is per ubatch boundary (65 536 rows / 41.9 MB at `-ub 4096`), which the host-expert fill hides at the wider ubatch.  Isolated on one binary: threads +0.69 %, direct write +0.17 %.  Decode is unchanged (one thread below 8192 rows), greedy text and prefill logits are byte-identical (`INVARIANTS.md` r243). |
| `LLAMA_LAYER_INP_EARLY` | **on** (r244, block 12) | kill-switch | where an extracted DFlash/DSpark layer input is copied into the device store (`build_layer_inp_dev()`, upstream #31506's order), the copy's four nodes (VIEW, RESHAPE, SET_ROWS and the index use) are moved to just after the next layer's input node instead of being appended after the whole graph.  Only the node **order** moves -- every node computes the same -- so the allocator can free each extracted layer input after its last reader instead of holding all of them to the end.  Measured on dense 27B UD-Q4_K_XL + DFlash2 (5 layers) at `-ub 2048`: the target's compute buffer 756.97 -> **624.99 MiB** (`llama-cli`, `-np 1`) / **580.99 MiB** (server, `-np 2 --kv-unified`), steady-state VRAM on the card -185 MB, and greedy text plus draft counts identical in every arm (1 GPU, 2- and 3-GPU `-sm tensor`/`-sm layer`).  The move is skipped (the copy stays where upstream put it) when the next layer's input is not a node of its own or its source is recorded later.  `LLAMA_LAYER_INP_EARLY=0` restores upstream's order -- the A/B arm.  Read once (static). |
| `LLAMA_LOAD_STAGE_MB` | `4` | tuning (r122, block 00) | size in MiB of each of the 4 pinned staging slots the non-mmap (`--load-mode none`) loader streams a tensor through on its way to a device.  The old 1 MiB slot measured 15.7 s on the cold Flash-Next load (`-sm tensor`), 2 MiB 13.7, **4 MiB 13.7**, 8 MiB 14.8, 32 MiB 16.3, 64 MiB 16.8; larger slots scale better on faster disks but cost 4x the pinned footprint.  DIO reads keep their own 64 MiB buffer. |
| `LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_MIN` | `256` | tuning (**opt-in**) | raises the n_kv padding floor (r25).  n_kv is padded to this many cells, so a larger value keeps the per-device tensor-split graphs constant for longer (the PR measured `1024` as the useful value, `4096` gave nothing more).  Default unchanged. |
| `LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_PREFILL` | `8192` on a multi-device split, `0` on a single device | default-on (r79, block 10); `0` disables | a coarser n_kv pad for **prompt ubatches only** (`n_tokens > 64`), rounded up to a power of two, so consecutive prompt chunks share one graph shape instead of rebuilding the split state and re-allocating every ubatch.  It applies only once the decode-padded occupancy is past `LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_PREFILL_FROM`, so a model's small-context shortcuts (qwen4exp's dense attention for the first ~2051 cells) still apply; decode/verify batches keep the decode pad, and the extra cells are masked exactly as before (prefill logits are unchanged).  **Default-on only when more than one device is in use** (`model.n_devices() > 1` or the meta split state has more than one device); a single GPU keeps the r77 default of `0` because the coarse pad only adds masked attention work there (-0.35 % measured), and that shape is now inactive.  The env value still overrides the default (`0` disables).  Measured on r79, `-ub 2048 -b 2048`, q8_0 KV, 31 k-token prompt: **+2.5 %** prefill on 35B-A3B (3 GPUs, `-sm tensor`), **+0.8 %** on the dense 27B (3 GPUs, tensor), and **+42 % / +49 %** on a 3-GPU `-sm layer` split (MoE / dense), with the decoded text identical; the r77 2-GPU numbers were +2.0 % (MoE) and +1.0 % (dense), and the 1-GPU shape is inactive.  It affects the **unified** KV cache only (not the iswa/DSA caches) and is not for training, whose graphs assert `n_kv == n_tokens`. |
| `LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_PREFILL_FROM` | `4096` | tuning (r77, block 10) | the decode-padded occupancy (cells) above which `LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_PREFILL` starts to apply.  Compared with `GGML_PAD(used, LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_MIN)`, not with the raw used count. |
| `GGML_READBACK_SYNC` | **on** | kill-switch (block 06, r43) | completes the per-ubatch device->host logits/embd readbacks before the next ubatch's graph reuses the same device output tensor.  Required because ROCm's pinned async D2H copy is not ordered against the following compute, which otherwise lets an earlier ubatch's host rows receive a later ubatch's data (multi-ubatch per-token outputs: perplexity / KLD).  `0` restores the unordered readback for A/B.  A single-ubatch decode is unaffected either way. |
| `LLAMA_STATE_GET_MERGE_GAP` | `262144` (256 KiB) | tuning (r56, block 12) | in `llama_io_write_host` (the state / prompt-cache save), consecutive deferred `write_tensor` ranges of one tensor whose gaps are at most this many bytes are read as one `ggml_backend_tensor_get` span and scattered on the host; contiguous ranges are read straight into the output.  Under concurrent decoding a slot save was ~8,000-10,000 short synchronous reads on ROCm (340-400 ms); merged it is one get per tensor (25-65 ms).  The saved bytes are identical.  `0` restores the per-range reads. |
| `LLAMA_STATE_ASYNC` | **on** (r96, block 12) | kill-switch | the save side (and the shared enable for both directions): `llama_io_write_host` queues its deferred state-save reads as `ggml_backend_tensor_get_async` into one pinned staging buffer kept by the context, then synchronizes each backend once and copies to the caller's buffer, instead of one synchronous read per range per device.  A recurrent model's checkpoint is about 75 small ranges, so under `-sm tensor` it paid about 150 device syncs before every prompt chunk of a follow-up turn (Flash-Next + MTP, 2 GPU: 55-59 ms of TTFT on the short follow-up turns).  `0` restores the synchronous path for both saves and restores.  The staged bytes are identical. |
| `LLAMA_STATE_ASYNC_MAX_MIB` | `256` | tuning (r96, block 12) | the largest state (tensor bytes) that takes the batched path, in either direction; a larger state, which includes every whole-slot save with a real KV cache, keeps the synchronous path, so no save or load pins gigabytes.  The staging buffer is **retained by the context** across saves and restores and rounded up to 16 MiB (about 64 MiB for the Flash-Next checkpoint), and it only ever grows, so a small save after a large one keeps the large allocation.  It is one buffer per context: saves and restores on one context must not run concurrently.  Raising this raises the per-context pinned ceiling by the same amount. |
| `LLAMA_STATE_ASYNC_READ` | **on** (r119, block 12) | kill-switch | the restore side: `llama_io_read_host` packs the deferred pieces into the context's pinned `state_stage`, queues each with `ggml_backend_tensor_set_async` on the backend that owns it, and synchronizes each backend once, instead of one synchronous set per tensor and device.  A hybrid model's checkpoint is ~75 pieces, and under `-sm tensor` each synchronous set bounced through a pinned chunk and synchronized every device.  Measured on 2 GPU `-sm tensor`, 1571-token checkpoint, q8_0 KV: 35B-A3B restore 49 -> 40 ms, Flash-Next GSQ 89 -> 75 ms, and the DFlash2 draft arm 68 -> 58 ms.  One GPU is about 2 ms slower because the extra staging copy has no multi-device sync to save; that is accepted for the multi-device win.  `0` keeps the per-tensor synchronous restore; the save side is unaffected.  The variable is read once (static).  The stage is shared with the save path, so a save and a restore on the same context must not run concurrently. |
| `LLAMA_STATE_PIPE_GROUPS` | `8` (r151, block 12) | kill-switch (`1` = off) | how many size-balanced groups the batched state copy (above) is cut into, so the host memcpy into/out of the pinned `state_stage` overlaps the device copies of another group.  A restore packs a group and queues its async sets before packing the next (the groups own disjoint stage ranges, so a queued copy is never overwritten); a save records one event per (backend, group) after that group's copies are queued and copies the group out when its event completes.  A backend with no events (the CPU backend) falls back to the single `llama_state_sync_all` path, which is also what `1` selects.  Clamped to `8` (the per-context event array size).  Measured on a prompt-cache restore of a 5246-token hybrid state: Flash-Next GSQ + MTP head 2 GPU `-sm tensor -ncmoe 40` 134.5 -> 129.7 ms, 35B-A3B Q4_K_M 1 GPU 69.4 -> 67.4 ms, 35B-A3B 2 GPU tensor within noise; the restored bytes are identical (`scripts/gate-state-pipe.sh`).  Read once (static). |
| `LLAMA_STATE_COPY_THREADS` | `2` (r152, block 12) | tuning (`0` = kill-switch) | how the host side of the batched state copy moves a checkpoint's bytes: `0` = the pre-r152 plain single-threaded `memcpy` per piece; `1` = non-temporal (streaming, SSE2) stores on one thread; `N` = the same stores split over `N` threads inside each copy group (default 2).  A plain store reads the destination line into the cache first (read-for-ownership) and a streaming store does not, which is the whole win where the checkpoint does not fit in the CPU's last-level cache (the submitter's 3900X + DDR4: 150 MiB 19.4 -> 11.8 ms on one thread) and about 0 where it does (this box: a 9950X3D has 192 MiB of L3 and a 164 MiB checkpoint's destination lines are mostly resident); the **threads** are where this box's win is (164 MiB in a 35B-A3B checkpoint's real piece sizes: 6.4 ms plain, 6.4 ms streamed on one thread, 3.0 ms on two, 3.3-3.7 ms on three or four).  Workers stay inside one pipe group, because a group's stage range may only be read after its event.  States below 16 MiB keep the plain copy (a thread hand-off then costs more than the copy), pieces below 64 KiB are copied with `memcpy`, and the streaming store is x86-only (`__SSE2__` / `_M_X64`), so other targets gain only the threads.  Measured on `scripts/gate-state-pipe.sh` (prompt-cache restore of a 5246-token state): the copy itself -12..-29 % per size class and -26/-30 % for the restore's pack, the server's save+load trace 39.9 -> 33.4 ms (35B-A3B 1 GPU), 44.2 -> 40.5 ms (2 GPU tensor) and 79.3 -> 74.9 ms (Flash-Next GSQ + MTP, 2 GPU); the restored bytes are identical to `LLAMA_STATE_COPY_THREADS=0` and to `LLAMA_STATE_ASYNC=0`.  Read once (static). |
| `LLAMA_STATE_STAGE_MIB` | `64` | tuning (`0` = kill-switch) | the band size of the **device-state** copy (`llama_state_seq_*` with `LLAMA_STATE_SEQ_FLAGS_ON_DEVICE`, block 12, r157).  That path keeps a copy of a sequence's memory in a device buffer, and on a tensor split the copy between the two meta buffers has to bounce through the host; each band of pieces is queued with `ggml_backend_tensor_get_async`/`set_async` on the backends that own them and synchronized once per band, instead of `ggml_backend_tensor_copy`'s pageable `malloc` + two synchronous copies per piece.  A 1024-token 2-slot state (213.6 MiB, 128 pieces) on 2 GPUs went from 2.23 s to save / 2.28 s to restore to **14.7 / 17.2 ms** (8 MiB per band already gets 15.3/18.4 ms, so the value only has to amortise a synchronize); the single-device arm is a real device-to-device copy and never reaches this, so it is unchanged (3.0/3.2 ms).  A single piece larger than the band gets a band of its own and grows the context's pinned `state_stage` to hold it (it is never cut, because the meta backend only serves a window aligned to the tensor's own layout).  `0` restores the previous per-piece path, which is also its A/B arm.  Read once (static). |
| `LLAMA_STATE_TIMING` | unset | diagnostic (r152, block 12) | logs one line per batched state read/write with the piece count, the MiB, the group count and where the time went (`state get: enqueue / copy+wait / GiB/s`, `state set: pack+enqueue`).  It is the instrument the r152 copy measurements were taken with; never default-on. |

---

## 5. CPU MoE offload threading

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_CPU_MOE_OFFLOAD_THREADS` | derived (block-06 cap) | tuning | overrides the thread cap for `-ncmoe` offloaded-MoE decode.  The cap only covers that case. |
| `GGML_CPU_DISABLE_TINY_GRAPH_SINGLE_THREAD` | off | diagnostic | disables the single-thread path for tiny graphs. |

Thread sizing is **not** an environment variable and is not optional: `/usr/local/bin/pin_gpu_irqs.sh`
puts the GPU IRQs on the top `NUM_GPUS` cores, and the default `-t` puts the worker pool there and
starves the GPU.  Size `-t` (or `--cpu-mask` with `-t` inside the mask) to leave those cores free.
See `AGENTS.md`.

---

## 6. Multi-GPU comms

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE` | `auto` on Linux/HIP (r165); `hybrid` on CUDA | tuning | `auto` (Linux/HIP default since r165): the **internal pipeline** for decode/verify-sized tensors and a **large-tensor arm chosen once, at the first large all-reduce**: RCCL's ring when the model offloads experts to host memory (`g_comm_host_experts`, set by the slab block), the **copy-engine (CE)** arm otherwise.  Peer access is **never enabled** by this chain, which is the point: a device with peer access enabled charges the *host submission path* ~9 % on a replaying decode (27B Q8_0 3x R9700 `-sm tensor tg128` 42.25 -> 38.77 t/s) while the GPU critical path is unchanged, and the cost is not reversible after RCCL has made its mappings (`TODO #109` closed at r165).  `hybrid` (the pre-r165 default) keeps RCCL with P2P for both arms -- for a topology with genuinely cheap peer links (xGMI/scale-up), otherwise 6-9 % slower on a dense decode; `ce` pins the copy-engine half with no RCCL at all; `q8ce` (r259) is the **same chain with an 8-bit wire**: the large-tensor payload is sent as q8_0 blocks of 32-point Walsh-Hadamard-rotated values (34 B per 32 values, 53 % of the bf16 bytes; the rotation is orthogonal and its own inverse, so the ranks add in the rotated domain).  **`q8ce` is LOSSY and therefore only ever entered by name** -- it is an algorithm of its own, not a knob on `ce`, and nothing else (not `auto`) turns it on: mean KLD 0.000697 / same-top-p 98.667 % against the bf16 wire on the delivery's own 2-device `-sm tensor` point (27B Q8_0, f16 KV, 40 chunks) and 0.000781 / 98.824 % on 3 devices, i.e. it passes the prefill-logit gate but it is an approximation, not a rounding-level change.  It reaches every tensor that is a multiple of 32 elements and any rank count up to 17; a non-multiple, a rank count above that, or a decode/verify-sized tensor (the internal pipeline) keeps the bf16 wire, so decode is untouched.  Worth **+12.9 % prefill** on 3x R9700 (27B Q8_0 `pp4096` 2130 -> 2404 t/s), **+9.5 %** on 35B-A3B Q8_0, **+2.5 %** on the 2-device x8 pair, decode flat on 3 devices (and -0.7 % `tg128` on the 2-device pair, where a 64-token all-reduce is above the CE crossover), and +17-21 % on a bandwidth-starved pair (contributor's and a reporter's measurements).  `internal`; `nccl`; `none` / `meta` (the butterfly, the pre-r165 baseline -- the same path, two spellings).  **`auto` is an accepted value and means the unset default** (r272): before r272 the literal string `auto` -- the very name this default is documented under -- fell through the unknown-value branch, so a run that asked for the default got the butterfly instead (measured **-5 %** on a 2-device host-expert prefill, `benchmarks/2026-11-12-allreduce-ce-single-exchange.md`).  An unrecognised value still WARNs and takes the butterfly, and the line now says so.  `nccl` is **not** bit-identical under `-sm tensor` (the internal AR BF16-round-trips), so use it as a smoke comparison only.  Numbers per shape: `benchmarks/2026-10-28-allreduce-peer-access.md`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_SPIN_TIMEOUT_MS` | `2000` (r59, block 01) | **diagnostic / tuning** | the internal all-reduce's **soft** spin deadline in ms, measured with `wall_clock64()` at the device's wall-clock rate.  A peer wait that exceeds it is a late peer (a driver queue stop), not a lost one: the kernel reports it once and **keeps waiting**, so the reduction still completes.  `0` disables the soft check (and, with it, the hard cap below).  Before r59 the budget was a `clock64()` cycle count with a 20 ms default; on gfx1201 `clock64()` can step backwards while a queue is stopped, so the budget wrapped and a slightly-late peer was treated as lost. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_SPIN_HARD_MS` | `20000` (r59, block 01) | **kill-switch / safety** | the internal all-reduce's **hard** spin cap in ms.  If a peer wait exceeds it the kernel treats the peer as lost: it sets the host poison flag and the next call falls back to the meta-backend butterfly.  This is the last-resort bound that keeps an unbounded spin from making the compute queue non-removable (the MES `REMOVE_QUEUE` hazard).  `0` = never poison (unbounded).  A value below the soft budget is clamped up to it. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_CE_PEER` | off (r165, block 01) | **opt-in** | let the copy-engine all-reduce `cudaDeviceEnablePeerAccess` on every device pair.  Measured to buy **nothing** and to cost the host submission path: pp2048 2212 vs 2206 and pp8192 2100 vs 2096 t/s staged-vs-direct on a 3x R9700 27B, while the dense decode is 42.2 vs 38.6.  Only for a topology where a direct peer copy is genuinely cheaper than the driver's staged one; the default chain never enables peer access at all. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_CE_SCRATCH_MIB` | `0` = unlimited (r47, block 12) | **opt-in tuning** | budget for the copy-engine (`GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=ce`) all-reduce scratch: each of the three per-rank staging buffers is `n_rank * ceil(ne/n_rank) * 2 B`.  Above it, CE returns `false` **before** any collective phase and the dispatcher falls back to the internal pipeline for that call; the fallback is sticky after the first failed grow, so an allocation failure also degrades cleanly instead of aborting.  `0` keeps the pre-r47 unbounded behaviour. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_CE_SINGLE` | **on** (r272, block 01) | **kill-switch** (`=0`) | the copy-engine all-reduce's **one-exchange form for 2 ranks**.  Off (default): each rank ships its whole bf16 partial to the peer once and one kernel forms `fp32(bf16(fp32(own) + fp32(peer)))` straight into the output; `=0` restores the two-phase reduce-scatter + all-gather.  The rounding is the same expression on the same two bf16 inputs (fp32 addition is commutative), so the output bits do not change -- a 5 GB log-prob dump of a 40x512 prefill is byte-identical across the two arms, and the 2-device `gate-prefill-logits.sh` arm reads mean KLD -0.000000 / 100.000 %.  Worth **+1.0-1.4 %** prefill on the delivery's 2-device x8 pair (27B Q8_0 `-sm tensor` pp512/2048/4096, `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=ce`, two interleaved rounds), decode flat.  It applies only at `n == 2`: 3+ ranks and the `q8ce` 8-bit wire take the two-phase form unchanged (the wire returns first).  `-lv 4` prints one `all-reduce: copy-engine 2-rank arm -- ONE exchange` line when it fires, so a census can name the form.  Record: `benchmarks/2026-11-12-allreduce-ce-single-exchange.md` |
| `GGML_CUDA_P2P` | off | tuning | enables peer access.  Note VMM allocations need explicit `cuMemSetAccess` for P2P (see `FOLLOWUP-compute-arena-chunking.md`). |
