| `GGML_CUDA_AR_SMALL_BLOCKS` | `4` (r46, block 12) | kill-switch / tuning | block count for a **small** internal all-reduce (decode/verify, `<= GGML_CUDA_AR_SMALL_BYTES`).  Each block signals and polls its own arrival slot, so fewer blocks shorten the call.  `GGML_CUDA_AR_SMALL_BLOCKS=8` restores the pre-r45 launch; the fused stage and larger messages always keep 8. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_SMALL_BYTES` | `65536` (r46, block 12) | tuning | the small-message threshold in bytes for the knob above. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_P2P` | off (r46, block 12) | **opt-in** | 2-device, not-fused P2P push all-reduce: each device writes its wire data and arrival token into the peer's fine-grained VRAM and reads locally, replacing the host ring and the three all-thread `__threadfence_system()` calls with a store-count drain plus one system-scope release store.  Falls back to the host ring if peer access / fine-grained allocation fails or there are not exactly 2 devices.  Bit-identical; MTP-neutral on the reporter's box, +2.6 % one-token decode. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_P2P_LITE` | `1` (r46, block 12) | kill-switch | `0` keeps the full `__threadfence_system()` fences on the P2P path (A/B only). |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_KER_EVENTS` | `0` = off | **opt-out / diagnostic** (r52, block 01) | the chunked kernel path no longer records `ev_pool[*][slot].ker` after every all-reduce (each record is a ROCm marker packet the GPU processes after every AR kernel); slot reuse on that path is already ordered on the GPUs by the arrival tokens, so `acquire_slot` syncs only when the slot's last user recorded the event.  `1` restores per-AR recording for A/B; pacing (`GGML_CUDA_AR_PACE=1`) also forces it. |
| `GGML_CUDA_AR_P2P_LARGE` | off (r52, block 01) | **opt-in** | 2-device copy-engine path: each device pulls the peer's contribution straight into `dev_tmp` with `hipMemcpyPeerAsync` (one PCIe crossing) instead of bouncing both halves through pinned host memory (two).  Same add kernel on the same bytes, so bit-identical; a 10.5 MB exchange measured 808 -> 437 us on the reporter's pair, +1.3 % on a 31.5k prefill.  Unset/`0` keeps the host-bounce path. |

---

## 7. Fusion A/B switches

Each of these disables exactly one optimization so it can be bisected.  **The feature is on by default;
the variable turns it off.**  Read the idiom column carefully — setting a presence-only switch to `0`
still disables the feature.

**The fusions are ON, and stay ON.**  The one known stand-down path — the cache-aware guard against
per-table slab eviction (`TODO.md` #50, evidence in `archive/work/slab-ring-region/FINDINGS-numerics.md` F3) — is a
pre-existing property of the arena, not a reason to turn them off.  Measured on the field config
(Qwen3.8-Flash-Next IQ4_NL, `-sm tensor -ncmoe 48`, 32 358-token prompt, `--spec-type none`, warm):

| | prefill t/s | decode t/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| fusions on (default) | 945-972 | 61.3-65.2 |
| `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1` | 845-890 | 57.5-65.3 |

so a full disable costs **~9-10 % prefill and ~6 % decode**, and the fusion-off prefill (845-890) is
*below* the unpatched stock reference (922) — it gives back the delivery's prefill win.

**To restore the full speed** after an A/B or a debug recipe, make sure none of the switches below is set:
unset `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION` and the rest of §7.2/§7.3, and unset (or `=1`) every `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_*` in
§7.1.  A clean environment is already the full-speed configuration — there is nothing to add, only
switches to remove.  (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1` is a **sledgehammer**: it also skips the
`graph_optimize` alloc-dependency pass and its shared-expert node reorder, so its cost is not the cost of
any single fusion.)

### 7.1 `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_*` — presence of the variable with a value, `0` disables

`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_ADD_RMS_Q8`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_CPY_BATCH`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GATE_BETA_VERIFY`,
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_BETA_SIGMOID`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_VERIFY`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_DECODE`,
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_GATHER`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_STORE`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_GATE`,
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GLU_Q8_1`,
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_HC_COMBINE_MIX`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_Q8_1_VERIFY`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_SHEXP_GATE_SIGMOID`,
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_SIGMOID_MUL_ADD`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_SWIGLU_MMQ`.
These use `getenv(...) == nullptr || atoi(...) != 0`, so unset = **on** and `0` = off.
(Git history note: these were originally *opt-in* `=1` switches; they are now default-on kill-switches.)

Four more from r102 (PR #200, block 10), all bit-identical with their own switch: `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GATE_BETA_BF16`
(qwen4exp's alpha/beta BF16 projections plus `softplus(alpha + dt) * a` in one `mul_mat_vec_f` launch),
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_NORM` (the q/k l2 norm pair folded into the direct conv kernel; falls back when a
SCALE output would overlap the conv's `x`/state/weight/output, which the allocator may do since all of them
are dead after the CONCAT), `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_CONT_GATE_Q8_1` (the attention gate's row-strided view read in
place by the gate -> Q8_1 kernel, so its CONT copies nothing) and `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_NORM_GATE` (qwen4exp's
GDN output `RMS_NORM * w` computed inside the gate kernel).  Worth +0.2-0.3 % on 27B MTP and +0.7 % on
Flash-Next 2 GPU `-sm tensor` + MTP, flat on 35B-A3B 1 GPU.  The GDN-norm pair is the only fusion in the set
whose skip and emit are two decisions (the norm node comes before the gate that computes it): while a norm is
pending no other fusion may start in between, so the two abort tripwires are unreachable.
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GATE_BETA_BF16` is **multi-sequence-clean since r108** (issue #204): it used to launch with the
gate chain's `MUL` as the `mmvf` dst, which gave the launch `n_seq_tokens` columns and `n_seqs` channels for a
flat one-channel `y`, so every sequence but the first got sequence 0's alpha/beta gates (invisible to
single-stream text/MTP gates; only multi-sequence serving saw it).  The switch still exists as an A/B
kill-switch, and `tests/test-fusion-gate-beta-multiseq.cpp` is the gate that covers it — no model needed, so
run it after touching any fusion that consumes a reshaped chain (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §49; **r202**: 33 cases —
`{BF16,1024}`, `{Q8_0,1024}`, `{Q8_0,5120}` x 11 `(T,S)` shapes — because a delivered GDN model's
`ssm_alpha/beta.weight` is Q8_0 at K=5120, and BF16-at-1024 alone hid the softplus rounding for ten
releases).  **`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GATE_BETA_VERIFY` gates only the VERIFY band**: the `ne[1] == 1` decode arm is
unconditional, so `=0` compares the fused kernel at one token against the chain from two tokens on (a valid
control -- it was an invalid one only while the fused kernel was not the chain, r201/r202), and the *default*
build is the fused kernel at every width (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §81).

The same idiom — and therefore also default-on despite the name — covers
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_IDX_RELU_SUM`, `GGML_CUDA_CONCAT_VERIFY`, (r46, block 15) `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_STATE_GATHER` and
(r183, block 10) `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GDN_CONV_SNAP`.

`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_NORM_PAIR` is value-gated (listed with §7.2): `0` keeps the paired GDN q/k norm.

`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_ADD_RMS_Q8=0` (r78) is the A/B switch for the **single-token** ADD + `rms_norm_q8_1`
fusion as well as the verify band: the r78 change dropped the old `ne[1] > 1` gate, so the FFN residual
ADD after an MoE/`ffn_out` producer now folds into the norm at one token too.  Measured on gfx1201:
dense 27B UD-Q4_K_XL, 3-GPU tensor, q8_0 KV, tg256 54.88 t/s default vs 53.91 with `=0` (the pre-r78
behaviour); the fused kernel's values are those of the unfused `k_bin_bcast` + `rms_norm_q8_1` pair, and
the same-seed text is byte-identical either way.  Not reached on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, whose residual add is
already consumed by the fused shared-expert output epilogue.

**Arch scope of the three verify-band switches (r95).**  `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_Q8_1_VERIFY`,
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_VERIFY` and `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_CPY_BATCH` gate the **verify band** (2..8-token
Q8_1 pre-quantize fusions, the direct GDN conv for 2..255-token batches, and the batched run of up to 16
same-layout f32 `GGML_OP_CPY` nodes) on **RDNA4 and RDNA3_0 (gfx1100-1102)**.  They were RDNA4-only
until r95 (PR #192 + PR #193); gfx1101-gfx1102 are inside the arch predicate but are **not explicitly
validated**, and **RDNA3_5 is excluded** (`GGML_CUDA_CC_IS_RDNA3_0` is `0x1100 <= cc < 0x1150`).
Every one of them is a default-on kill-switch: `=0` restores the pre-r95 RDNA3_0 path.  The three are
bit-identical to the paths they replace (gfx1100 `W = 1..8` width-probe maxdiff 0, greedy text identical
for `none` and `draft-mtp n3`, prefill-logit KLD 0.000000 / 100.000 %); the batched CPY and the GDN conv both
run on every device count (**r186 removed the GDN/PLE conv's single-device restriction** — see the r183 note
below), and RDNA3_0 multi-device (`-sm tensor` / `-sm layer`) is a recorded open gap — the batched CPY fires
~450 times (`-sm layer`) / ~1350 times (`-sm tensor`) per 60-token MTP run on 3x gfx1201, so that arm is
exercised on RDNA4 but not measured on RDNA3_0.

**r183 (block 10) extends that same gate to one token.**  `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_DECODE` widens
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_VERIFY`'s band to `T = 1` (only where the q/k norm pair folds too and the decode-only
`ssm_prescan` would not take the conv first), and `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GDN_CONV_SNAP` /
`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_GATHER` ride the direct conv on whatever width it runs (prefill, the verify band and,
with `..._DECODE`, decode).  **r186:** all of them (and the direct conv itself) now run on **every device
count**.  The single-device restriction existed because the 3x R9700 `-sm tensor` split diverged from the
reference (2026-09-23, never root-caused); that no longer reproduces, so `ggml_cuda_get_device_count()` is no
longer consulted and `GGML_CUDA_CONV_FUSION_MULTI` — the old force-on knob — is **retired** (forcing the fusion
on is now the default, so the knob has nothing left to do and is not read).  `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_CONV_FUSION`
(§7.3) is the off switch and the A/B arm for every multi-device measurement.

### 7.2 `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_*` — value-gated (`=1` disables, `0` keeps it on)

`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSED_ADDMUL`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GDN_CPY`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_HC_FUSION`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_L2_NORM_PAIR`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MOE_DOWN_FOLD`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_NORM_PAIR` (r81, the paired GDN q/k norm below),
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MOE_MMQ_FUSION`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MWR`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_NORM_Q8_1`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_NORM_ROWS`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_SETROWS`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SCALE_UNARY`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SHEXP_DOWN_GATE`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SNAKE`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SSM_CONV_IN`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SSM_PRESCAN`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_WEIGHTED_DOWN`.

`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SHEXP_DOWN_GATE=1` is the A/B switch for the byte-identical fused shared-expert band
(`1 <= nt <= 8`).

`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_NORM_PAIR=1` (r81) restores the two separate `rms_norm_f32<256, false, false, true>`
launches for the GDN q and k l2 norms that `build_gdn_l2_norm` builds; the default runs both in one
`rms_norm_scale_pair_f32<256>` launch (blocks `[0, nrows)` take q, `[nrows, 2*nrows)` take k, same
per-row body, so each output is bit-identical).  It is taken only when both inputs are F32 views of the
same `view_src` with equal shape, strides, eps and scale and only no-op nodes sit between the pairs.
Measured on gfx1201 35B-A3B Q4_K_M 2-GPU tensor: tg256 114.4 -> 115.7 t/s (+1.2 %, 3/3 rounds) and the
kernel count for the norms halves (7500 -> 3750 per 120-token decode).

### 7.3 `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_*` — **presence-only** (any value, including `0`, disables)

`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_CONV_FUSION`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MMID_512`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MMQ_ROUTED`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MMVQ_DENSE_BAND`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MMVQ_MOE_BAND`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_RMS_NORM_MUL_ROPE`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_SET_ROWS`,
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_TOPK_MOE_FUSION`,
(r78, block 10) `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_FWHT` and `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FWHT_Q8_1`,
and (r81, block 10) `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_MROPE_FUSE` and `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_FWHT_SET_ROWS`.

`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_MROPE_FUSE` (r81) puts `GGML_ROPE_TYPE_MROPE` / `IMROPE` back on the unfused
`rms_norm_f32` + `rope_multi` + `fwht_cuda` chain (the r78 matcher, NORMAL/NEOX only);
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_FWHT_SET_ROWS` (r81) stops the fused rope kernel from also writing the q8_0 K/V
cache rows, so the two `SET_ROWS` launches come back.  **Since r101 it is live on iSWA archs too**: the
iSWA KV builder now expands `q, v, k` (instead of `q, k, v`), so the V block no longer sits between the K
rotation and the K cache write and the matcher admits the fusion — gemma4 with `-ctk/-ctv q8_0` folds
1152 `k_set_rows_q8_0_group` launches to 192 and runs `rms_norm_mul_rope_f32<...,kv=true>` 480 times in
a 8-token run.  It stays inert where the V-row write cannot apply (gemma4's 512-wide full-attention
layers: `fwht_n` is `{64,128,256}` and the fused V-row write assumes `ncols <= 256`).

The two r78 switches split PR #172's two fusions: `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_FWHT` keeps the KV rotation's
FWHT as its own `fwht_cuda` launch (rope only, no shared-memory staging row) and
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FWHT_Q8_1` stops the attention-output FWHT from also writing the output projection's
Q8_1 activation (the projection then runs its own `quantize_q8_1`).  Both fusions are bit-identical to
the unfused path; measured together on gfx1201 Gemma4 12B QAT UD-Q4_K_XL (1 GPU, layer, q8_0 KV):
tg256 61.05 t/s default, 60.41 with `DISABLE_ROPE_FWHT=1`, 60.80 with `DISABLE_FWHT_Q8_1=1`, 60.15 on
the r77 build.  They only fire for NORMAL/NEOX rope with the `RMS_NORM, MUL, ROPE` chain, so on r78 they were
inert on `rope_multi` models (all Qwen3.x here) -- a trace showed `rope_multi` + `fwht_cuda` there.
**r81 closes that gap:** `GGML_ROPE_TYPE_MROPE` / `IMROPE` are admitted too, so the same two fusions
(and the K/V cache write fold) now run on every Qwen3.x model.  Measured on gfx1201 35B-A3B Q4_K_M,
1 GPU, 120-token decode: `rope_multi` 2500 -> 40, `fwht_cuda<256>` 2500 -> 40,
`rms_norm_f32<256,true,...>` 6120 -> 3660, and 2460 `rms_norm_mul_rope_f32<256,...,256>` launches
replace them; with the cache-write fold, `k_set_rows_q8_0_group` 2500 -> 20 (all 35B-A3B 2-GPU tensor,
see `../WORKLOG.md` 261017.008).

### 7.4 Other kernel tuning

> **The r187 quantized-KV decode fusions cover `q8_0`, `q4_0` (r199), `q5_0` and `iq4_nl` (r205).**
> `GGML_CUDA_QK_ROTATE_HADAMARD`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_V_FWHT`, `GGML_CUDA_FA_COMBINE_FWHT`,
> `GGML_CUDA_QK_PAIR_FWHT` and `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_ROPE_FWHT` all key on the cache `SET_ROWS` being
> `GGML_TYPE_Q8_0`, or — on a wave32 device, which is every RDNA target — `GGML_TYPE_Q4_0` (r199),
> `GGML_TYPE_Q5_0` or `GGML_TYPE_IQ4_NL` (r205), because those are the quantizers the fused kernels
> reproduce.  `-ctk/-ctv q5_1`, `q4_1`, `f16`, `bf16`, `f32` … keep their separate launches: `q5_1`/`q4_1` have
> no group form yet, and a **non-quantized** cache is not a scope gap at all — it carries no Hadamard rotation
> (`attn_rot_k/attn_rot_v` require `ggml_is_quantized(type_k/type_v)`), so the folds have no pattern to match.

| variable | default | notes |
|---|---|---|
| `GGML_CUDA_KV_FOLD_Q4_0` | on (r199, block 10) | kill-switch | whether the fused rope + FWHT kernel also writes a **q4_0** K/V cache row (and a q4_0 V row), i.e. whether `ggml_cuda_kv_fold_type_ok` admits `GGML_TYPE_Q4_0` beside `Q8_0`.  `0` restores the pre-r199 arm (a q4_0 cache keeps its separate `k_set_rows_quant` launches, its own `fwht_cuda<64>` and the single Q / K norm+rope launches).  **Wave32 devices only** (`ggml_cuda_quantize_q4_0_group` ballots and shuffles over one 32-lane wave), which is every RDNA target; a wave64 device keeps the separate launches whatever this says.  Bit-identical: `--kv q4_0 --ub 1` prefill-logit A/B `-0.000000 / 100.000 %`, the width probe matches at `P=3584`/`P>=4096`, greedy text identical on gfx1201/gfx1100/gfx1151.  Worth **+10.7 % tg128 / +10.2 % @4096** on gfx1201 (30B-A3B `q4_0` KV), **+12.0 % / +11.2 %** on gfx1100 and **+2.5 % / +2.6 %** on gfx1151, with f16 and q8_0 KV unchanged; `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §74, `benchmarks/2026-11-01-kv-fold-q4_0-promotion.md`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_KV_FOLD_Q5_0`, `GGML_CUDA_KV_FOLD_IQ4_NL` | on (r205, block 10) | kill-switch | whether the fused rope + FWHT kernel also writes a **q5_0** / **iq4_nl** K/V cache row, i.e. whether `ggml_cuda_kv_fold_type_ok` admits `GGML_TYPE_Q5_0` / `GGML_TYPE_IQ4_NL` beside `Q8_0` (and the r199 `Q4_0`).  `0` restores the pre-r205 arm (a `k_set_rows_quant` launch per row, V's own `fwht_cuda<64>`, the single Q / K norm+rope launches).  **Wave32 devices only** (both group quantizers ballot and shuffle over one 32-lane wave); a wave64 device keeps the separate launches whatever this says.  Bit-identical: `--kv q5_0`/`--kv iq4_nl` `--ub 1` prefill-logit A/B **0.000000/-0.000000 / 100.000 %**, the gpt-oss default-ubatch rope-only arm `0.000000 / 99.990 %` and `/ 100.000 %`, the width probe identical at `P=3584`/`P>=4096`, greedy text identical on 25 arms and three arches (r204 == patched == switch-off).  Worth (**q5_0** / **iq4_nl**) **+10.2 %/+24.8 % tg128** and **+8.8 %/+22.1 % @4096** on gfx1201 (30B-A3B), **+12.2 %/+23.4 %** and **+11.1 %/+22.8 %** on gfx1100, flat/+0.5 % on gfx1151, with f16, q8_0 and q4_0 KV unchanged; census 8547 -> 6915 launches (gpt-oss 9831 -> 9063).  `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §74, `benchmarks/2026-11-01-kv-fold-q5_0-iq4_nl-promotion.md`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_J_MAX` | tuning | MMQ `J` tile width cap. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_RDNA3_SMALL_M` | `8192`, RDNA3_0 only — **kill-switch / tuning** (block 08) | row-count `M` band for the dense mmvq *weight* kernel's narrow (1-warp) block.  `Q4_0/Q4_1/Q5_0/Q5_1` always take it; `Q8_0` takes it for `2048 <= M <= this`; `Q6_K`/`IQ4_NL` never do.  `M` is the weight's row count, i.e. a tensor property, so all widths `W = 1..8` of a tensor take the same branch — and the three fusion kernels that reproduce a dense weight launch (`ggml_cuda_op_ssm_gate_beta`, `_ssm_conv_l2_gatebeta`, `_shexp_down_gate`) take the same value, which is the width-purity requirement (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §19).  `0` restores the pre-2026-10-19 wide-block table (byte-identical to r91).  Measured on gfx1100: 35B-A3B MTP n3 **+10.7 %** (prose) / **+6.3 %** (code), gemma-26B `tg128` **+5.0 %**, 35B-A3B `tg128` @ d16384 **+1.7-2.0 %**, gemma-12B +0.4 %, 27B neutral; `W = 1..8` probe PASS on all four models and all eight KV types.  Record: `archive/work/nwarps/`. |
| `GGML_Q6_COMPACT_J` | tuning | Q6 compact-`J` variant. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ENABLE_RDNA3_5_SINGLE_TOKEN_FUSIONS` | off (**opt-in**, RDNA3.5 only) | the qwen4exp weighted-down single-token chain. |
| `GGML_PAIR_OFF` / `GGML_PAIR_DENSE_OFF` / `GGML_PAIR_ROUTED_OFF` / `GGML_PAIR_2X` | off (**A/B / bisect**) | paired-kernel variants (block 10).  `GGML_PAIR_OFF` disables the gate+up pair fusion on both arms; `GGML_PAIR_DENSE_OFF` only the dense (`MUL_MAT`) half and `GGML_PAIR_ROUTED_OFF` (r171) only the routed (`MUL_MAT_ID`) half, which is the arm to compare against the mmq/mmvq triple it falls back from.  The pair is the LAST owner of a gate+up pair — MMB's fused GLU and the mmq/mmvq triples are tried first — and it stands down on `ggml_cuda_mmb_supported_mm()`/`_supported_mmid()`, so on RDNA4 (`mmb_cfg().routed = 0`) the routed arm always fuses and these switches are inert.  Pair and triple are bit-identical (same dot-product order, same `J` tile); `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §69, `benchmarks/2026-10-29-mmb-pair-ownership.md`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_SPLICE_GATHER`, `GGML_CUDA_SCALE_UNARY`, `GGML_CUDA_HC_MIX_BAND`, `GGML_CUDA_HC_MIX_PREQ`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_HC_COMB`, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_HC_MIX`, `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_MIX`, `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_COMBINE`, `LLAMA_FUSED_DSV4_HC_PRE/POST`, `LLAMA_HC_MIX_BF16`, `GGML_CUDA_LIGHTNING_INDEXER4_GFX1100/GFX1201`, `GGML_LF_FAST_TOPK`, `LLAMA_INDEXER_NOBLOCK`, `LLAMA_INDEXER_NOGROUP` | off / default-on | model-specific fused chains. A/B only.  One of them has a **split-mode-dependent default** (r97): the deepseek4/glm5-next `DSV4_HC_POST` arm is the fused op whenever a **Meta (tensor-split) device** is in play, and the primitive `hc_combine_norm` chain otherwise — the chain is faster on one device but is not correct across a tensor split (NMSE 1.74e-01 vs the CPU reference through the chain, 6.5e-06 through the op; `patches/README.md` block 10).  `LLAMA_FUSED_DSV4_HC_POST=0/1` still forces either arm.  **The qwen4exp pair `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_MIX` / `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_COMBINE` are two INDEPENDENT arms, and every one of the four combinations must produce the same text** — it is the oracle `scripts/gate-hc-fusions.sh` checks.  Through r147 two of the four were wrong (the fused mixer published a raw inject logit while the fused combine divided it by `hc` again, issue #228: the collapse on any hc-down quant that is not Q8_0/BF16, and an empty generation on a BF16-hc model with the combine switched off), so a recorded result from one of those arms is not comparable to a recorded result from another.  **The three CUDA-side knobs (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_HC_FUSION`, `_HC_MIX`, `_HC_COMB`) disable MATCHERS over the unfused chain, so on a default qwen4exp build they are inert**: the fused ops replace exactly the patterns the matchers look for (measured on Flash-Next GSQ-RCO IQ3_XXS, gfx1151: all three produce the same greedy text as the default).  An HC-fusion A/B is only meaningful with `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_MIX=0` / `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_COMBINE=0` on the arm being compared, which is also why the issue #228 reporter's `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_HC_COMB=1` arm could not have changed anything. |
| `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_VERIFY_GRAPHS`, `GGML_HIP_GRAPH_FORCE_UPDATE` | off | HIP graph-capture controls. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_OPT` | off (**opt-in**, block 10) | the concurrent fork/join scheduler: a decode graph whose `attn_norm` forks 3 ways (Q/K/V) runs the three branches on their own streams and joins before the attention op.  **Diagnostic/experimental, not a shipped default.**  Since r251 the stream-number layout is a contract: `0` compute, `1..4` copy streams (`copy_stream()` = 1 -- every staging fill, `stage_gather` and pool fill; 2..4 reserved for the parked multi-copy-stream work), `5..7` these branch streams, `static_assert`ed against `GGML_CUDA_MAX_STREAMS`, with a `-lv 4` census that prints both ranges (`concurrent graph opt: fork attn_norm-NN, 3 branches -> branch streams 5..7; copy streams 1..4 are reserved`).  Gate: `scripts/gate-graph-opt-streams.sh` (text identity vs unset + the disjoint-range census; it **needs `--fit off`**, because with the default `--fit on` `common_fit_params` consumes the graph and no concurrent event is created, and it fails rather than passing vacuously on an empty census).  Measured effect is inside noise (+0.4 % decode / -0.1 % MTP verify on 35B-A3B `-ncmoe 40`) -- enable it to experiment, not for a win. |
| `GGML_HC_UP_V2` | on | kill-switch | `0` restores the row-at-a-time BF16 `hc_mix` up/collapse kernel (r27); v2 is bit-identical and 4-21 % faster on the op. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_KEY_DATA` | on (r115, block 09) | kill-switch | `0` restores the old HIP graph-cache key (the first node's `ggml_tensor` *object* address).  The key is now the first node's **`data`** pointer, which is what a captured graph bakes in and is stable across a Meta (tensor-split) rebuild; with the object address the cache never warmed under `-sm tensor` (3954 keys / 1206 captures / 484 ms of `hipGraphInstantiate` per 300-token decode at 3 devices), which was the 3-GPU stutter and the r115 **+41 %** decode (A/B and bisect only). |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_KEY_WEIGHT` | on (r149, block 09) | kill-switch | also key the graph cache on the subgraph's **weight identity** (the data of the first device-resident weight it reads, falling back to `nodes[0]->src[0]` when the experts are host-resident) plus a structural signature (node count, the first node's geometry/first source, the last node's data, the first and last node names).  A first-node-data key alone cannot tell the **per-layer subgraphs of one split graph apart**: they all begin on the same activation buffer, so one cache entry served every layer and each layer was compared against the *previous* layer's properties — a source `data` pointer that had "moved" by one layer's worth of weights — and the graph was then run directly (or recaptured) on almost every call.  Measured 2x R9700 x8, Flash-Next IQ4_NL `-sm tensor -ncmoe 48`, 2000-token decode: `direct=549499 replay=31696 capture=149 properties_changed=452439` -> `direct=3882 replay=574826 capture=2636 properties_changed=28`, **37.7 -> 42.8 t/s (+13.5 %)**; +12.0 % on the same shape at 3 devices, +1.4-2.0 % with a `draft-mtp` draft, and flat on 1 GPU, on 2/3-device dense `-sm tensor`, on `-sm layer` and on `llama-bench tg128`.  **The r149-era "known cost" is gone (r165):** a 3-device `-sm tensor` qwen35 dense plain decode used to lose 6-8 % (27B Q8_0 `tg128` 41.7 -> 38.6) because the comm layer enabled peer access; the cause was isolated to `hipDeviceEnablePeerAccess` itself and the default no longer enables it, so that arm is now **42.2** with replay working (same build, `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=hybrid` keeps the old 38.6 arm for an A/B).  `benchmarks/2026-10-28-allreduce-peer-access.md`.  `=0` restores the (first node, token count) key: it also restores the never-replayed behaviour, so use it as the A/B and bisect arm, not as a fix. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_MEM_GEN` | on | kill-switch | `0` disables the per-device graph memory generation (r25): a graph that captured pool temporary / FA-staging / H2D-ring memory freed since is otherwise recaptured. |
| `GGML_CUDA_Q8_1_ARENA_FREE_OLD` | off | kill-switch (**opt-in**) | `1` restores the immediate `cudaFree` of a grown-out Q8_1 input arena (the r24 behaviour, A/B only): captured decode/verify graphs keep pointers into it, so this reintroduces the `quantize_q8_1` page fault. |
| `GGML_CUDA_BF16_SRC1_CACHE` | on | kill-switch | r36.  `0` disables the per-graph reuse of the BF16 copy of a shared F32 src1 across the qwen4exp hc-mixer cuBLAS BF16 GEMMs (prefill only, `>= 64` columns); bit-identical either way. |
| `GGML_CUDA_HC_POST_RMS` | **on** (r242, block 11) | kill-switch | fuses the qwen4exp prefill chain `DSV4_HC_POST -> RMS_NORM -> MUL(gamma)` into one launch (`dsv4_hc_post_rms_f32`, `norm.cu`): the kernel computes the hc_post sum with `dsv4_hc_post_f32`'s own two statements, stores it (it is the next layer's residual), and then does `rms_norm_f32`'s scalar-path reduction, scale and gamma product, so the result is bit-identical to the three ops.  `0` restores the chain.  Only reachable under a **Meta (tensor-split) device** (`DSV4_HC_POST` is emitted only there, `cparams.fused_dsv4_hc_post`), and only for a prefill-width graph -- the `nt <= 16` band runs the fused `HC_MIX` op and never emits the chain, so decode is untouched.  Stands down under `GGML_CUDA_RMS_NORM_VEC=1` (a float4 sum order) and on an aliasing window.  Measured 2 R9700 `-sm tensor`, Flash-Next GSQ-RCO IQ3_S: prefill **2330 -> 2470 t/s (+6.0 %)** on a 31.5k prompt and **2421 -> 2617 (+8.1 %)** on a 101k one, **+6.8 %** on the delivery-style arm (attendance pool on, `-ub 4096`, `-c 32768`); greedy text identical in every arm and on the MTP decode arm; prefill logits **-0.000000 / 100.000 %**; 1 device neutral (no Meta device, so no chain to fuse).  Gate: `scripts/gate-prefill-logits.sh --sm tensor --device 0,2` + `scripts/gate-hc-fusions.sh`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_HC_POST_RMS_DEBUG` | off | **diagnostic** (r242, block 11) | `1` logs one `hc-post-rms: i=N fused=1 ...` or `hc-post-rms: i=N declined=<reason> ...` line per candidate node at INFO (so `-lv 4`), which is how a gate proves the fusion is live instead of assuming it (`INVARIANTS.md` r225/r238: a silent fusion makes an arm vacuous).  Never changes behaviour. |
| `GGML_CUDA_BF16_PRODUCER` | **on** (r242, block 11) | kill-switch / diagnostic | lets the qwen4exp prefill producers of the cuBLAS/rocBLAS BF16 GEMM activations write the RNE BF16 copy of their output in the same pass and leave it in `bf16_src1_cache`, so the GEMM skips its own F32 -> BF16 conversion: the fused `hc_post_rms`'s `xn` (feeds `hc_*_down`/`hc_*_inject`) and `dsv4_hc_pre`'s mixed stream (router / indexer / beta-alpha).  The F32 output is always written too (except on the MMB BF16-only mark, where it is not needed).  `0` disables both producers; `2` is a **debug** mode that re-converts every cache hit and compares it element by element (it allocates from the device pool and synchronises the stream per hit, and skips hits inside a graph capture -- never use it for timing).  The gates match the GEMM lookup's (`>= 64` columns, contiguous root, `GGML_CUDA_BF16_SRC1_CACHE` on), so decode is untouched.  Worth **+2.2 %** prefill on top of the fusion (2 R9700, 31.5k prompt); `=2` reported **0 mismatching elements** over 3501+ hits on both producers. |
| `LLAMA_HC_PIN_BLOCK_OUT` | off | kill-switch (**opt-in**) | `1` restores pinning every qwen4exp layer's `block_out` as a prefill graph output (r24 behaviour; costs ~1.9 GiB of the `-ub 2048` compute buffer). |
| `LLAMA_HC_MIX_PLANAR` | on | kill-switch | `0` restores the interleaved HC_MIX output (r24) instead of the planar layout (r25; BF16 CUDA path only). |
| `LLAMA_CONV_TAIL_CONT` | off | kill-switch (**opt-in**) | `1` restores the `cont` + `cpy` conv-state tail copy instead of the direct strided 2D memcpy (r25). |
| `LLAMA_SHARE_SLOT_REV` | **on** | kill-switch (r241, block 03) | the conv-state store's rollback index (`arange` + `scale_bias` + `cast`, `K = n_rs_seq + 1 <= 8` int32 values) is built **once per graph per device** and each conv layer takes its `{Kw-1, ..., 0}` tail as a view; `0` restores upstream's per-layer chain, i.e. the 3 nodes and 3 launches per conv layer per graph that issue #291 measured. Built per layer it is 144 nodes of the dense-27B MTP verify graph (1268) and is re-created on every speculative round; sharing it makes that graph **1127** nodes. Bit-identical (same index values, same `set_rows`). Measured 1 R9700, `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1`, `-np 2 --kv-unified`, MTP n3: **58.37 -> 59.01 t/s** decode at 10.5k depth (+1.03 %) and 45.93 -> 47.76 on a decode-dominated short prompt (+4.0 %), text unchanged. The cost it removes is per **launch**, so it is worth most where a launch is expensive (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1`, Windows). Gate: `scripts/gate-slot-rev-share.sh`. |
| `GGML_FORCE_NO_INTEGRATED` | off | ignore integrated GPUs when picking devices. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ASYNC_INPUT` | **on** (r90) | **kill-switch** | async graph-input uploads: a `set_tensor` of `<= 1 MiB` into a COMPUTE buffer stages through a per-device 16 MiB pinned ring with a done-event ordering, so the host does not block on every copy; any setup failure falls back to the synchronous path.  **`=0` disables it.**  `=0` also restores the block-10 `< r90` behaviour of the two `set_tensor_*_async` paths (a direct copy from the caller's host pointer).  The per-copy `cudaStreamSynchronize` is invisible on a fast host and measured ~1.4 % of decode on a slow one; default-on is worth **+5.8 %** (35B-A3B 2-GPU tensor) and **+3.6 %** (27B MTP 2-GPU) decode, flat on one card.  **History:** r69 reverted the r68 default-on because a run was not start-to-start deterministic when the consuming graph was captured.  **r90 fixes that**: the ring was never the defect (uploads verify byte-correct at the graph's first read, no inter-graph overlap, HIP's wait/record ordering correct); `ggml_backend_cuda_set_tensor_async` / `_set_tensor_2d_async` copied **directly from the caller's live pinned host pointer**, and the scheduler uses that path for a CPU split's output, which the next ubatch rewrites -- a rare single-graph corruption that propagated.  Both paths now snapshot through the pinned ring (unconditionally, i.e. also with `=0`, where the old direct copy is the fallback), and the ring's wait/done guard is unconditional so the snapshots stay ordered.  Record: `archive/work/async-input-graph-ordering/` (TODO #73, closed).  **r235:** the ring's `1 MiB` snapshot limit is now `GGML_BACKEND_ASYNC_SNAPSHOT_MAX` in `ggml-backend-impl.h`, and the scheduler gates its `set_tensor_async` path on the same value: above it a **non-weight** host split input is copied synchronously (see `GGML_SCHED_ASYNC_LARGE_HOST_INPUT` in §2), so an upload the ring does **not** snapshot can no longer read a buffer its owner rewrites. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ASYNC_INPUT_BATCH` | **on** (inside `ASYNC_INPUT=1`) | **kill-switch** (r89, block 10) | PR #182 `0001`: a graph's staged inputs are queued per device and copied by **one kernel** on the compute stream when the next graph starts, instead of one `cudaMemcpyAsync` + one event record per input.  Any other buffer/backend access (get/set/memset/clear/cpy, the 2-D and async forms, `synchronize`, `free_buffer`) first drains the queue through the old per-copy path and orders `cudaStreamPerThread` after the last batch kernel; an overlapping upload, a full queue (64) or a ring wrap also waits.  `cudaHostGetDevicePointer` failure falls back to per-copy.  Byte-exact.  `=0` restores the per-copy uploads.  Worth **+4.6 %** over per-copy on 35B-A3B 2-GPU tensor and +1.4 % on 27B MTP 2-GPU (decode); it also *widened* the r69 nondeterminism window up to r89 (which is what made that defect cheap to isolate); with r90's split-input snapshot fix that window is closed, so batching is a pure win (`archive/work/async-input-graph-ordering/`). |
| `GGML_CUDA_ASYNC_INPUT_LAZY_DONE` | = the `..._BATCH` default | **kill-switch** (r89, block 10) | PR #182 `0002`: `done_ev` is recorded by the **next upload** on that graph's stream instead of after every `graph_compute` (a decode step runs hundreds of graphs per device, each record a marker packet).  Only uploads read the event, and recording it later on the same stream covers at least the same work; a context destructor flushes a still-pending event before its streams are destroyed.  `=0` / `=1` override.  With per-copy uploads alone it measured ~1 % slower on one GPU, hence the coupling. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ASYNC_INPUT_DEBUG` | unset | diagnostic (r105, block 10) | prints, on stderr and for the first 16 events only, every ordering **origin** the ring creates (`AIN origin: dev=… key=… thread=… origins=…`) and the first 16 waits (`AIN wait: … origins=… issued=… ev=… flush=…`).  Diagnostic only — it never changes what is computed.  Its purpose is to make the origin count a **measurement**: the ring's ordering state is per host thread (keyed on `std::this_thread::get_id()`, issue #201), so two contexts decoding on one device from two threads must log `origins=2`, and a wait must never consume another origin's ordering state.  The two-thread reproducer that reads it is `archive/work/async-input-per-origin/repro/`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_Q8_1_CACHE_EXACT` | unset (canonical match) | **kill-switch** (r69, block 10) | set to any value to restore the pre-r69 exact-shape Q8_1 cache key.  By default the cache matches the canonical row layout (size-1 dims dropped, contiguous dims merged), so a `MUL_MAT_ID` view `[K, 1, n]` and a `MUL_MAT` view `[K, n]` of one activation share one quantization (PR #163, output bit-identical).  **Not a knob on the identity itself (r132):** the key walk stops at the first pointer change, so an offset view never shares the root's entry, and `q8_1_src_range_ok()` aborts (with the layout logged) if a source's read extent leaves its root buffer.  Both are unconditional — a stale source pointer is a defect, not a mode (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §55). |
| `LLAMA_SHEXP_FIRST` | on | **kill-switch** (r69, block 10; the qwen4exp node order it moves is block 11) | `0` restores the qwen4exp node order with the shared expert expanded after the MoE; the default expands it first so it shares the MoE segment's Q8_1 (PR #163, output bit-identical).  On a model whose shared-expert down+gate fusion fires (`ffn_down_shexp` Q8_0 + `ffn_gate_inp_shexp` F32), `=0` keeps that fusion. |
| `GGML_HC_MIX_Q8` | on | **kill-switch** (r69, block 11) | `0` restores the pre-r69 `hc_mix_bf16_up_collapse2` path and row mapping; the default writes the mixed rows' Q8_1 straight into the quantize cache for the mmvq consumers (PR #163, output bit-identical). |
| `GGML_CUDA_BOUNCE_MIN` | `1` = every size | **kill-switch / tuning** (r56, block 09) | `buffer_get_tensor`, `buffer_set_tensor` (not weight buffers) and `buffer_get_tensor_2d` copy pageable host data through a reused 64 MiB pinned chunk per device.  A pageable device<->host copy is slow on ROCm at any size (~25 us 1-D / ~57 us 2-D on gfx1201), and the server's context checkpoint + `--cache-ram` slot saves make thousands of them.  A pinned host pointer keeps the direct copy; a failed chunk allocation leaves the copy direct and is not retried.  `0` (or unparsable) disables the bounce; a byte count is the minimum copy size that bounces.  HIP/CUDA only — the pointer-attribute helper is not defined for MUSA, which stays direct. |
| `GGML_CUDA_STAGE_PIN` | on (r116, block 08) | **kill-switch** | `0` restores the per-tensor `cudaMallocHost`+`cudaFreeHost` of `ggml_backend_cuda_buffer_set_tensor_2d`'s pinned staging buffer (the pre-r116 behaviour).  A `-sm tensor` model load uploads each tensor through that gather buffer, so the old code allocated and freed ~8-32 MiB of pinned memory **per tensor** — 678 pairs, measured as 2.2 s of `hipHostMalloc` + 1.1 s of `hipHostFree` of a ~10.7 s load (27B Q8_0, 3 devices).  The buffer is now grow-only per device, retained for the process (bounded at **64 MiB**; a larger tensor falls back to the one-shot allocation), which takes the same load to **9.5 s** and 5 allocations.  Bytes, stream and sync are unchanged, so text is identical; `0` is for an A/B or a bisect. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MOE_RPB_SHORT` | unset = on (RDNA4) | **kill-switch / tuning** (r52, block 10) | rows per block for a short-K (`< 8` K-blocks per row) `MUL_MAT_ID` expert GEMM: RDNA4 uses **8** instead of `ceil(8 / K-blocks)` (the Flash-Next q2_0 down split under `-sm tensor` measured 43.0 -> 29.8 us per 4-token verify call).  `0` restores the old rule; `2`/`4`/`8` force a value on any architecture; other values are ignored. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_COMBINE_Q8` | **on** (r175, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the flash-attention combine kernel also writes its output's Q8_1 blocks into the quantize cache, so the following quantized matmul (the attention output projection) skips its `quantize_q8_1` launch.  Taken only for a contiguous F32 combine output whose row length is a multiple of `MATRIX_ROW_PADDING` (so its rows are unpadded) and whose `DV` is a multiple of `QK8_1`; the math is `quantize_q8_1`'s exactly, and the write is indexed by the combine's own `(sequence, head, token)` unrolling, so every width agrees.  `=0` restores the separate quantize launch.  Worth ~2.5 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode (gfx1100). |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_COMBINE_FWHT` | **on** (r187, block 10) | **kill-switch** | at a **1-token** `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` whose combine kernel runs (`parallel_blocks > 1`) and whose output feeds the **quantized-KV output rotation** (the 64-wide Hadamard) and then an mmvq projection, the rotation is folded into the combine: each output row is staged in shared memory, rotated with the same in-warp `ggml_cuda_fwht_warp<64, 32>` and scale, stored, and its Q8_1 published (`fwht_cuda`'s own reduction/rounding), so the FWHT launch goes away and the projection finds the cache entry (exactly as `GGML_CUDA_FA_COMBINE_Q8` does).  If the combine kernel does not run, the rotation runs as before and **no** cache entry is created; the plain `flash_attn_combine_results<D>` instantiation is unchanged.  `=0` off.  Worth ~3.4 % of q8_0-KV decode (Qwen3-30B-A3B, gfx1100). |
| `GGML_CUDA_MOE_GLU_Q8` | **on** (r175, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the 1-token fused MoE gate/up + GLU kernel publishes the down projection's Q8_1 input: each block adds its rows to a self-resetting `(expert slot, 32-row group)` counter and the block that completes a group quantizes it with one warp (same 32-lane reductions and rounding as `quantize_q8_1`), and the completing block of a row's **last** group also zeroes that row's padding blocks up to `MATRIX_ROW_PADDING`.  Needs a 32-lane wave, `n_ff % QK8_1 == 0`, `dst->ne[2] == dst->ne[3] == 1` (one token) and `n_used * n_ff/QK8_1 <= 4096` counters; the down `MUL_MAT_ID` then finds the entry and skips its quantize.  `=0` off.  Worth ~2.1 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MOE_SHARED_GLU_Q8` | **on** (r188, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the 1-token fused MoE gate/up + GLU kernel's **shared-expert lane** (the last `blockIdx.y`, writing `shared_dst`) also publishes its GLU output as Q8_1, into its own quantize-cache entry keyed on the **shared GLU node** — the key the shared down `MUL_MAT` looks up — so that down skips its `quantize_q8_1` launch.  Its group counters sit in the lane **after** the routed ones (`lane = gridDim.y - 1 = nchannels_dst`), the counter budget check is `(n_used + 1) * n_ff/QK8_1 <= 4096`, and the write is `quantize_q8_1`'s 32-lane reduction/rounding plus the same zeroed padding blocks, so the result is bit-identical.  It is **nested inside** `GGML_CUDA_MOE_GLU_Q8`'s arming block, so that switch (or any condition that stands the routed publish down: not a 32-lane wave, not one token, `n_ff % QK8_1 != 0`, a non-contiguous `dst`, the counter budget) disables both; `=0` disables only this.  **Reach:** it only wins where the shared down is its own node — a `ffn_down_shexp` that is **Q8_0** is already fused away by `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SHEXP_DOWN_GATE`'s epilogue (every Unsloth UD quant), where this is inert (measured: 0 launches saved).  Worth one launch per layer: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B launches/token **748 -> 708** and **+1.1 %** tg128 / **+0.8 %** @4096 on gfx1201, **+2.6 % / +2.3 %** on gfx1100 (LMStudio `Q4_K_M`, whose shared tensors are Q4_K/Q6_K). |
| `GGML_CUDA_ROPE_SET_ROWS_V` | **on** (r175, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the fused `RMS_NORM + MUL + ROPE + VIEW + SET_ROWS` kernel (the plain F32/F16 K-cache path) also writes the V row of the immediately following V `VIEW + SET_ROWS` into a cache of the same type and row length, with `k_set_rows`' cast, so the V `set_rows` launch goes away.  The matcher refuses unless the V view is used once, is not a graph output, and the V write's range touches nothing the fused kernel reads or the K rows it writes.  `=0` off.  Worth ~2.2 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MOE_RPB_WIDE` | **on**, RDNA3_0 only (r175, block 10) | **kill-switch** | MoE GEMV (`mul_mat_vec_q_moe`) takes **4 rows per block** instead of 2 when the row has `>= 8` K-blocks, `blocks_per_row_x % n_groups == 0` (so every row keeps its K-blocks in the same thread group and order, which is what makes it bit-identical) and the launch still leaves `>= 32` blocks per WGP.  `=0` restores the 2-row rule.  Worth ~3.6 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode; Qwen3.6-35B-A3B stays at 2 rows by the row-count rule (4 measured slower there).  The gate is the architecture, so it is inert on RDNA4 / RDNA3.5 / NVIDIA. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MWR_FOLD` | **on** (r175, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the 1-token MoE down `MUL_MAT_ID` also writes the following `moe_weighted_reduction` (`fusion.red_dst`): the last expert lane to finish a row block sums `(e * scale) * w` over the experts in `moe_weighted_reduction`'s own order and expression, and the reduction node then launches nothing.  Armed only when the down node's output is exactly the reduction's `experts` operand with no shared-expert merge, when the reduction's output does not overlap the expert-id tensor later blocks still read, and (with `GGML_CUDA_MWR_FOLD` on) it takes **8 rows per block** to quarter the counter atomics.  `=0` off.  Worth ~1.0 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ROPE_QK_PAIR` | **on** (r175, block 10) | **kill-switch** | a layer's Q norm+rope (F32 out) and K norm+rope (with its cache `SET_ROWS`, and the V row when `GGML_CUDA_ROPE_SET_ROWS_V` allows it) run as one `rms_norm_mul_rope_f32_qk_pair` launch, Q blocks first: each block runs exactly the single launch's body with its own recorded arguments.  The Q chain is deferred to the K node, so the matcher refuses unless nothing in between writes over the Q chain's inputs or output and nothing in between reads the Q chain; a deferred Q whose K node is taken by another fusion is launched on its own.  Both chains must have `n_embd_head < 1024`, identical rope parameters and positions.  `=0` off.  Worth ~3.7 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode (the pairing needs the Q and K norm+rope nodes to be adjacent; the upstream `qwen3moe` order is not, and the delivery does **not** carry the submitter's model-file reorder for it). |
| `GGML_CUDA_QK_PAIR_FWHT` | **on** (r187, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the **quantized-KV form** of `GGML_CUDA_ROPE_QK_PAIR`: Q's norm+rope+**rotation** is deferred to the V-rotation + K site seven nodes later (the `GGML_CUDA_QK_ROTATE_HADAMARD` order) and both run as one `rms_norm_mul_rope_f32_qk_pair<.., fwht_n, true>` launch, each block running the single launch's body.  Q's rotation output is checked against every read of the K half and the K/V cache rows against every read of the Q half; an orphaned deferred Q is launched on its own (with the FWHT when that is what was deferred).  `=0` off.  Worth ~4.4 % of q8_0-KV decode (gfx1100). |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_ROW_CONCAT` | **on** (r175: RDNA3_0, r177: RDNA3_0 + RDNA4) | **kill-switch** | two or three consecutive 1-token dense `MUL_MAT`s over the same `src1` with the same weight type and `K` (V, K and Q projections; the FFN gate/up pair is taken by the dual-output fusion instead when it runs first) run as one ksplit launch: the largest weight's rows come first and the others are appended, each written to its own destination, with every row's dot product unchanged.  A separate `row_cat` template instance (the plain ksplit kernels are untouched: an in-kernel segment check cost Qwen3.6 2 % with the feature off).  **RDNA3_0** requires `small_m` and the rows-per-block to agree for each weight and for the total; **RDNA4** (r177) needs only that every segment has an **even** row count, because its single-token launch may take two rows per block and a 2-row block must not straddle a segment boundary (the argument is that at `ncols_dst == 1` the warp count takes no row argument, `small_m` is RDNA3_0-only, and `two_rows`/the decode row loop/`grid_stall_pad` only change which block computes a row).  `=0` off; `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_ROW_CONCAT_RDNA4=0` turns it off on RDNA4 only.  Worth ~2.8 % of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode on RDNA3_0 and ~2.6 % on RDNA4 (on top of `GGML_CUDA_QK_ROTATE`, which is what makes V/K/Q adjacent). |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_ROW_CONCAT_RDNA4` | **on**, RDNA4 only (r177, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the RDNA4 half of the row concat above; `=0` restores the r176 behaviour on RDNA4 (the row concat stays RDNA3_0-only).  Inert on every other arch. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_ROW_CONCAT_MIXED` | **on**, RDNA4 only (r198, block 10) | **kill-switch** | the RDNA4 row concat also takes a **K-quant x Q6_K** pair: when `attn_v` is `Q6_K` while Q/K are `Q4_K`/`Q5_K`/`IQ4_XS` (the K-quant "M" files, e.g. Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B Q4_K_M: 12 of 48 attention layers), one `mul_mat_vec_q_ksplit_cat2` launch runs the `Q6_K` body (with that segment's own row stride) for V's row blocks and the launch type's body for the rest.  Both types take the same nwarps (`static_assert`), every segment's row count must be even, and the launch tensor must be the largest (its row count sizes the row guards); every same-type segment must also share the launch tensor's `nb[1]` (one row stride is passed for those blocks).  The ksplit body is `#include`d from `mmvq-ksplit-body.inc` into the unchanged kernel and into the cat2 launch: as an inlined `__device__` body it changed the compiled code of the multi-column verify kernels and cost 6-7 % of MTP decode on gfx1201 with identical text, acceptance and censuses (the reason `TODO` #128 existed).  `=0` restores the same-type-only matcher.  Worth **+0.6-1.4 % tg128 / +1.0-1.2 % @4096** on Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B and **+1.1 %** at 3 GPU `-sm tensor`; inert on the UD files (their attention is `Q8_0`) and compiled out on RDNA3/RDNA3.5. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_CONCAT_REORDER` | **on**, RDNA3_0 + RDNA4 (r180, block 10) | **kill-switch** | a structural pass in `ggml_backend_cuda_graph_optimize`, right after the V/K rotate, that gathers **small quantized projections into the row concat**: from the first `MUL_MAT` of `<= 256` rows whose weight is quantized (and not `Q8_0`), it moves up to 2 later `MUL_MAT`s with the same `src1`, weight type, `K` and row stride directly after it (at most one of them larger than 256 rows), so the Qwen3.5/3.6 GDN `ssm_beta` + `ssm_alpha` + `attn_gate` projections run as one ksplit launch.  It never moves a `MUL_MAT` whose output feeds a `CONCAT` within 8 nodes (`ggml_cuda_ssm_conv_in_try_fuse` skips those nodes) and never one with a `Q8_0` weight (the SSM prescan matches nodes by position and only fires on `Q8_0`) — both guards are load-bearing, not tuning.  Same nodes, one stream, every ubatch size, idempotent.  `=0` off.  Worth **+2.8 %** of Qwen3.6-35B-A3B decode on gfx1201 (60 fewer launches per token) **only on a GGUF whose GDN small projections are quantized** (llama.cpp-quantized files such as lmstudio-community's; the unsloth UD quants keep them `F32` and the Q8_0 quant is skipped, so it is inert there). |
| `GGML_CUDA_QK_ROTATE` | **on** (r177, block 10) | **kill-switch** | a structural pass in `ggml_backend_cuda_graph_optimize` that moves the V / K projections (their `MUL_MAT` + `RESHAPE`/`VIEW` nodes) of a Q-norm attention layer in front of the Q `MUL_MAT`, so the Q GEMV can join the row concat and the Q / K norm+rope pairs (`GGML_CUDA_ROPE_QK_PAIR`).  It matches the Q chain (`MUL_MAT`, `RESHAPE`, `RMS_NORM`, `MUL`, `ROPE`), requires the nodes in between to be only `MUL_MAT`/`RESHAPE`/`VIEW` that read none of the Q nodes, and matches the K chain's rope parameters and position tensors before rotating — same nodes, one stream, bit-identical.  Structural only (no data pointers), every ubatch size, idempotent.  `=0` off (also disabled with `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`).  Worth **+5.9 %** of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode on gfx1100 and **+3.2 %** on gfx1201 (all of it the re-enabled fusions; on a dense 27B it is flat).  **r187:** with a quantized KV cache `build_attn` rotates Q and K after their ROPE and V before the cache write, so the pass bails at Q's rotation; `GGML_CUDA_QK_ROTATE_HADAMARD` (below) extends it. |
| `GGML_CUDA_QK_ROTATE_HADAMARD` | **on** (r187, block 10) | **kill-switch** | with a **quantized** KV cache the Q chain ends `ROPE -> RESHAPE -> Hadamard MUL_MAT -> RESHAPE` and V's rotation sits between the V and K projections, so the rotate above never matched.  This term takes Q's rotation into the Q chain (`q_end = i + 7`), and **defers V's rotation** — the Hadamard `MUL_MAT`s between the chains, the `RESHAPE`/`VIEW`s they read, and everything that reads one of those — behind the Q chain, while the projections stay in front.  Same nodes, one stream, bit-identical; **1-token graphs only** (the row concat is a 1-token fusion, so prefill keeps the stock order), and the pass is skipped entirely if a node left in front would read a deferred one.  `=0` leaves the Q chain ending at its ROPE (the pre-r187 order).  Worth **+2.8 %** of q8_0-KV Qwen3-30B-A3B decode on gfx1100 on its own; with the three folds below it takes that arm from **726 to 510 kernel launches per token** (= the f16-KV count) and **+13.4 % / +10.1 %** tg128 / @4096 on gfx1201. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_V_FWHT` | **on** (r187, block 10) | **kill-switch** | after the rotate above, V's 64-wide rotation (`fwht_cuda<64>`) sits directly in front of the K chain whose fused kernel already writes the q8_0 K and V rows, so that kernel rotates the V row itself — the same `ggml_cuda_fwht_warp<64, 32>` and the same `ggml_cuda_quantize_q8_0_group` lane->element map — and the standalone FWHT launch goes away.  Requires a single-use rotated V that is not a graph output, a 32-lane wave, `ne0 % 64 == 0`, and a rotation input that does not overlap the cache rows it writes.  `=0` off.  Worth ~1.5 % on top of the rotate (gfx1100). |
| `GGML_CUDA_TOPK_SORTED` | unset = on | **kill-switch** (r52, block 08) | `topk_moe`'s argmax uses DPP lane exchanges (`ggml_cuda_xor_lane`) and per-lane sorted 64-bit keys for the gating outputs (softmax/sigmoid/sqrt-softplus, no bias, no delayed softmax, power-of-2 candidates per lane).  Same ids, weights and order (ties to the lower index).  `0` restores the per-round scan; the argmax rounds drop 7.1 -> 3.8 us per 512-expert/10-used call. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ROUTER_TOPK` | **on** (r182, block 10) | **kill-switch** | folds a 1-token MoE router (`ffn_gate_inp`, an F32 `MUL_MAT` with one column) into the fused `topk_moe` launch: `topk_moe_cuda`'s router mode computes each logit with `mul_mat_vec_f`'s own 256-thread arithmetic and the block that finishes last runs the unchanged selection, so the router GEMV launch disappears.  Fires only where the dispatcher would run `mul_mat_vec_f<float,float,1,256>` (F32, contiguous, 64/128/256 experts, wave32, not MMB, no bias, no delayed softmax), and **steps aside on RDNA4 above 128 experts**, where the one launch measured slower than the two.  `=0` restores the two launches.  Worth **+3.1 %** (tg128) / **+1.7 %** (d4096) of Qwen3-30B-A3B decode on gfx1100 and **+1.2 % / +1.1 %** of Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (256 experts, RDNA3); +1.7 % on gfx1201's 128-expert model (noisy).  **Inert with a speculator** (a verify batch is wider than one token) and on any router with an excluded shape; bit-identical logits (the `--ub 1` prefill-logit probe reads 0.000000 / 100.000 %).  Record: `archive/work/router-topk-fold/PROMOTED.md`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_ROPE_FWHT` | **on** (r187, block 10) | **kill-switch** | a `ROPE` with **no** `RMS_NORM`/`MUL` in front (Llama-style attention) followed by the quantized-KV rotation (`RESHAPE`, Hadamard `MUL_MAT`) runs as one `rms_norm_mul_rope_f32<.., no_norm = true>` launch — the fused norm+rope kernel with the norm skipped and `x` read as is — and for K it also takes the `RESHAPE`/`VIEW`/q8_0 `SET_ROWS` that follow, plus the V row's `VIEW`/`SET_ROWS` when allowed.  NORMAL/NEOX rope with the **full** rotation only (`n_dims == ne0`, no offset), the same rope arithmetic as `rope_norm`/`rope_neox`, the same FWHT and the same q8_0 quantization, and the same hazard checks as the norm form.  **Any token count, so prefill takes it too** — judge it with `gate-prefill-logits.sh --ab "GGML_CUDA_FUSE_ROPE_FWHT=0"` (plus the `--ub 1` arm).  On our models it fires on **gpt-oss** (their Llama-3.2-1B is the submitter's arm), not on qwen/gemma (their ROPE follows a norm).  `=0` off.  Worth **+5.2 %** of Llama-3.2-1B decode (gfx1100), **+4.0 %** tg128 / **+6.1 %** @4096 on gpt-oss-20b (gfx1201). |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRID_STALL_PAD` | on, HIP only (r46, block 07 helper / block 10 relocation) | kill-switch | `0` disables the gfx1201 grid-size dispatch workaround: a launch whose block count is a multiple of 256 (or work-item count a multiple of 65536) waits ~6-11 us on gfx1201 (ROCm/legacy-rocm-build#6689), so `mul_mat_vec_f`, `mul_mat_vec_q_ksplit`, `mul_mat_vec_q_moe`, the FA stream-K fixup, the GDN q/k norm pair and the four `get_rows` launches (r226, issue #277) add one returning block.  Bit-identical.  A site whose one-step pad would land on another multiple of 256 (a `get_rows` grid whose gathered-row count is itself a multiple of 256) is refused, not padded for nothing.  Delete the helper and its call sites once AMD ships the fix. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRID_STALL_PAD_DEBUG` | off | diagnostic | `1` logs one line per pad decision at INFO (`-v`/`-lv 4`): the site tag, the grid and block shape, the block count before and after, or `NO pad possible` when the pad would keep the multiple.  This is what makes "the pad fired at that site" an observation instead of an assumption (`scripts/gate-grid-stall-pad.sh` asserts on it).  Latched once; off has no cost. |

### 7.5 Numerics selectors

These are not optimizations that can be turned off: they choose which of two numerically different
implementations of the same reduction runs.  See the `Numerics selector` class above — treat a change in
one of these as a **new reference**, not as a tuning experiment.

| variable | default | notes |
|---|---|---|
| `GGML_CUDA_RMS_NORM_VEC` | **off** (value-gated: `=1` opts in) | (r145, issue #229) Upstream [ggml-org/llama.cpp#29720](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/29720), which the r127 re-base adopted, replaced `rms_norm_f32`'s **strided scalar** reduction with a **float4** one (a lane owns the four contiguous columns `4*i .. 4*i+3` of `i = tid + k*block_size`).  Different partial sums ⇒ a different last bit ⇒ every layer moves by ~1 ulp, which is enough to move **MTP draft acceptance** by a payload-dependent amount.  Measured on gfx1201 (Qwen3.8-27B UD-Q4_K_XL, 1 GPU, `q8_0` K/V, greedy, `draft-mtp n3`): the default arm gains **+0.7..+1.3 points** of acceptance (94k and 5k contexts), the same model with the SSM decode fusions disabled **loses 18 % t/s** (acceptance 0.611 vs 0.792), and the issue's reporter measures **−7..−9 % tg** on his box.  It is worth **+1.0-2.7 % prefill and ~0 % decode**: 27B 1 GPU pp512 1397 vs 1371, pp2048 1403 vs 1366, pp8192 1341 vs 1316 (tg128 30.89 vs 30.87); 35B-A3B 1 GPU pp2048 4976 vs 4922; 27B 3 GPU `-sm tensor` pp2048 1990 vs 1972.  So the **strided reduction is the default**: it is the reference every recorded MTP/acceptance number, the adaptive-MTP controller's tuning and the decode-band norm fusions were built on — `rms_norm_q8_1_f32` (the rms_norm→Q8_1 fold), `gdn-conv`'s folded q/k norms, the GDN norm gate, `rms_norm_scale_pair_f32` and `hc_combine_norm_f32` are documented **bit-identical to `rms_norm_f32`**, which only holds while both reduce the same way.  Verified with the decode-shape KLD probe (`scripts/gate-prefill-logits.sh --ab "<fusion env>" --ub 1`): every fusion arm is **0.000000 / 100.000 %** on the default and 0.0005 / 99.2 % with `=1` (the fused arm becomes *approximate*, it is not disabled).  `=1` is an A/B / bisection arm, not a shipping configuration.  Rule: `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §60; numbers: `benchmarks/2026-10-26-rms-norm-float4-ab.md`. |

---

## 8. Flash attention / QSA (qwen4exp)

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_HIP_FA_BAND_WIDE` | on | kill-switch | `0` disables the wide band: query widths 5..8 (head 256, `K->ne[1] >= 4096`) then take the same 32-column block twice instead of one 64-column block.  The single pass and its own 16-warp geometry (r134, issue #224 — 16 warps is what keeps its per-column KV split, `np = nwarps*cols_per_warp/ncols = 4`, identical to the 32-column block's, i.e. bit-identical widths 1..8) measured **at parity** with this arm end to end (8K, a 131072-cell FA range and the W=8 MoE arm), so `0` is a bisect/diagnostic switch, not a faster arm. |
| `GGML_HIP_FA_BAND_WMMA` / `GGML_HIP_FA_BAND_WMMA_SPLIT` / `GGML_HIP_FA_BAND_WMMA_GQA_MAX` | unset | tuning | band kernel selection.  `GGML_HIP_FA_BAND_WMMA=0` disables the band; `2` / `4` force `ncols1` (any other non-zero value means 4).  The default is **4 for every K/V type** since r92 (the 2-byte f16/bf16 arm used 2 before TODO #27; with the band's 64-half2 row the narrower tile only wins ~5 % at `n_q = 1` and loses at every verify width).  `SPLIT` = blocks per output tile: default `nsm` for the native-quantized types and `nsm/2` for the 2-byte types (r92; was `3*nsm/4`).  It must not depend on `n_q` or the KV length, or decode and verify would split differently.  `GQA_MAX` (default `12`) is the largest GQA ratio the band folds into its `ncols2 = 8` groups; `<= 8` keeps the old whole-group fold (issue #133). |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_WMMA_256` / `GGML_CUDA_FA_WMMA_MAX_HEAD` | unset | tuning | WMMA tile / head caps.  The RDNA4 cap is 576, which covers the head-512 Gemma 4 global layers; that only pays because r92 retuned the `(512, 512, 32)` RDNA MMA row (issue #188), so `GGML_CUDA_FA_WMMA_MAX_HEAD=256` is the A/B arm that shows the tile kernel is 1.35x slower there. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_RDNA3_WMMA_512` | **on** | kill-switch | r102 (PR #199): RDNA3_0 (gfx1100-1103) head-512 (`DKQ == DV == 512`, e.g. Gemma 4's global layers) takes WMMA prefill on top of the 256 head cap, using its own rows in `ggml_cuda_fattn_mma_get_config_rdna` (Q in shared memory, 128 threads, 64-wide K/V/combine batches: the gfx1201-tuned head-512 rows keep Q in registers and spill ~1.0-1.4k VGPRs per lane on gfx1100).  `=0` sends head 512 back to the tile kernel, which is the pre-r102 behaviour and bit-identical to r101.  `GGML_CUDA_FA_WMMA_256=0` also takes it out; `GGML_CUDA_FA_WMMA_MAX_HEAD` only decides whether head 512 takes WMMA at all (the rows themselves are RDNA3_0-only).  Worth +34 % deep prefill at d32768 and +45 % at d65536 (gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT, `q8_0` K/V) with decode unchanged.  It is a tile->WMMA **family switch**, so it moves the prefill logits against the tile kernel; judged with the gate's `--family-switch` mode (calibrated point + a same-kernel ubatch control) it measures **mean KLD 0.001778 / same-top-p 98.967 %**, i.e. inside the absolute limits, and 8x more faithfully than the same-kernel control; at a wikitext point (base PPL 607) even a benign `-ub` change measures 0.105 / 85 %.  See `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §46 and `archive/work/fa-rdna3-head512-purity/RESOLUTION.md`. |
| `LLAMA_TEST_SEED` / `LLAMA_TEST_OP_NMSE` | unset | diagnostic (test harness only) | r103, `tests/test-backend-ops.cpp`.  `LLAMA_TEST_SEED=<n>` makes the test data deterministic so the same op on two builds/arms can be compared element by element (unseeded, an NMSE moves by up to 13x between runs — that is how the first "WMMA is more accurate" reading in the head-512 campaign was produced and then refuted).  `LLAMA_TEST_OP_NMSE=1` prints the error magnitude every case already computes against the CPU reference, including the passing ones, so a kernel-family switch can be judged by *how far* each arm is from the truth rather than by a pass/fail at a fixed threshold.  Neither variable affects a model run. |
| `GGML_HIP_FA_BAND_FETCH_AHEAD` | **on** | kill-switch | r98 (PR #195): with `q8_0` K and V read in place (decode/verify, `n_q <= 8`, head 256) the RDNA4 band runs a kernel variant that fetches the next K/V batches into registers ahead of use and orders only shared memory at its barriers.  `=0` runs the same kernel without the fetch-ahead, as every other K/V type does.  Bit-identical either way; worth ~+1 % decode at 8K and ~+2.4 % at 110K context (27B, `q8_0` K/V). |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_KV_NATIVE` / `GGML_CUDA_FA_MASK_SKIP` | unset | tuning | native-KV and mask-skip paths. |
| `LLAMA_ATTN_ROT_DISABLE` | unset = rotation **on** | kill-switch / A/B arm (**upstream**, #21038; not added by the delivery) | `1` forces the KV Hadamard rotation off.  With a **quantized** K or V cache (`n_embd_head_%64 == 0`, or a DeepSeek/GLM-DSA lightning indexer) the cache rows are rotated on the way in and the attention result on the way out, so the graph carries four `llama_mul_mat_hadamard` chains per layer plus one rotation constant **per layer** as a graph leaf -- 14 hash entries per layer that `graph_max_nodes()` has to budget for (`INVARIANTS.md` r267, GitHub issue #302: phi-4 `q8_0` needed 2064 against a 1944 budget and aborted).  `1` is the reference arm for a suspected rotation problem and the pre-#21038 numerics; it also drops the budget term with the tensors, so it is the arm that loads a high-layer quantized-KV model when the budget is what is wrong.  Not a speed knob: the rotation is what makes a quantized KV cache accurate, so a cross-arm text comparison is expected to move; measure *within* an arm, never across. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_BAND_SKIP` | **on** | kill-switch (block 05, r133) | the fully-masked KV-group skip in the decode/verify band (PR #223).  With `--kv-unified` a sequence's cells usually sit behind another slot's, and since `LLAMA_SERVER_LAZY_IDLE_SLOTS` (r130) an idle slot's context stays resident, so the band used to read every one of those `-INF` cells on every decode step.  The band now gets the packed per-(stream, query tile, 256-cell group) bitmap and skips the groups that are `-INF` for every query row of its tile, keeping its round-robin KV split (block i still owns iterations i, i+P, ...), so the result is bit-identical.  `0` restores the pre-r133 behaviour; `GGML_CUDA_FA_MASK_SKIP=0` disables the whole mechanism (this switch is the band's arm of it).  The band is the gate, so it applies on RDNA4, head 256, GQA 5..12, `n_q <= 8` only; the tile-kernel decode other models use does not skip.  Measured (gfx1201, 27B UD-Q4_K_XL, 2 slots unified, `q8_0` K/V, 384 greedy tokens): a resident ~105K conversation in front of an active 9.8K one, **50.8 -> 41.7 ms/step (-18 %)**, token ids identical (with a DFlash2 draft at `n_max 5`: 18.6 -> 14.8); a single slot at 9.8K **-0.6 %** and at 105K neutral (the padded tail group is skipped too, so the per-layer prepass is not a tax). |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB` | unset | tuning | FA staging budget: the **per-operand** cap on a staging request (MiB, `0` = unbounded).  A K/V view larger than the cap is not staged at all (the native read is used); it also caps the growth hint, so it is the knob that bounds the arena on a deep prefill.  It is per operand because the arena request covers **both** operands: `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB=256` on a K+V op caps each at 256 MiB and the arena at **512 MiB** (the reporter's question on issue #237 — intended, not rounding).  `=1` is the "never stage" arm (the native read). |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_CHUNK` | off (`0`/unset) | **opt-in** (r159) | `<rows>` = the FA prefill staging becomes a **KV-chunked ring**: a wide, quantized-cache prefill is launched one KV chunk at a time over ONE slot of `<rows>` F16 rows per operand instead of converting the whole prefix into the arena first, and the launcher merges the chunks with an online softmax and normalizes once.  Same attention work and the same total conversion volume; per chunk it pays a fold plus a merge pass.  **The staging transient becomes O(chunk) and flat at any depth**: a 16384-row chunk is **64 MiB** on a 27B against 160 MiB at 33k / up to 1024 MiB at `-c 262144`.  **Use it when the prefix-sized transient is what hurts** — tight VRAM against `--fit`, or Windows/WDDM where the late arena step lands in shared memory (issues #138/#237).  Cost, measured (27B UD-Q4_K_XL, 1 GPU, q8_0 KV, `-ub 512`, `llama-bench -r 3`): **+0.0 % at pp32768** (2 chunks) and **-4.1 % at pp65536** (4 chunks), i.e. ~1.5 % of prefill per chunk beyond the second; smaller chunks cost proportionally more (8192: -2.9 % at 32k, 4096: -4.4 %, 2048: -6.1 %).  The same shape applies to prefill-logit drift (KLD 0.001 at 2 chunks, inside the gate; 0.0053 at 4 chunks, just outside it) and greedy text is unchanged up to ~5 chunks.  Rows are rounded up to `lcm(nbatch_fa, FATTN_KQ_STRIDE)` (256 on the RDNA4 prefill row) and chunking only engages when the prefix needs **>= 2 chunks**.  Recommended `16384`.  **On a slab config (`-ncmoe` host experts) leave it off** — that arena already comes out of the slab's wide-aux region and is released at the prefill->decode drop, so a ring buys nothing there.  `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_CHUNK_DEBUG=1` prints one `FA_CHUNK n_q= n_kv= rows= align= chunks= ring= chunk= nbatch_fa=` decision line per op (llama-bench swallows logs; use `llama-cli -v`). |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_PREALLOC` | **`1`** | tuning (opt-in `2`) | Growth policy of the prefill staging arena: `0` = the plain 1.25x ladder (pre-r53, issue #138), `1` = **the default**: the r156 ceiling (a growth aims for at most 4x the request), `2` = **eager** (r159, opt-in): allocate the root-based hint in ONE go at the first request — the r149 behaviour, and the answer for Windows/WDDM, where a freed allocation stays committed and a late step lands in shared memory.  The reporter's re-test measured **pp 592 -> 781** (r149: 791) and one allocation at 0:22 instead of a `16 -> 72 -> 296 -> 1024` MiB ladder at 1:48; the trade is that the arena goes back to its r149 **peak** (1 GiB per context at `-c 262144`), which `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_CHUNK` or `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB` then bound.  `2` is opt-in on purpose: eager costs the full peak even when memory is free, so the default stays one policy on every platform.  The prefill staging arena's growth is capped **upwards** by the F16 size of the K/V view roots (per-operand capped by `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB`) and aims for at most 4x the request that just arrived, so a shallow prompt stays at the 16 MiB floor while a deep one reaches the hint in <= 3 steps; the hint is only honoured while `optional_alloc_within_free_cap` allows it.  **It is a ceiling, not a target** (r156, issue #237): treating it as a target allocated 1 GiB per context on the first prefill ubatch of a `-c 262144` config, and since r125 the MTP draft context stages too, which made a `q8_0` draft KV cost more than the f16 default it saved.  An unbounded cap (`GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB=0`) passes no hint at all.  `GGML_CUDA_SLAB_STATS=1` prints one `FA_STAGE dev= stream= request= cached= alloc=` line per growth (the arena's resulting size is the `alloc` field). |

**The FA prefill staging transient: four documented choices, no free lunch.**  The staging arena is an F16 copy
of the K/V rows a prefill reads.  It exists because reading the quantized cache per output tile is slower
(`GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB=1` measures that arm).  Its *size* is the problem: it is proportional to the
prefix, it can approach an F16 cache's size at depth, and on WDDM it can land in shared memory.  Pick your
poison — all numbers are 27B UD-Q4_K_XL, 1 GPU, q8_0 KV, `-ub 512`, relative to the default arm:

| arm | prefill transient | prefill cost |
|---|---|---|
| **(nothing — the default)** | O(prefix): 160 MiB at 33k, up to 1024 MiB at `-c 262144` (transient, freed at decode) | fastest |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_PREALLOC=2` | same size, but **one allocation at the start** (r149 behaviour) | none (bit-identical) — the Windows/WDDM answer |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_CHUNK=16384` | **64 MiB, flat at any depth** | +0.0 % at 32k, -4.1 % at 64k |
| `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB=1` (or `<MiB>`) | **none** (native read), or a per-operand cap | -1.5 / -2.7 / -5.1 % at 8k/16k/32k (or the cap's own cost) |
| `LLAMA_QSA_OFF` | off | kill-switch | disables QSA. |
| `LLAMA_QSA_SPARSE_FA` | on | kill-switch | `0` = the dense masked path. **This is the QSA oracle**: the `W=1..8` matrix is blind to a width-uniform corruption, so measure the perplexity ratio against `LLAMA_QSA_SPARSE_FA=0` when touching the QSA op. |
| `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED` | **on** | kill-switch (block 05) | `0` builds and fills the packed `n_kv × n_q` mask instead of registering the derived per-cell/per-query inputs, i.e. the pre-derived-V3 path.  Kill switch and **A/B reference arm**: the derived form is bit-identical to it at the logits (`gate-prefill-logits.sh --ab "LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED=0"` measures 0.000000 / 100.000 % on bailingmoe3), and it is also the work-around for a kq-mask fill site that is not guarded (r131 closed the last three — see `patches/README.md` block 05).  The derived form is decided per batch: a batch it cannot serve (2-D positions, alibi, or a multi-sequence batch that `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED_MULTISEQ`/`kq_mask_derivable_seqs()` refuses — see that row) falls back to the packed mask by itself. |
| `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED_MULTISEQ` | **on** | kill-switch (r172, block 05) | `0` restores the single-sequence rule: a ubatch holding several sequences (`--parallel > 1 --kv-unified`: two slots prefilling/verifying together, one slot's prefill next to another's decode) takes the packed mask, and `sched_reserve()` reserves it again.  On, sequence `k` of the ubatch has its cells and its tokens' window shifted by `k * 2^23`, so the one predicate `tok_lo <= cell_pos <= tok_hi` keeps each token inside its own sequence's range and the kernels are unchanged.  Taken only with one KV stream, more than 32 tokens, every token in exactly one sequence, no cell carrying two of the batch's sequences (a prefix copied with `llama_memory_seq_cp`; O(1) unless one exists) and every position below `2^23`; a batch that does not qualify latches `kq_mask_multiseq_seen` and re-reserves once with the packed mask, so nothing grows mid-run.  **Measure and use this as the A/B reference arm** (`gate-prefill-logits.sh --ab "LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED_MULTISEQ=0"`): the derived form is bit-identical at the logits (0.000000 / 100.000 % at `--ub 2048`, 4 sequences per batch, f16 and q8_0 KV).  Worth **1.03 GiB of device memory and 0.94 GiB of pinned host memory** on `-c 245760 -ub 2048 --parallel 2 --kv-unified` (dense 27B, r172), neutral on prefill time; no effect on a graph that also holds an `allow_derived = false` builder (qwen4exp/QSA, MLA), whose packed mask exists anyway. |
| `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED_REUSE` | **on** | kill-switch / A/B arm (r164, block 05) | Whether the derived kq mask input may be reused when the graph's memory context wraps the attention cache it was built from (a `llama_memory_hybrid_context`, i.e. every Qwen3.x hybrid).  `0` restores the pre-r164 rejection, under which **every** single-sequence batch of more than 8 tokens rebuilt its graph — a long n-gram draft's verification, or an MTP verify step at `--spec-draft-n-max >= 8` (measured: 0 graphs reused over 300 tokens at a fixed depth 15; r163 was 7.1 % slower than its own `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED=0` at 3 GPU and 5.4 % at 2 GPU).  Reuse only — no arithmetic, no kernel: greedy text and draft acceptance are byte-identical on and off.  iSWA (`llama_memory_hybrid_iswa_context`) keeps rejecting in both arms, because it builds two derived inputs (base and SWA) and there is no single cache to look through; `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED=0` remains the packed-mask reference arm. |
| `LLAMA_KQ_MASK_PACKED_RESERVE` | unset = auto | **A/B arm / safety valve** (r160, block 05) | Whether `sched_reserve()` sizes the compute buffers for the **packed** kq mask.  Auto reserves it for an alibi model, a context whose multi-sequence batches the derived form cannot serve (`LLAMA_KQ_MASK_DERIVED_MULTISEQ=0`, a multi-stream cache, or a memory `kq_mask_kvs()` does not cover — else nothing, see that row), and an M-RoPE model that can actually receive 2-D positions — `llama_context_params.pos_2d`, which `common` sets when `--mmproj` is given, or a 2-D batch that arrives on a context which did not declare one (that first batch forces one re-reserve, logged as `decode: 2-D (M-RoPE) positions in this batch - re-reserving with the packed kq mask`).  A **text-only** M-RoPE context (Qwen3.x is IMROPE even for text, where every position is degenerate) therefore reserves the much smaller **derived** layout: at `-c 220160 -ub 2048` that is **860 MiB of VRAM and 860 MiB of pinned host memory** per context (issue #238).  `1` = always reserve the packed mask for an M-RoPE context (the pre-r160 behaviour, i.e. the exact A/B base); `0` = never (derived only, latch disabled; the growth path is the fallback).  Allocation sizes only — no kernel, no default and no logit changes in either direction. |
| `GGML_CUDA_QSA_SCORE_LOG` | off | **diagnostic** (r236, block 11; `TODO` #297) | `1` prints one `indexer-score: fused-score launched name= dst=[blocks,tokens,streams] ktype= derived-cache|full-read` line on stderr for each of the first 4 launches of the fused indexer-score op (and the same for the derived-cache fill: `derived-fill launched`).  It exists because the **sparse QSA decode arm is env-forced and both ops are device-only**, so a re-derivation that stops building the node is silent — a text comparison of the arm against itself cannot see an op that never launches (a broken sparse arm aborted for five re-base rungs with nothing to notice).  stderr, not the log stream, so it lands in every arm's log at any verbosity and can never perturb the generated text; `scripts/gate-qwen4exp-quant-coherence.sh` section E asserts ≥ 1 line on the sparse arm, **0** on its `GGML_CUDA_QSA_INDEXER_SCORE=0` chain arm and **0** on the dense arm (so the census's sensitivity is proved every run).  No cost when unset. |
| `LLAMA_QSA_KEYS_ONLY`, `LLAMA_QSA_PLE_HOSTGATHER`, `LLAMA_QSA_DENSE_SHORTCUT`, `LLAMA_QSA_SCORE_BOUNDS`, `LLAMA_QSA_SCORE_STRIP`, `LLAMA_QSA_SCORE_WMMA`, `LLAMA_QSA_SCORE_WMMA_MB`, `GGML_CUDA_QSA_IDENTITY`, `GGML_CUDA_QSA_SLICES`, `GGML_CUDA_QSA3`, `GGML_CUDA_QSA_INDEXER_CACHE`, `GGML_CUDA_QSA_INDEXER_SCORE`, `GGML_QSA_DERIVED_BIAS`, `GGML_QSA_DERIVED_VIS`, `GGML_QSA_SCORE_MEM` | off / unset | tuning | QSA variants and indexer tuning. |

The QSA op has a CPU oracle: `test-backend-ops -o FLASH_ATTN_QSA` (18/18 minimum).  The **fused
indexer-score** op (`GGML_OP_INDEXER_SCORE`, what `GGML_CUDA_QSA_INDEXER_SCORE` builds) has **no**
`test-backend-ops` case and no CPU implementation of its own, so its oracle is the model arm:
`gate-qwen4exp-quant-coherence.sh` section E requires the sparse arm's text to equal its
`GGML_CUDA_QSA_INDEXER_SCORE=0` chain arm byte for byte — that chain (the per-op rope + `mul_mat` + relu +
head-sum the fused op replaces) is the reference.  A `test-backend-ops` case would need a hand-written
reference for the whole fused sequence, which is why it is recorded as optional rather than owed
(`TODO` #297, closed with the model arm + the reachability census).
