## 9. GDN / SSM (block 02)

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_CUDA_GDN_CHUNKED` | on | kill-switch | compared as a **string** against `"0"` — literally `GGML_CUDA_GDN_CHUNKED=0` forces the sequential kernel on the whole batch (correct, bit-identical, slow; the exact reference the prefill-logit gate compares against). The chunked prefill is K-independent with threshold `max(K>16?K:16, n_rs_batch)`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GDN_TAIL` | on (r91, block 10) | kill-switch | inside that chunked path, a single-sequence batch above the threshold runs the chunked kernel on its first `n_tokens - D` tokens and the **sequential** kernel on the last `D = max(K>16?K:16, n_rs_batch)` in the same launch, so the rollback snapshots `0..D-1` exist for every batch.  `=0` restores whole-batch chunking (the r90 behaviour, and the A/B base for the prefill-logit gate).  Prefill-only (decode / verify batches ≤ the threshold never take it); no extra memory or graph; measured pp2048 −0.58 %, decode flat. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GDN_CHUNKED_BF16` | on | kill-switch | BF16/WMMA chunked GDN variant, **default-on since r30** (RDNA3/RDNA4, `S_v == 128` prefill). Near-lossless, not bit-exact (bf16 operands, fp32 accumulation; ~1e-5 op NMSE; prefill mean KLD 0.0007 / 0.00005 / 0.00015 on gfx1201 / gfx1100 / gfx1151, same-top-p >= 98.7 %). `=0` runs the fp32 chunked kernel. r29 had defaulted it off as a workaround for the n_seqs > 1 `A_sc` stride aliasing bug (issue #113), fixed in r30. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_STATE_GATHER` | on (r46, block 15) | kill-switch | in the one-sequence `<= 16`-token decode/verify band the graph skips the per-GDN-layer recurrent-state `GET_ROWS` and the sequential kernel reads `s0` from the cache row directly (`GET_ROWS` is a plain copy, so bit-identical).  Uses the §7.1 presence idiom (unset = on, `0` = gather as before). |
| `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GDN_CONV_SNAP` | on (r183, block 10) | kill-switch | where the direct GDN conv runs, its kernel also writes the 3-column recurrent snapshot tails, so the `CONCAT` and the snapshot `CPY`s launch nothing.  Only taken when a runtime byte-span check shows nothing between the CONCAT and the conv writes over `state`/`x` or touches a snapshot destination and the destinations are disjoint; the batched-copy run stops at a folded snapshot `CPY`.  `1` restores the r182 path (a plain `CONCAT` + `cpy_batch` run).  Bit-identical: the values the CPYs would write are the ones the kernel writes, and the conv matcher already requires the CONCAT to have no reader other than the conv and the snapshot views. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_GATHER` | on (r183, block 10) | kill-switch | in the one-sequence, `<= 16`-token band, `build_rs`'s `GET_ROWS` of the conv cache (the `CONCAT`'s state operand) is deferred into the direct conv kernel, which reads the cache row itself (`row id = the gather's src[1]`) and writes it back; the three state values are loaded into registers first.  Requires the snapshot fold above, the CONCAT to be the gather's only reader and only folded snapshot `CPY`s writing the cache before the conv.  `0` (or `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_STATE_GATHER=0`, which turns both off) restores the gather.  A `CONCAT` over a deferred gather the direct conv does not take **aborts** by design. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_DECODE` | on (r183, block 10) | kill-switch | the 1-token direct GDN conv on RDNA4 / RDNA3_0, and only where it also elides the q/k l2 norm pair (`gdn_conv_norm_match() > 0`) and the decode-only `ssm_prescan` would not take the conv first.  It is the enabler for the two folds above at one token and is worth +0.3 % on its own; the dense Q8_0 case that cost −1.1 % in PR #249 stays neutral because the norm-match gate keeps the `ssm_conv_in` epilogue (and its free `CONCAT` write) there.  `0` turns off only the 1-token path; `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_VERIFY=0` still turns off the whole band. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_STORE` | on (r244, block 10; **1..8 sequences since r249**) | kill-switch | the **streamed-KV-era** (`upstream #31660`) GDN conv step of a speculative graph (1..16 tokens *a sequence*, **1..8 sequences**, RDNA4) as one kernel: it reads the conv state (the cache row itself when the `GET_ROWS` is deferred), `x` and the taps, and writes the conv+silu row, the normalized q / k and the `Kw` snapshot windows straight into the rollback slots, so the `CONCAT`, the window view, the `SET_ROWS`, `SSM_CONV`, `SILU` and the q/k norm pair all launch nothing.  The chain it replaces is what a graph with `n_rs_seq > 0` (i.e. every MTP/DFlash verify graph) runs when this stands down.  Bit-identical: the norm reproduces `rms_norm_scale_pair_f32<256>`'s reduction (the square summed in the column loop, a head's four warp sums as `((s0+s2)+(s1+s3))`, a run-time divisor, `contract(off)`), measured as prefill-logit KLD -0.000000 / 99.990 % and width-probe maxdiff 0 at `P=2048/3584/4096` on 27B and 35B-A3B, 1 GPU and 3-GPU `-sm tensor`/`-sm layer`.  `=0` restores the chain; **`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_CONV_FUSION=1` also stands this kernel down** -- it replaces the same conv step as the direct conv, and with only the direct conv disabled the "chain" reference arm still ran this kernel and the W=1 logits diverged from W=8 by 5.88 (the r244 measurement; it is fixed, and the gate would be vacuous otherwise).  `GGML_CUDA_CONV_DEBUG=1` prints `GDN_CONV_STORE C=.. T=.. S=.. Kw=.. col0=.. K=.. private_chain=..` once per process **for each sequence count S it fires at**, the census that proves it fired -- without it a run of this path cannot be told from the chain.  The multi-sequence form is only reachable on a **`--kv-unified` server whose verify ubatch carries several sequences** (`-np 2` with two slots decoding together, DFlash or MTP), where the state stays the `GET_ROWS` copy and the gather is deferred for `S = 1` only; r249 measured it bit-identical to r248 and bit-identical to the chain (`=0` and `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_CONV_FUSION=1`) on both texts and every per-token logprob, ~1 % per verify step, `WORKLOG.md` 261110.005. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_SHEXP_GATE_SIGMOID` | on (r183, block 10) | kill-switch | the shared-expert gate `MUL_MAT` (one row) + `SIGMOID` + `MUL` + `ADD` of qwen4exp in one `mul_mat_vec_f` launch: every block recomputes the one gate row and applies `sigmoid_mul_add_f32`'s own `op_sigmoid` and `contract(off)` expression to its slice, so the gate, the sigmoid and the product are never stored.  BF16/F32 gate weights only (F16 can take a half accumulator), decode/verify band.  Inert on every quantized model we hold and **+0.6 %** on Flash-Next GSQ-IQ3_XXS tg128. |
| `LLAMA_RS_ROLLBACK_DEBUG` | unset | **diagnostic** | r91 (block 03).  `1` logs one `RSPREP` line per prepared ubatch and one `RSROLL` line per partial recurrent rollback (rollback distance, the sequence's own last-batch shape, `n_rs_seq`, `n_rs_batch`).  This is the instrument that proved the issue-#131 warning was a stale per-sequence tracker entry.  Verbose; diagnosing only. |
| `GGML_CUDA_GDN_GATHER_INDEX` | unset = on (r68) | **kill-switch / diagnostic** | replaces the `O(gathers × nodes)` `ggml_cuda_gdn_state_gather_deferrable` forward walk with one index per graph evaluation, built **lazily on the first candidate** so a non-GDN graph pays only one pointer check per `GET_ROWS`.  `0` = the per-gather walk; `2` = both, `GGML_ABORT` on a different answer.  Conservative relative to the walk (a disagreement can only drop a deferral, never add one), so the kernel path is unchanged.  Issue #151: 0.63 ms/graph less host work on gfx1201 (13 %), ~+0.3 % decode on a slow host. |
| `GDN_DBG_SKIP_KKT`, `GDN_DBG_SKIP_SCAN` | off | diagnostic | skip stages (debug). |

---

## 10. MMB (block 08)

The `mmb` campaign is part of the delivery.  `GGML_CUDA_MMB` defaults to `1`; the rest are tuning knobs
for tile/shape selection and cache sizing.  Treat them as internal: the validated configuration is the
default, and they exist to reproduce measurements from `benchmarks/`.

`GGML_CUDA_MMB`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_BF16W`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_CACHE`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_CFG`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_DENSE`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_DENSE_TYPES`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_DOWN16`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_F32SPLIT`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_F32SPLIT_MIN_K`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_F32SPLIT_MIN_M`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_GLU`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_GLU_THRESH`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_HC16`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_IQ3XXS`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_MIN_T`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_RDNA3`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_ROUTED`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_ROUTED_THRESH`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_SHADOW`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_SHADOW_MB`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_TALL`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_TALL_MIN_M`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_TILE`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_TINY_M`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_TINY_TT`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_TYPES`, `GGML_CUDA_MMB_LOG`,
`GGML_CUDA_MMB_MARK_LOG`, `LLAMA_MMB_CVT_LOG`, `LLAMA_MMB_F32SPLIT`, `LLAMA_HC_BLK16`,
`LLAMA_HC_GATEMIX`, `LLAMA_HC_RES16`.

The per-arch policy above (`GGML_CUDA_MMB_*` defaults, the weight-type sets, the dense flag) is resolved
**per device** from the cc of the device the kernel runs on, not from `devices[0]` (r132), and
`graph_optimize` selects its own context's device before consulting it.  That is what makes a mixed-arch
box (an RDNA2 card beside an RDNA3/RDNA4 one) behave: MMB stays off on the RDNA2 device.  There is no knob
— on a single-arch box the two spellings are the same cc (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §56).  **Since r245 every one
of those accessors is per DEVICE too** (they used to cache the first caller's value process-wide, so the
first device decided the whole box); `GGML_CUDA_MMB_CFG=1` now prints one `MMB_CFG device=<n> …` line per
device at `-lv`-independent WARN level, which is how a mixed box's two rows are read off.

### 10.1 `GGML_CUDA_MMF32` -- the F32 skinny prefill GEMM (block 06)

`mmf32` serves the F32 prefill GEMMs that `mmb` cannot (non-quantized weights, more than 16 token
columns, `m <= 256`: the GDN `ssm_alpha`/`ssm_beta` projections and the MoE routers).  Taking them out of
rocBLAS/hipBLASLt is what makes those models' prefill logits reproducible across fresh processes, and it is
faster there (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §47).

| variable | default | kind | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GGML_CUDA_MMF32` | **on** (RDNA4, RDNA3_5); unset = off on RDNA3 | kill-switch | `=0` disables (the old rocBLAS path).  **RDNA4 (gfx1201) and RDNA3_5 (gfx1151) are the validated devices**, so the kernel is default-on there and the variable exists to disable it; both passed the same four legs (exact, deterministic by construction, a prefill win, and a `--family-switch` PASS at a confident point — `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §47).  gfx1151: `test-backend-ops -o MUL_MAT` 2444/2444 with NMSE 6.11e-14 vs rocBLAS 6.83e-13, 12/12 fresh starts one logits class, **+1.0 % pp512 / +1.6 % pp2048** with decode flat, family-switch 0.013016 / 98.673 % against a same-kernel control of 0.019125 / 97.975 %.  On **RDNA3 (gfx1100) it is not a win** (−1.3…−2.2 % at `pp2048`, one visit a tie), so it stays **opt-in** (`=1`) there until that box has a pinned per-arch tile. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MMF32_LOG` | unset | diagnostic | prints the first 16 resolved plans on stderr (`MMF32 m=… n=… k=… tile=… ks=…`).  Does not change what is computed. |

Scope: F32 weights only, `m <= 256`, `17 <= n <= 65536`, `256 <= k <= 8192`, `ldc == m`; every other mul_mat
keeps the rocBLAS call.  BF16/f16 prefill (Flash-Next) is untouched, and so is its hipBLASLt advantage; a
full-F32 model's FFN GEMMs (`m >> 256`) stay on rocBLAS (a recorded limitation).

---

## 11. Diagnostics and profiling

None of these change the computed result on the default path.

| variable | notes |
|---|---|
| `GGML_CUDA_OP_TIMING` | per-op GPU timing. |
| `GGML_FORCE_N_RS_SEQ` | **diagnostic** (block 10) | forces the target's rollback snapshot depth (`cparams.n_rs_seq`) so a plain `llama-cli`/`llama-perplexity` arm can reach the `n_rs_seq > 0` graph the GDN conv store and the conv restore live on.  It is the only way to gate those fusions without a draft model.  **Since r250 the value must fit one ubatch**: the rollback tracker keeps `n_rs_seq + 1` tail tokens inside a single ubatch, so the arm needs `--ub >= n_rs_seq + 2` and the context constructor now refuses anything narrower with a one-line error naming the minimum instead of aborting in `llama_batch_allocr::split_equal()` (`TODO` #315).  `GGML_FORCE_N_RS_SEQ=3` therefore needs `--ub 5`, and the `--ub 4`/`--ub 1` arms of the r244 recipe are impossible at that depth by construction (`--ub 8` is the working narrow arm). |
| `GGML_CUDA_GCDBG` | CUDA-graph debug. |
| `GGML_STREAMDBG` | stream debug. |
| `GGML_ALLOCATOR_DEBUG` | build-flag companion for allocator tracing. |
| `GGML_CUDA_MWR_DEBUG`, `GGML_CUDA_HC_BLK16_DEBUG`, `GGML_CUDA_IDX_RELU_SUM_LOG`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_CPY_BATCH_DEBUG`, `GGML_CUDA_CONV_DEBUG`, `LLAMA_HC_CN_DEBUG`, `LLAMA_HC_GATEMIX_DEBUG`, `LLAMA_QSA_SCORE_STRIP_DEBUG`, `GGML_CUDA_QSA_DEBUG` | feature-specific debug output.  (`GGML_CUDA_CONV_FUSION_MULTI` was the conv fusion's old force-on knob; **retired in r186** — the fusion is on for every device count and `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_CONV_FUSION` is the off switch, so it is no longer read.) |
| `GGML_CUDA_NORM_ROWS_DEBUG` | **diagnostic** (r225, block 10) | `1` logs one decision line per narrow-row norm candidate: `norm-rows: i=<n> fused=1 gated=<0|1> rows=<n> ncols=<n> x=<ptr> z=<ptr> dst=<ptr>` when the fusion runs and `norm-rows: i=<n> declined=<reason> gated=<0|1> x=<ptr> z=<ptr> dst=<ptr>` when the aliasing guard sends the node back to the unfused chain (`reason` is `gate-out`, `dst-x` or `dst-w`, issue #276).  **`declined=gate-out gated=0` was the GitHub-issue-#285 signature** (a plain match that had inherited a failed gated match's gate index) and must not appear from r238 on; `gate-out gated=1` is the legitimate #276 alias decline.  Diagnostic only, never a behaviour switch — it is how `scripts/gate-norm-rows-alias.sh` proves both that the fusion is live and that an arm really reached the guard.  The line is `INFO`, so it needs `-lv 4` (`-v`) on `llama-cli`/`llama-server`; the gate's own driver prints it unmodified. |
| `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_TRACE` | **diagnostic**, presence-only (block 10) | one line per process per class of fusion decision the cache/fusion gates make: `FUSE_TRACE ne2=<n> BLOCK|allow (<why>)` from the expert cache's band gating of a routed MoE fusion, and (r239, issue #287) `FUSE_TRACE ALIAS <dst-op><-<src-op>/<copy|node>@<elements>` for every `ggml_cuda_check_fusion_memory_ranges()` decline, where `copy` is a **compute-buffer leaf** (a scheduler copy of a host expert table) and `node` is a graph input or an op node outside the window.  Diagnostic only, never a behaviour switch: it is how `scripts/gate-fusion-alias-copy.sh` proves the leaf-copy check really declined something (a run that logs no `ALIAS .../copy@` line has not reached the #287 shape, so its text comparison would be vacuous).  The line is **`WARN`**, so `llama-cli` needs `-lv 2` (its default is errors-only) and the trace arm's generated text must be thrown away. |
| `FA`, `KV`, `RS`, `GDN_DBG_A`, `GDN_DBG_P`, `GDN_DBG_ONES`, `GDN_DBG_DUMP`, `GDN_DBG_SAVE` | **internal probes.** Single/double-letter or `_DBG_` names used by debug dump blocks. Never set these in production — two of them take a value that selects a dump *mode*. |

## 12. Non-ggml environment (HIP/ROCm runtime)

These are not llama.cpp variables but they control how the delivery runs.

| variable | notes |
|---|---|
| `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES` | which GPUs are visible, in order. Part of every validated command (`0,1` or `0,1,2`). |
| `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` | must include the matching ROCm runtime libs, e.g. `/opt/rocm-10.1.0-gfx120X/lib` (the canonical build tree), or the build will not start. |
| `ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES` | lower-level alternative to `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES`; prefer `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES`. |
| `GGML_CUDA_ENABLE_UNIFIED_MEMORY` | forces managed allocations; not used in this repo's validated configurations. |

There is **no** environment variable for thread pinning; use `-t` / `--cpu-mask` as described in §5.

## 13. Retired / superseded

| variable | status |
|---|---|
| `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_RESERVE_MIB=8192` | the pre-r20 server workaround. **No longer needed** — the default `1024` plus the r20 compute-buffer slack and the fail-soft arena yield cover it. |
| `MOE_ARENA_HEADROOM_MIB` | tried and **removed**. A flat arena headroom changes nothing: the failure is a realloc needing a block bigger than the one just freed, so the slack has to be on the *allocation*, not the arena. |
| `MTP_DRAFT_N_UBATCH=4` | was a workaround for the same failure; a cap is now the default (`512`), so this is just an over-tight value. |
| `GGML_HIP_NO_VMM` (build option, not runtime) | HIP VMM is **off by default** (`ON` = do not use VMM).  Whether to enable it is an open investigation — see `archive/work/moe-cache-autosize/FOLLOWUP-compute-arena-chunking.md`. |
| `LLAMA_SPEC_DRAFT_WINDOW` | **removed in r246**: promoted to the first-class option `--spec-draft-kv-window [auto\|N]` (§17.2).  The env var is not read any more — a leftover export is silently ignored, so check the startup log (`MTP draft KV window`) after an upgrade. |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_POOL` | **removed in r246**: promoted to the first-class option, now spelled `--kv-attendance-pool [on\|off]` (r247; `--kv-region-pool` is kept as an alias, §17.1).  Same caveat: a leftover export is ignored, and the pool is **off** unless the flag asks for it. |
| `LLAMA_ARG_KV_REGION_POOL` | **still honoured**, but it is now the env form of `--kv-attendance-pool [on\|off]`: the size is derived and a numeric value is accepted-and-ignored (r247, §17.1). |
| `GGML_SCHED_STAGE_MODE` | **removed in r250**: the staging **redirect** form (`1` = point the split input at the ring slot instead of copying it in with `stage_d2d`).  Deleted with `TODO` #284.  It bought one D2D (**+0.2-0.3 %** prefill, measured) and its correctness rested on "the consuming graph is not captured" -- i.e. on the staging width gate keeping the ring out of the *captured* decode band, a heuristic rather than an invariant (the meta path had already defaulted its own redirect off for that reason on 2026-10-06, `archive/docs/TENSOR-CORRUPTION.md`), and the VMEM it was meant to buy (`input_cpy`, ~1 GiB on Flash-Next) needs an arena-sizing policy that does not exist.  A leftover export is silently ignored: the stage-then-D2D path is now unconditional.  Detail: `INVARIANTS.md` r250. |

---

## 14. Build-time gates (not environment variables)

These are compile-time, because a device-side runtime gate does not work cleanly here.

| flag | default | what |
|---|---|---|
| `-DGGML_HIP_RDNA4_Q4K_DENSE_VDR4` | off | RDNA4 uses the 32-element dense Q4_K VDR-4 dot product (`vec_dot_q4_K_q8_1_vdr4_dense`); RDNA3_0 always uses it (PR #162 0002).  One flag gates both the device selection (`mmvq-vecdot.cuh`) and the host K-split (`get_vdr_mmvq_dense`), because the K partition must match.  It changes the K summation order, so greedy text changes on some prompts (deterministically); width purity is preserved (`none == draft-mtp`, width probe PASS).  Measured only ~+0.3 % `tg128` on a single R9700, so it stays off by default. |
| `-DGGML_HIP_RDNA4_Q4K_MOE_VDR4` | off | the **MoE expert** kernel (`mul_mat_vec_q_moe`) uses `vec_dot_q4_K_q8_1_vdr4_dense` (PR #167 0002; RDNA3_0 uses it by default since r79).  Same 32-element layout and VDR 4, so only the summation changes (one integer `dp4a` chain per sub-block); the MoE VDR, the width-uniform selector and the pinned fusion `calc_nwarps` are unchanged.  Separate from the dense flag above, so the two can be A/B'd independently.  The patch also defines a host-visible `ggml_cuda_rdna4_q4k_moe_vdr4` constant so ccache keys the HIP object on this device-only flag (without it the unchanged object was returned for the flag toggle).  It changes greedy text on some prompts.  RDNA4 2-GPU tensor measured flat on single-token decode (+0.06 % tg256), +0.5-1.5 % at the verify widths (batched B=1/4/8), and mixed MTP (R +3.4 %, K +0.8 %, P -1.65 %, C -0.5 %); not a clear default-on win, so it stays **off**.  RDNA3_0 ignores it (its path is already on). |
| `-DGGML_HIP_Q6K_DENSE_VDR1` | off (dense Q6_K VDR=2) | the dense Q6_K `mmvq` uses the **VDR=1** chunk (one block element per thread iteration) instead of upstream #30517's VDR=2 on **every** arch, i.e. the association order a pre-#30517 base (`097cf33d9` and earlier, the whole v14 line) produced.  This is a **reproducibility** escape, not a performance switch: the flag exists for a consumer that gates on output *identity* across a base bump (issue #228 follow-up), because VDR sets the fp32 reduction order, so greedy text moves by design (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §1-§8, §68).  One macro gates both sides — the device-side `get_vec_dot_q_cuda()` selection and `get_vdr_mmvq()`'s value, which the host's K-split arithmetic reads — so the partition cannot disagree.  Measured on gfx1151 (Qwen3.8 Flash-Next Q5_K, 13 dense Q6_K tensors incl. the lm_head, batched-bench q8_0 KV): VDR=2 vs VDR=1 is tg128 33.86 vs 33.83, B=1 33.50 vs 33.48, B=4 81.76 vs 81.75, **B=8 104.14 vs 102.94 (+1.2 % for VDR=2)**, tg128 @ d16384 31.86 vs 31.86 — so VDR=2 stays the default and the flag costs nothing when off.  Width purity is unaffected either way (`plain == draft-mtp`, `n_max` 3 and 7 byte-identical on that payload).  The MoE-expert VDR is per kernel and is **not** affected by this flag. |
| `-DGGML_HIP_NO_DPP_XOR` | off (DPP on) | restores `__shfl_xor` for the wave32 warp reductions on RDNA3/RDNA4 (PR #110's DPP lane exchange, folded into block 15).  The DPP path is **bit-identical** and speeds up the HC_MIX / Flash-Next band (measured on gfx1201 / ROCm 7.14: BF16 HC_MIX 58.84 -> 45.26 us at 4 tokens, 100.00 -> 62.55 us at 8, i.e. +23 % / +37 %; bandwidth-bound matmuls unchanged).  **There is deliberately no runtime env var**: a device-side runtime gate was implemented and A/B-tested but reproducibly wedged the FA prefill at ~4096 tokens with MTP, so it was dropped. |

The `llama` C++ target compiles with `-Wshadow=local` under **GCC** and plain `-Wshadow` under **clang**
(block 00).  The container build's host compiler is GCC (the `rocm/dev-ubuntu` image default, unlike the
local `~/bin/build-llama-rocm`, which forces clang), and GCC's `-Wshadow` additionally reports
"declaration shadows a member" / "hides constructor" for the ubiquitous `Foo(int x) : x(x) {}` idiom.
`-Wshadow=local` keeps the class the gate exists for (a local shadowing a local) and drops that
member/global output; clang has no `=local` form.  The container's `RUN cmake` step zeroes ccache stats
(`ccache -z`) before the build, so its trailing `ccache -s` reports the rate of that build rather than
the lifetime average.

---

## 15. llama-server prompt cache (block 12)

The server keeps a host-side copy of a slot's context so the slot can be resumed after its KV cells were
reused.  The variables below govern *when* that copy is taken, whether its buffers are reused (r130,
PR #210) and whether the resident idle slots are parked instead of saved and cleared (r136, PR #225;
r146 moves a mirroring indexer cache with them, issue #227).
The copy itself is bounded by `--cache-ram` (MiB; `0` disables the prompt cache and the idle-slot saving
with it).

A **context checkpoint** is a different copy: on a hybrid/recurrent (or SWA) model the memory cannot roll
back, so the server saves the parts it cannot re-derive (`common_prompt_checkpoint`, up to
`--ctx-checkpoints`, default 32) and a prompt that resumes from a cached prefix restores one.
`LLAMA_SERVER_CKPT_REUSE` (r155, PR #236) governs that copy's reuse; it is not bounded by `--cache-ram`.

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LLAMA_SERVER_LAZY_IDLE_SLOTS` | **on** | kill-switch (r130, block 12) | with a **unified** KV cache (`--kv-unified`, which `--parallel auto` also selects) and `--cache-idle-slots` (on by default), an idle slot keeps its context when a new task starts, instead of being saved to the prompt cache and cleared on every task.  When the KV cache actually runs out of room, `try_clear_idle_slots` saves the **least recently used** idle slot to the prompt cache and only then clears it (it used to purge it without saving).  A restore that fails for want of cells still held by another idle slot moves idle slots out of the way one at a time, with the entry being restored held out of that eviction, and retries; the same hold covers the slot-selection save that runs before a restore.  Measured on gfx1201 with Qwen3.8-27B + DFlash2 and 1-4 slots: a conversation switch at 11.5K tokens 0.5 -> 0.2-0.3 s, and the PR's overflow case (a second conversation fills the unified KV while the first idles) 8.3 s recompute -> 0.5 s restore with 4 tokens evaluated (the reporter's 135 s -> 57 tokens at 110K).  Text is byte-identical across arms in the sequential runs; a different cache history places a sequence's KV in other cells, which is the `--kv-unified` non-determinism already documented in `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §38.  `0` restores the eager save-and-clear on every new task (the pre-r130 behaviour, and the only mode in which a slot that is never taken over is published to the cache).  Without a unified cache the flag only suppresses the per-task RAM copy: idle slots were never cleared there. |
| `LLAMA_SERVER_CACHE_REUSE` | **on** | kill-switch (r130, block 12) | the buffers of restored or evicted prompt-cache states are kept and reused for the next save (the smallest spare that fits), so a save does not allocate and zero-fill a multi-gigabyte `std::vector` every time (a zero-filled 3.8 GB vector costs about 2 GB/s to allocate and fault in, and 0.4 s to free, against about 8 GB/s for the device copy itself).  The spares count against `--cache-ram` **together with** the cached states, and the smallest spares are trimmed first.  `0` restores a fresh allocation per save.  Measured peak RSS on a repeat-save workload (six 13.4K-token prompts, `--cache-ram 4096`, 27B): 6938 MiB with reuse vs 6898 MiB without, i.e. bounded by the cache limit. |
| `LLAMA_SERVER_CACHE_HUGEPAGE` | **on** | kill-switch (r173, PR #248, block 12) | a **new** prompt-cache state buffer of 64 MiB or more is reserved, has its 2 MiB-aligned part advised `MADV_HUGEPAGE`, and only then is zero-filled, so a multi-GiB slot state is faulted in 2 MiB pages instead of 4 KiB ones (Linux only, and only where transparent huge pages are enabled in `madvise` mode — Ubuntu's default; with `never` the advice is a no-op and with `always` it is not needed).  A recycled spare buffer keeps the pages it already has, so this only affects the allocation `LLAMA_SERVER_CACHE_REUSE` could not serve.  It is the other half of that buffer cost: 4 KiB pages make the zero fill (not the copy) the dominant term of a whole-slot save.  Measured on this box (3.47 GiB buffer): alloc+zero 470 -> 125 ms, and the server's `prompt cache update took` for a 100K-token slot saving into a new 3.4 GiB buffer **828.1 -> 497.0 ms**; the recycled-buffer case is unchanged (292.6 -> 291.0 ms), as is everything copied.  `0` restores 4 KiB pages. |
| `LLAMA_SERVER_CKPT_REUSE` | **on** | kill-switch (r155, PR #236, block 12) | two things about a **context checkpoint** (not the prompt-cache copy above): (1) the checkpoint the current task restored is not saved again at the position it was just restored from, when nothing has been decoded since (the server used to restore a checkpoint and then write the identical state back before its first batch, logging `superseding context checkpoint`); (2) the buffers of erased, superseded and invalidated checkpoints are kept (up to 4, the largest kept) and an exact-size one is taken by the next checkpoint, so a save is only the copy and not an allocate + zero-fill + page-fault of the recurrent state's size.  Cost: up to 4 retained host buffers (150 + 21 MiB for Qwen3.8-27B with its DFlash2 draft, 50 MiB for Qwen3.5-4B), measured as +0.15 GiB (4B) and +0.45 GiB (27B) of RSS.  `0` restores the pre-r155 behaviour (always re-save, fresh buffer per save).  There are no arithmetic changes and no KV cell moves, so the greedy text is byte-identical in every arm measured (36 requests over 6 configs, including 2 slots + parking and a slot save/restore round trip).  Measured prompt time of a request that resumes from a checkpoint: 45.1 -> 31.4, 40.0 -> 28.7, 46.4 -> 32.4 ms on the 4B; 197.1 -> 163.2, 186.9 -> 161.9, 144.1 -> 112.0, 187.6 -> 126.7 ms on the 27B + DFlash2 at 2 GPUs and 407.9 -> 373.6, 291.2 -> 236.2, 182.4 -> 142.0, 251.7 -> 150.6 ms at 3 GPUs (the submitter's slower host measured 195-223 ms; page-fault cost is machine-dependent).  Only reaches a **hybrid/recurrent** target: on a dense (or SWA) memory the restore lands at `pos_max` = `n_tokens - 1`, so nothing is re-saved at the same position and the switch changes only the buffer reuse.  Gate: `scripts/gate-ckpt-reuse.sh`. |
| `LLAMA_SERVER_SPEC_CKPT_DEVICE` | **on** | kill-switch (r184, PR #254, block 12) | where the **target's speculative checkpoint** lives: the one the server saves before a verify whose draft is longer than `n_rs_seq` on a recurrent or hybrid target and restores when only part of the draft is accepted (with an n-gram drafter, the long-draft steps of any copy-heavy request).  In host memory that save is 149.6 MiB of Qwen3.8-27B state, ~4 ms on this box and ~16 ms on a slower CPU; the default passes `LLAMA_STATE_SEQ_FLAGS_ON_DEVICE`, so save and restore are device copies into one staging buffer per slot that the context keeps.  Device memory is used only when the target is on exactly one GPU, its state is offloaded and, at the slot's first checkpoint, that GPU has the state's size plus 512 MiB free (a lazily allocated buffer must not take the last of it); otherwise, and always under `-sm tensor` or a layer split, it stays in host memory (`--no-kv-offload` / `-ngl 0` too, which logs its own reason).  Decided once per slot and logged.  Cost: +150 MiB of VRAM per slot that takes a checkpoint on the 27B (+63 MiB on 35B-A3B), not visible to `--fit`.  Text, drafted and accepted tokens are identical in every arm.  Measured 1 GPU (gfx1201): 27B UD-Q4_K_XL + DFlash2 rewrite **310.2 -> 316.8 t/s (+2.1 %)**, 27B `ngram-mod` alone **+1.6 %**, 35B-A3B `-ncmoe 40` + DFlash2 **+4.3 %**, a plain prose request **0 %**; the win is (host save cost) x (checkpoints taken), so a slower host gains more (the submitter measured +10.0 % / +15.8 %).  `0` keeps host memory always.  Gate: `scripts/gate-spec-ckpt-device.sh`. |
| `LLAMA_SERVER_PARK_IDLE_SLOTS` | **on** | kill-switch (r136, block 12) | with a **unified** KV cache and the lazy idle slots, park each idle slot's cells at the top of the cache when a task starts, and move the slots that run back to the low cells (`llama_memory_seq_park()` in `llama-ext.h`, a device copy of the K/V rows and the cell state).  New cells and `get_n_kv()` then stop below the parked stack, so a request that runs alone attends over the same range at the same cell indices as with `LLAMA_SERVER_LAZY_IDLE_SLOTS=0`, which restores the one-at-a-time reproducibility that #210's resident idle slots had broken (27B UD-Q4_K_XL + DFlash2, 4 slots, 16 prompts x 2 rounds, `cache_prompt: false`: **16/16** inside a round and **32/32** equal to lazy-off, against 13/16 and 13/32 for the un-parked #210 arm).  A cache that cannot park (not one stream, cells shared with another cache or a draft, a transposed V, or a sliding window; `-fa off` is the common case) keeps #210's behaviour and logs `not supported` once.  A slot whose cells are not one run of its own (a cached prefix restored through `seq_cp`) falls back to save-and-clear, which is the lazy-off output; with `--no-cache-idle-slots` it is left resident instead.  `0` keeps #210 as it was.  **Concurrent decoding is still placement-dependent** (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §38); this only restores the one-at-a-time case.  On a model whose memory carries a second cache that mirrors the attention cells (qwen4exp/glm5-next's QSA indexer) the park moves **both**, or neither: a move the indexer cannot follow is refused and the slot falls back to save-and-clear (r146, issue #227).  Gate: `scripts/gate-park-idle-slots.sh`. |
| `LLAMA_KV_PARK_DEBUG` | unset / `0` | diagnostic (r136, block 12) | log every park, unpark and compaction move, and why one was refused (`seq_park: seq 0 [0, 241) -> [130831, 131072)`, `its 233 cells are not one run of its own`, `cell ... of the target ... is taken`, `no run of N free cells below ...`).  A memory with a mirroring second cache logs one line per cache per move (two identical ranges), and a park it refuses because the two no longer mirror each other logs `the indexer cache does not mirror the attention cache` (r146).  The same switch also logs the server's `cannot park the idle slot` fallback at INFO; without it the moves are silent.  This is a diagnostic only, never default-on. |

## 16. Backend sampling (`--backend-sampling`, block 12)

`llama-server --backend-sampling` sets a sampler chain on the context for each request (the chain's backend
prefix runs as part of the decode graph) and clears it when the slot is released; a request whose chain
cannot be offloaded (a grammar) clears it instead of setting it.  A **reserve** re-creates the scheduler and
with it every compute buffer, so `set_sampler()` asks for one only when it has to.

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LLAMA_BACKEND_SAMPLER_RESERVE_ONCE` | **on** | kill-switch (r169, contributor PR #245, block 12) | the compute buffers remember what they were reserved with (`sampling.reserved`: per sequence, the backend sampler chain's node count, `llama_sampler_backend_n_nodes()`) and `set_sampler()` reserves only when a sequence gets a **wider** chain than that, or has no record yet.  A chain set again, a narrower one, or a removal asks for none: the graph is rebuilt anyway (the samplers are part of the graph-reuse check), the output buffers are sized per decode, and the graph allocator grows a buffer if a chain needs more bytes.  The key is the **node count**, not the sequence id, because a reserve is the only thing that recomputes `graph_max_nodes()` — which sizes the graph's node capacity (`llm_graph_result::max_nodes` -> `ggml_new_graph_custom`), the scheduler's node/leaf arrays and the compute-metadata arena — and the allocator's growth covers buffer sizes only.  `0` restores the pre-r169 behaviour (a reserve on every set and every removal: 4 `sched_reserve: reserving ...` lines over three requests instead of 2, and +68.7 ms of median prompt time per sampled request on a 27B at `-c 65536 --parallel 2 --kv-unified`).  No arithmetic changes: request text and the prefill logits are byte-identical across the arms.  Only reaches a context that sets a backend sampler — `llama-cli`/`llama-bench` and a `-sm tensor` context (backend sampling is refused there) never touch it. |

## 17. The streamed KV (block 15): the resident window, the attendance pool and the MTP draft window

Block 15 carries **three** ways to move the attention KV out of VRAM, and they are easy to confuse because they
share vocabulary.  They are not layers: §17.0 and §17.1 are two **alternative** designs for the same bytes
(one copy tiered by address, versus a host-authoritative copy plus a device cache keyed by the sparse
selection), and §17.2 acts on a **different cache** entirely (the MTP drafter's) and **truncates** it.

**All three default to OFF (r247).**  Each one trades something for VRAM, and none of the trades is free:
a placement that changes decode by 10 % cannot be a default, and a default run must not carry a placement
decision at all.  `auto` (where it exists) means *the system picks the size*; `N` is explicit; the default is
neither.

| option | env form | default | what |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--kv-window [auto\|N]` | `LLAMA_ARG_KV_WINDOW` (`LLAMA_KV_RESIDENT_CELLS` = deprecated alias) | **`0`** (off) | §17.0.  **One copy**, tiered by address: the newest `N` cells stay in VRAM, everything older is pinned host RAM inside the same reservation.  Lossless and model-agnostic; a spilled cell is just read over PCIe (11-26 % once the request is deeper than the window).  `auto` = `--fit` sizes it |
| `--kv-attendance-pool [on\|off]` | `LLAMA_ARG_KV_REGION_POOL` (`--kv-region-pool` = alias) | **`off`** | §17.1.  **Two copies**: the attention KV's authoritative copy in a pinned host **mirror**, plus a compact device **pool** of indexer pages + a page table that the sparse QSA decode resolves through.  qwen4exp only, never with a window.  The pool's SIZE is derived from the model and is not a knob |
| `--spec-draft-kv-window [auto\|N]` | `LLAMA_ARG_SPEC_DRAFT_KV_WINDOW` | **`0`** (off) | §17.2.  The **MTP draft** cache is truncated to a sliding window of `N` cells per sequence.  Lossy (the drafter forgets), so it can only cost decode; measured -9.7 % on a `-sm tensor` split past the window.  `auto` = the cap or nothing |

The delivery's own options live in `llama_context_params` (`kv_resident_cells` / `kv_region_pool` /
`spec_draft_kv_window`, all `0` = off), so a consumer that builds against the library can set them without a
CLI.  The upstream-style `LLAMA_ARG_*` env vars still work because every `common/arg.cpp` option gets one --
they are that option's env form, not a second knob.

**Which one, when the KV does not fit** (they are alternatives, and the choice is about the reader):

* a **dense** read set (every model but qwen4exp/Flash-Next) reads the whole used prefix every step, so
  recency is a good predictor: `--kv-window` is the only option, and `--kv-window auto` lets `--fit` size it.
* a **sparse** read set (the QSA top-k reader) reads a scattered selection, where recency predicts nothing:
  `--kv-attendance-pool` keeps the authoritative copy in host RAM and caches exactly the selected pages
  (one DMA fill, then hits), at the cost of prompt throughput.  A window can serve that reader too (the
  `sparse` decode arm), but it pays a PCIe read **every step** instead of a fill per page.
* neither is a default, and enabling one declines the other, with a message naming it.

**`--fit` names them, and takes neither (r247).**  When the context you asked for does not fit, the fit lists
`--kv-window N` and `--kv-attendance-pool` with their measured costs, says they are alternatives, and stops there
— it will not take one on your behalf (a placement chosen for the user, at a cost the user never asked for, is what
produced the r246 → r247 regression).  When one of them is already active it is left out of the advice.

**r264: it only ever offers a placement the model and the devices can actually take.**  The pool needs the sparse
indexer geometry (a qwen4exp/glm5-next trunk) **and** a device whose memory is not a shared host/device pool, and a
tensor split has no per-device mirror; a placement the cache would refuse is not named at all (neither eligible →
no hint).  The advice is also a **WARN**, not part of the ERROR block: the over-budget facts are the errors.  This
is why an APU running a qwen35moe trunk no longer sees a `--kv-attendance-pool` paragraph it never asked for
(`TODO` #300 retired; `INVARIANTS.md` r264).


### 17.0 The resident window (`--kv-window`, `LLAMA_KV_RESIDENT_CELLS` = deprecated alias)

The KV cache's VRAM cost becomes a **resident window** of the newest `W` cells; the cells below it live in
pinned system RAM, in the same VA reservation (so no kernel and no op changes).  **Default: off** — with the
knob unset the KV takes the plain device buffer and the run is byte-identical to a build without the block,
including its OOM.  The opt-in semantics are deliberate: a window below the depth you actually run costs
11-26 %, so it is a decision, not a default.

| variable | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `--kv-window [auto\|N]` (env form `LLAMA_ARG_KV_WINDOW`; **`LLAMA_KV_RESIDENT_CELLS` is the deprecated alias**, still read and now warns) | **`0` = off** | `N` = keep the newest `N` cells in VRAM; `auto` = `--fit` sizes it (only for a `-c` the user pinned — the fit then reports the split, the per-token read cost and the depth below which nothing spills); `0` = off.  Promoted from the env var in **r247**, so the placement is visible in `--help` and per-run.  **A tensor split carries the same window since r204 (`TODO` #124):** one region per child device, all children at the same `W` cells, the grow fanned out to every child, and `--fit` sizes it against the split's per-device budget (before r204 the Meta device had no backend reg, so `-sm tensor` declined the window with one message -- r181).  A **MIRRORED** cache (dsv4 / HRM, where every device holds the whole cache) still declines it, with one message: one window would cost `n_devices x` and the pinned-spill plan is per logical cache.  `-sm layer` and one device are unaffected.  `auto` hands down three states to the cache, so "the fit decided the KV fits fully resident" is silent while "nobody decided" still warns (r181).  **`-nkvo` declines it** (one message): the window is a device region plus a pinned host tail, and a cache that is not device-offloaded is already pageable host RAM, so there is nothing to window -- and the streamed decode arm keys on the placement actually taken, not on the plan (r197). |
| `LLAMA_KV_SPILL_RAM_PCT` | **`85`** (**kill switch**: `0` disables the cap) | Cap the pinned spill at this % of `MemAvailable`.  At creation the window is **widened** until the worst case fits; at run time a mapping that would exceed it is refused loudly instead of being OOM-killed.  `--fit` only takes that widening while the wider window still meets its VRAM target: if the ceiling would demand a window that no longer fits VRAM, the fit reports it and drops the window (then fits the context by shrinking `-c`) rather than planning a spill bigger than the machine (r181).  **Windows (r209)**: `MemAvailable` comes from `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`, so the ceiling applies there too (it was skipped: no figure off Linux). |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION` | **on** (**kill switch**: `0` disables the provider) | Whether the region provider may be used at all.  It is only ever *taken* when a window is asked for, so this switch is for A/B testing and bisection. |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_QUARANTINE` | **on** (A/B switch: `0` = the bug) | Holds the physical a `cuMemUnmap` just released across the `cuMemCreate`/`cuMemMap` that follows.  **Without it a spilled KV is silently wrong** (the driver zeroes a neighbouring live host mapping).  Default on; `0` exists only to reproduce the hazard — never ship it in a server config. |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_DEBUG` | unset (presence) | Per-growth lines with the window's own state, a byte-exhaustive restore verify, a stage-out cross-check and a fingerprint of every host mapping.  **10-40x slower on a spilling arm — never quote a timing from it.** |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_TRACE` | unset (presence) | Every address-level map/unmap/memset/ensure.  One line per 4 MiB unit; for sizing, not for a hot loop. |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_CANARY` | unset (presence) | With `DEBUG`: writes a 0xAB pattern into a mapping right after its flip, so a later change proves something *wrote* there. |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_DUMP` | unset (path) | At region free, write the whole mapped extent to this file and print the maps/mapped/host totals.  `= /dev/null` is the cheap **spill proof** (`KVREGION_FREE … host=` > 0). |
| `GGML_CUDA_VMM_REGION_SELFTEST` | unset (presence) | Run the provider's selftest at backend init: prefix/span growth, a HOST-backed span, aligned window flips (a straddling request is refused), the **host-mapping hazard** (a whole flip sequence re-verified) and the watermark contract.  The verdict is an **ERROR** line on FAIL and a **WARN** line on PASS (r222: it used to be INFO, i.e. invisible at every default, so a FAIL passed a gate silently); a `llama-cli` arm needs `-v` to see the PASS, because that tool drops to error-only and the `--fit` probe wraps the logger as well.  Must print **PASS**, and must print **FAIL** with `LLAMA_KV_REGION_QUARANTINE=0` — `scripts/gate-kv-residency.sh` runs both arms itself. |
| `LLAMA_KV_REGION_SPILL_SKIP` | unset (**diagnostic only**) | Comma-separated tensor indices of a windowed buffer that stay fully resident; it bisected the A4 purity hunt and is not shipped behaviour. |



### 17.2 The MTP draft window (`--spec-draft-kv-window`, **default `0`** since r247; `auto` = cap or nothing since r246)

The MTP draft context carries its own KV, sized `-c` cells, although the drafter only ever reads its recent
prefix (one draft layer: 400 MiB at `-c 102400` on the 27B, ~1 GiB at `-c 262144`).  This makes it a sliding
window.  **It is off by default since r247, and the old claim that it "measures non-negative everywhere" was
wrong**: a window the request outgrows costs MTP decode, measured at **−9.7 %** on a `-sm tensor` split at
~105k tokens (65 536-cell `auto` window 72.7 t/s against 80.5 off, text byte-identical), through both a lower
draft acceptance (0.500 → 0.453) and a per-step cost past the ring wrap that *grows* with the window
(104 960 cells: 67.8 t/s).  It is a **fittability** feature — the freed VRAM reaches the expert arena — so it
is a conscious opt-in, with the caveat measured and recorded (`benchmarks/2026-11-10-mtp-draft-window-depth.md`).

| option | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `--spec-draft-kv-window` | **`0`** (off) | `N` = a window of `N` cells **per sequence** (floored at one ubatch, and the window is dropped when it would hold the whole cache); `0` = a full `-c` draft cache, byte-identical to a pre-r193 build; `auto` = **the cap or nothing**: `min(65536, ctx)` cells, and if that does not leave one batch of room below every sequence's own context the window is **declined** rather than lowered (the log says so once).  The cap is where the window stops buying anything -- at or above the context the drafter works over it changes no draft, and every cell below it that it masks is a decode cost (measured −9.7 % of MTP decode on a `-sm tensor` split at ~105k tokens when the request outgrows it — `benchmarks/2026-11-10-mtp-draft-window-depth.md`).  **r246 replaced the quarter-of-the-context sizer**: that policy took `n_ctx_seq/4` clamped to `[32768, 65536]`, so at `-c 131072` it picked 32 768 -- a quarter of the context, below the depth a 62k request reaches -- and cost 8 % of decode on both a pre- and a post-re-base build (issue #293).  **MTP only, and only an embedded MTP head** (qwen3.5/3.6/3.8): a separate MTP draft GGUF (qwen4exp/Flash-Next) keeps its full cache.  `-c`-sized draft KV, `n_swa` = the window; the target still verifies every token, so a truncated draft can only be slower.  **The window is per sequence:** under `--kv-unified` the one draft cache holds every sequence, so its cell count is `(win + one batch) x n_seq_max` (capped at the context) and that is what the log line and the reserve report -- the r193-r199 `win + n_ubatch` shape starved a second slot (issue #264), and a window that cannot fit per sequence is declined rather than lowered.  **`--fit` sees it**: an embedded MTP head with an active window derives its slab reserve from the model's own metadata with the weights zeroed, `aux 3.50 -> 0.76 GiB` (arena +2.8 GiB); without a window, or for a separate draft GGUF, the flat 3584 MiB constant stays.  **The window is a TRUE ring since r248 (issue #297):** reclaim is by POSITION, so it frees nothing when a batch's positions do not advance -- exactly what an M-RoPE image chunk does (every row of an image shares one temporal position), which used to exhaust the `win + one batch` spare room mid-chunk and fail the whole request (`failed to find a memory slot` → HTTP 500 `failed to process mtmd chunk`).  Now, when position cannot place a batch, the drafter's cache evicts its own OLDEST cells (a batch-sized shortfall only, and never a cell at or above a position the batch writes), which costs a little draft acceptance and no token: the drafter's cache was already lossy by design and the target verifies every proposal.  It is one INFO line per run (`draft window ring eviction is active`), then silent.  A model's own sliding-window attention never does this.  **And a drafter failure can no longer fail a request (r248):** a draft decode that fails drops the draft KV and retries once, and if it still fails only that sequence stops drafting until the next request (`drafting is disabled for the rest of this request`) -- the target decodes the tokens itself, so the text is unchanged. |

Measured **with the window opted in** (1 GPU, `-n 2000`/`-n 3000`, static depth 3 — i.e. a request depth
the window covers, which is where it pays): 35B-A3B Q8_0 `-ncmoe 48 -c 262144` arena
20 910 -> 23 715 MiB (residency 64.1 -> 72.7 %), decode 111.2 -> 113.7 t/s, prefill +3.4 %, **drafts
identical**; dense 27B `-c 102400` Protocol A neutral (71.9 vs 72.1 layer, 72.6 vs 72.5 tensor, acceptance
2083/2747 in all four arms); target text byte-identical off/auto/4096 and plain == draft-mtp == draft-mtp
(`W=512`) at `-c 16384`.  A unified 2-slot cache sizes the window per sequence, and the windowed drafter's
text is byte-identical to `LLAMA_SPEC_DRAFT_WINDOW=0` on both slots of `--kv-unified -np 2` (issue #264).
Records: `patches/README.md` block 15, `WORKLOG.md` 261031.014 and 261101.006,
`archive/work/kv-residency/O3-DRAFTER-RING.md` §2.3.


### 17.1 The attendance pool (`--kv-attendance-pool`, alias `--kv-region-pool`; **default off**)

**It is a cache, not a spill.**  The attention KV lives twice: its **authoritative** copy in a **pinned host
mirror** (cell-indexed, so the
writers, `state_write`/`state_read`, the K-shift, defrag and parking all work on it unchanged) and a compact
**device pool** of pages of one indexer block in VRAM, with a page table `block -> slot`.  A decode step
resolves its top-k selection through the table and fills the pages it missed from the mirror; the QSA reader
gathers K/V at the pool slots while the mask stays cell-indexed.  **Default: off** — with the variable unset
the KV takes the plain device buffer and the run is byte-identical to a build without the block, including its
OOM.  Opt-in is deliberate: it is a **fittability** feature like block 15 (a 204 MiB pool instead of a 1.6-2.4
GiB resident KV at 103k-180k depth), and on a config whose KV already fits it costs the sparse-vs-dense decode
gap (7-12 % at depth, `patches/README.md` block 15).

**It also costs the PROMPT, and that is the bigger number (r216).**  The page-fill path is per *decode* step;
a prompt chunk writes every cell of its range and reuses nothing, so on one RX 7900 XTX at `-c 204800` with
this model `LLAMA_KV_REGION_POOL=16384` read **427.5 t/s at pp32K against 829.3** with the plain device KV
(-48 %, and 409.2 against 808.2 at pp113K), for +12 % decode (59.4 against 52.8 t/s over 192 tokens) -- the
decode being exactly the 2.3 GiB of expert arena the pool frees (12 084 against 9 742 MiB).  The KV of this
model is ~13 KiB per token, i.e. ~2.6 GiB at `-c 204800`, which a 24 GiB card can hold; the pool's own case
does not arise until the KV does not fit.  **Do not enable it on a prompt-heavy server**, and never take it for
a decode default on one: block 15 Goal A (`LLAMA_KV_RESIDENT_CELLS`, the window) is the placement that buys the
arena back with no kernel change.  `WORKLOG.md` 261105.001.

| variable | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `--kv-attendance-pool [on\|off]` (alias `--kv-region-pool`; env form `LLAMA_ARG_KV_REGION_POOL`) | **`off`** | `on` takes the pool, `off` (the default) keeps the plain device KV — byte-identical to a build without the block, including its OOM.  **The size is NOT a knob (r247).**  It is derived from the model: `QSA_DECODE_BAND (8) x ceil((indexer_top_k + page - 1) / page)` cells, clamped to the cache's own cell count — one decode band of the indexer working set, which is the minimum that lets a batch resolve without evicting a page it claimed in the same launch.  At the measured Flash-Next shape that is **4 104 pages = 16 416 cells (204 MiB)** against a 6 441-page working set, i.e. the forced misses are built in, and the gate measures **89-91 %** per-id hits.  The old numeric request only ever bought ~1 point of hit rate for 2x the VRAM (8 192 pages: 90.9 %; the derived floor: 89.9 %), and `auto` had nothing to size it (`TODO` #313, **closed in r247**) — so a legacy numeric value or `auto` is **accepted and IGNORED**, with a WARN saying so.  **Per layer and per device** (r213): on a `-sm layer` split every device gets its own pinned host mirror buffer type, page table and counter block, so a multi-device cache is N small pools, not one shared one (a **tensor split** declines -- its Meta device has no per-device mirror).  Declines (one message, KV unchanged) on anything but a single-stream, non-MLA, uniformly-geometried, device-offloaded qwen4exp trunk cache with no window active, and when the pinned mirror would exceed `LLAMA_KV_SPILL_RAM_PCT`.  **NOT SUPPORTED on a shared host/device pool (an integrated GPU), by design (r264, `TODO` #300 retired):** the pool buys device memory back from a *separate* host tier, and on an APU the KV is already in the memory the GPU reads, so there is nothing to buy back and no two-tier model for the resolve — one WARN, KV unchanged, and `--fit` never offers it there (`INVARIANTS.md` r264).  **`--fit` sees the pool**: the mirror is charged to host RAM and only the pool to VRAM, so a context that only fits with the pool is accepted with it (r190).  **Prompt time:** the page-fill path runs over a whole chunk with no reuse, which is ~48 % of pp32K on a KV that fits -- see the paragraph above (r216); decode favours it, prompt does not. |
| `GGML_CUDA_POOL_TRACE` | unset (presence) | **Diagnostic (POSIX only, compiled out on Windows).** Records a backtrace for every live device-pool block and, when the pool's LIFO invariant is violated, prints the stack of the block being freed and of the block that should have been freed first.  It costs a backtrace per temporary allocation, so it is a triage switch, never a run mode.  The assert itself always prints the sizes/offsets (that part needs no switch). |
| `GGML_CUDA_POOL_STACK_CENSUS` | unset = off (r240) | **Diagnostic.** Once per graph per device, before the per-graph caches are yielded, prints what the device pool stack still holds: `POOL_STACK_CENSUS dev=N live=<entries> dead=<entries>/<MiB> bytes=<MiB> \| hc=<live>/<MiB> made=<copies made> mmb=<live>/<MiB> bf16=<live>/<MiB>`.  The per-kind counts are **live** entries; `dead` is the killed-but-blocked pile (a LIFO pool frees a killed block only when it reaches the top) and `bytes` is what the graph really still holds.  A pool block that only has to live for one statement (the `hc_combine_norm` base copy) must be `hc=0` at that point; `made` says the matcher really produced copies, so `hc=0` is not a vacuous arm; `bf16` is the BF16 src1 cache and `mmb` the MMB activation cache, which hold **one** block and `cache_max` (4 on RDNA4) blocks per graph, so anything above that is churn.  It is a WARN, so a `llama-cli` census arm needs `-lv 2` (`-lv 4` also works).  Issue #290 (a base copy per layer held to the end of a `-sm layer` prefill graph) is exactly the `hc` column; the gate is `scripts/gate-hc-base-copy.sh`. |
| `LLAMA_KV_POOL_VERIFY` | unset (presence) | The oracle: every page selected by a decode step is compared byte-for-byte with the mirror at read time and a mismatch is counted (the teardown line reports `bad`).  Expected **0**.  It costs a second pass over the selected pages, so never quote a timing from this arm. |
| `LLAMA_KV_POOL_STATS` | unset (**a path**) | At every cache teardown append one line -- pages x cells, layers, **counter blocks**, claims, misses, ids, hit %, dirty re-fills, resolves, `bad` -- to this file.  The counters are **summed over the per-device blocks** (r213), so a multi-device run reports the events of every device on one line and `counter blocks` says how many were summed (1 on a single device).  A **path**, not a flag: a line on stderr would land between the generated text and `llama-cli`'s perf line and break `scripts/extract-generated.py`.  Reads the counters back once, so it is not default-on. |
| `LLAMA_KV_POOL_MIRROR_UNIT_MIB` | **`1`** | The mirror's **grow unit** in MiB (r196).  The mirror reserves its full `-c` as VA but pins only the units the run's cell watermark reaches, one `cudaHostRegister` per unit; a larger value pins more per tensor up front (a short run in a big `-c` then costs more RAM), a tiny one is the "grow on every step" diagnostic arm. |

The mirror is **grown on demand** (r196, `TODO` #123): the buffer type reserves the whole `-c` as one
page-aligned `mmap` range but pins only the units a run actually reaches, so a 5 298-token run in a
`-c 262144` context pins **72 MiB** instead of 3264.  Every write grows first (a state restore included) and a
read above the pinned extent is refused loudly; `LLAMA_KV_SPILL_RAM_PCT` (block 15's ceiling) bounds it.
Parking on a pooled cache **declines** to the server's save-and-clear fallback: a park packs to the top of the
cache, so growing the mirror to its destination would pin the whole context again.


## 19. Host-read transport (block 12, `TODO` #306, r262)

Which TRANSPORT serves a per-expert host read (`--host-experts pool`): the model **mapping** (a `memcpy` out of
the page cache), a **buffered `pread`** through the page cache, or **`O_DIRECT`**.  All three read the same bytes,
so the choice can only change timing -- proven: greedy text `sha=de81b28cab7d` on every arm, and the MTP
four-axis acceptance is identical to five decimal places across the arms.

`--load-mode` names the transport as well: **`buffered` is new** (a *mapped* master whose host reads are buffered
`pread`s -- the combination that used to be unrepresentable).  `mmap` / `buffered` / `dio` **lock** the arm;
`auto` (the default) is the adaptive one; `none` / `mlock` / `mmap+mlock` keep their meaning.

| variable | default | class | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TRANSPORT` | `auto` via `--load-mode` | pin / A/B | `map`, `file`, `dio` lock one transport; `auto` is the ladder below.  Wins over `--load-mode`. |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_STALL_K` | `400` (= 4.0x) | tuning | a **stall** is a read that took K times this arm's own running mean, so nothing here is a latency or a rate in absolute units |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_STALL_N` | `4` | tuning | consecutive stalls before the ladder moves |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_PROBE_FILLS` | `128` | tuning | every Nth **fill is served by the next arm** instead (the probe-fill A/B: same bytes, no extra I/O) |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_PROBE_GAIN` | `150` (= 1.5x) | tuning | the next arm must win by this much, `PROBE_N` samples running |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_PROBE_GAIN_DIO` | `300` (= 3.0x) | tuning | entering `O_DIRECT` needs a bigger win: it bypasses the page cache, so a single-read win can be a mirage |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_PROBE_N` | `3` | tuning | consecutive probe wins that latch the next step |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_SRC` / `_DEMAND_SRC` | unset | **diagnostic** | `file` moves only the pool fill / only the bounce read.  The static split the campaign measured with; the shipping control is `MOE_HOST_POOL_TRANSPORT` |
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_TR_START` | unset | **diagnostic** | start on a named arm (`map`/`file`/`dio`) while still monitoring -- the only way to stage an escalation test |

**`auto` is one way.**  It starts optimistically on the **mapping** (the arm whose advantage is cache-dependent,
so it should be sampled rather than predicted) and falls `map -> file -> dio`, never back: a box that fell short
once is likely to again, and the page cache a reverse switch would rebuild may be the thing that caused the
shortfall, so a slower consistent point beats a flapping optimum.  Every transition is logged with its evidence
(`stall: ...` / `probe fill: ...`), and the exit census reports the arm, the switch count and the probe/fill split
-- a benchmark arm that moves is NOT reproducible, so pin the transport when measuring.

**Measured (r262).**  Unpressured, the three arms are equal end to end (decode 25.9-26.2 t/s on the 56 GiB rig;
O_DIRECT 11.2).  Under pressure the mapping collapses -- fills **1.5-1.6 GiB/s, decode 5.5-6.3 t/s** in a 32G
scope -- where the buffered arm holds **12.8-12.9** and `auto` reaches it on the stall detector.  `batched-bench
-npl 1,4,8` is neutral at width.  Gate: `scripts/gate-host-read-transport.sh`.

**The coupling.**  A per-expert buffered read without the coalesced prefill span is the measured **-41 %** pair, so
`MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_SPAN=0` **refuses** the buffered arm (it falls back to the mapping) and the ladder steps over
`file` to `dio`.  Nothing an operator can type lands in that pair.

## 20. The UMA GTT expert store (block 16) — an APU's MoE expert set in one registered region

On an **integrated GPU** "VRAM" and host RAM are one pool, so r236 refuses the host-expert tier there
(`-ncmoe`/`--host-experts` are inert) and upstream's answer is to disable the expert cache outright: a MoE model
whose expert set exceeds RAM simply cannot run.  Block 16 adds the tier that can: **ONE anonymous region**
(`mmap` + `MADV_HUGEPAGE`, `hipHostRegister(Mapped|ReadOnly)` once, `mlock`'ed, sliced per `(tensor, expert)`) that
the GPU reads in place at 230-233 GB/s.  **`-lme N|auto` sizes it** — the same flag, units and heat ranking as the
discrete host-expert budget — and the shape follows that budget: a budget that covers the whole set **places** it
(static, no eviction); one below it takes the **arena** (the store is the cache's slot storage, the expert tensors
stay as holes in the read-only model mapping, LFRU residency serves every read, a miss is one buffered `pread` into
the vacated slot).  The variables below are the tier's knobs; **`-lme` is the size and it is also the opt-in for
streaming** (without it a model whose weights do not fit RAM is refused at load with the fix rather than placed and
left to swap).  Everything here is inert on a discrete GPU, measured: zero census lines with the env forced on, and
`scripts/gate-host-pool.sh` 42/42 with the same build.

| variable | default | class | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT` | `1` | **kill-switch** | `0` = no GTT store at all: the delivery's previous behaviour and log, byte for byte |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_RO` | `1` | **kill-switch** | `0` = register the region writable instead of `ReadOnly` (the measured 232 -> 60 GB/s A/B) |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_MLOCK` | `1` | **kill-switch** | `0` = do not `mlock`.  A registered-only region is RECLAIMABLE: under pressure the kernel splits its huge pages and swaps parts of it out, and the next GPU read stalls 12-37x while the driver faults them back (measured). Diagnostic/bisection only |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_ARENA` | unset = the budget decides | **override (tri-state)** | `1` = force the arena, `0` = force placement, unset = **arena iff the store cannot hold the whole expert set**.  The default is the budget because `-lme` caps NON-SWAPPABLE memory: only the arena keeps that promise (the excess streams from the reclaimable page cache).  Note the old parse was presence-only, so `=0` used to ENABLE it |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_PREFILL` | `1` | **kill-switch** | `0` = the per-expert used-expert copy for the prefill band (block 16's streaming read is the default) |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_FILL_DIRECT` | `1` | **kill-switch** | `0` = the buffered fill goes through the per-table bounce ring instead of straight into the arena slot (no bounce, no H2D on the direct arm) |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_PREFILL_READERS` | `8` | tuning | reader-pool width for the coalesced prefill spans.  `1` reads inline on the caller (the parallel-transport A/B).  Contends with `-t` for CPU |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_PREFILL_VERIFY` | unset | **diagnostic** | byte-compare every coalesced span against the pageable model mapping and log the first mismatch (a shard-relative offset or an `O_DIRECT` slip shows up here and nowhere else) |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_PREFILL_FAULT` | unset | **diagnostic** | make every span read fail, so every window takes the per-expert source (the gate's fault arm) |
| `MOE_EXPERT_GTT_PREFILL_DEBUG` | unset | **diagnostic** | one line per (table, ubatch): used / resident / read / arena / fallback |
| `MOE_HOST_LIMIT_FRAC` | `80` | tuning | the `auto` ceiling as a % of `MemAvailable`, shared with the discrete arm (measured: `-lme auto` is the only arm that sizes to the whole set on a fitting model) |
| `MOE_EXPERT_SEED` | **`eager`** | **opt-in (measured arm)** | **r270 (`TODO` #318): how much of either tier's sizing fill happens up front.**  `eager` (default, = the pre-r270 behaviour) fills the arena from the persisted heat prior + prompt tally and the host pool from the same prior at sizing.  `auto` = the APU policy applied to BOTH tiers: a tier that holds a **subset** (`slots < n_experts`) fills on first miss and keeps the prior for RANKING only; a tier that covers its tensor is PLACED at sizing.  `lazy` = defer a covering tier too.  **Measured on both tiers and neither is a default** (`benchmarks/2026-11-12-expert-seed-policy.md`): the seed is the *prefetch*, so deferring it does not save the bytes (storage reads are 45-47 GiB in every arm on the discrete tier) — it moves them into the request.  Discrete pool 32 GiB / 40 GiB set: prompt eval **0.88 s -> 7.24 s** on a 57-token prompt, decode flat, wall -3 %.  Discrete MIRROR pool (the whole set): `lazy` costs TTFT +5.1 s, prompt eval **0.68 -> 8.01 s** and decode **49.2 -> 40.0 t/s (-18.6 %)** because a covering pool with holes stands the device remap's cold source down, while `auto` is inert there (13.30 s / 49.44 t/s / remap served, against 13.58 / 49.17 / served).  APU GTT arena (`-lme 70` of a 79 GiB set): TTFT **-39 %** and a 32-token answer -12 % of the whole run, but decode **11.7 -> 4.8 t/s** and +70 GiB read over a 250-token answer.  So the window for `auto` is a one-shot APU prompt with a very short answer; a server or a long answer loses.  `eager` reproduces r269 exactly (text, hit rates, decode) on both tiers |

**The store is sized by `-lme`, never by a variable.**  The extent is clamped to `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK`, a model that fits
in RAM *declines* the store when the limit buys nothing useful, and a model that cannot fit is **refused** at load
(fast, before a byte is read) when the store cannot be locked.  `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_TOUCH` is a **documented no-op on
this tier** (every miss fills: the pageable mapping is not a GPU-readable cold source), and
`MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` / the movable-boundary slab / the pinned host pool do not apply — the census says so once.

**Measured (r263).**  A **123.45 GiB model runs on a 123 GiB box**; the buffered fill straight into the slot is
+15.6 % decode warm and the RDNA3_5 UMA start arm +13.7 % warm / +56-67 % on a >RAM model; prefill streaming is
+63-67 % on a >RAM model with the logits unmoved (mean KLD -0.000000, same-top-p 99.990 %).  Gate:
`scripts/gate-uma-experts.sh` (16 arms, 40 checks).

## 18. Optional artifacts (`optional/`) — **not part of the delivery**

These exist only in a checkout made with `scripts/apply-all.sh --include-optional` (`[y/N]` per artifact) or
`--include-optional-all`; the delivery itself never reads them.  They are catalogued here because a consumer
who applies an optional artifact is running these code paths and needs the same default/class discipline
(`optional/README.md`).

`optional/host-pool-fill-src` (the second half of issue #281):

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_SRC` | unset = the pool's **file** (buffered `pread`) | kill-switch (value) | (**SUPERSEDED in r262 by the delivery's `MOE_HOST_POOL_TRANSPORT` / `--load-mode buffered`** -- §19.  Kept because the artifact still applies to older trees.) `map` restores the pre-r232 fill: one `memcpy` per expert out of the read-only model mapping.  Both read the same page-cache pages, so the pool contents are identical and only the transport changes — a mapped fill on Windows is a demand fault per 4 KiB page (715 MiB/s measured) where a buffered `pread` gets the OS readahead.  **Keep `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_SPAN` on:** this arm is +38 % *on top of* the coalesced span and −41 % with the span off.  Inert where the disk tier is on (an O_DIRECT fill already reads the file), where the arena serves the table, and where the pool file I/O is a stub.  `optional/host-pool-fill-src` only. |

`optional/two-lane-prefill` (from PR #268/#279/#296, @slavap):

| variable | default | class | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LLAMA_PREFILL_LANES` | `1` | opt-in (value) | `2` enables the two-lane prefill (two consecutive prefill ubatches on two backend schedulers, splits submitted alternately).  Anything else, a layout the artifact cannot reach, or a **draft context** logs one WARN and stays single-lane.  **Read and refused under `-sm tensor` (one backend) and under plain `-sm layer` (pipeline parallelism on)**; `-sm row` does not load on ROCm at all, so `-sm layer` + any `-ot` is the only reachable layout — the reachability table is in `optional/two-lane-prefill/README.md`.  A **target** context arms even when its ubatches carry `nextn` rows (an MTP target pairs); a **draft** context never arms — `ctx_other` for DFlash/EAGLE3/Gemma4-assistant/qwen4exp, `ctx_type == MTP` for an MTP draft, which the fork does not mark with `ctx_other`.  The WARN reports which field refused it: `ignored (n_gpu = …, pipeline_parallel = …, draft = …)`.  Arming a layer-input draft's *target* still costs ~1-4 % prefill even though nothing pairs there. |
| `LLAMA_LANES_SPLIT_MIN` | `1024` | opt-in (value) | minimum batch size in tokens for the two-lane sub-ubatch split; `0` disables the split (whole-ubatch pairing only, the r220 behaviour).  The split is gated exactly like the pair, so an F1 layer-input workload declines both; an MTP (nextn) target now takes both.  `optional/two-lane-prefill` only. |
| `LLAMA_LANES_SPLIT_FRAC` | `0.55` | tuning (value) | fraction of the batch the first sub-ubatch takes (clamped to [0.1, 0.9], rounded up to 64, the chunked-GDN chunk).  `optional/two-lane-prefill` only. |
| `GGML_SCHED_PAIR_DEBUG` | `0` | diagnostic (value) | `1` logs each pair decision; `>= 2` also runs the cache-hazard self-test; `>= 3` logs per-lane/per-backend GPU event spans (this is the only consumer of `ggml_backend_event_elapsed_time`, and it makes the backend create **timing** events — that cost is why it is not the default). |
| `GGML_SCHED_PAIR_HAZARD` | on | diagnostic (value) | `0` keeps only the split-name guard and skips the cache-hazard analysis of the two lanes.  A/B only: the hazard analysis is what makes a mis-matched pair run one-after-the-other instead of racing on the KV cache. |

## Maintaining this file

The tables were derived from the code, so re-derive rather than trust them after a block change:

```bash
# every variable the delivery adds or repoints, with the idiom used
for f in ~/llama-cpp-rdna-boosts/patches/*.patch; do
  grep -hoE 'getenv *\( *"[A-Za-z0-9_]+"|GGML_ENV_STR *\( *"[A-Za-z0-9_]+"|env_int *\( *"[A-Za-z0-9_]+"' "$f"
done | grep -oE '"[A-Za-z0-9_]+"' | tr -d '"' | sort -u
```

Then, for each name, grep the tree for its usage.  **Do not** classify by name alone: `*_DISABLE_*` is
not consistently value-gated, and `*_FUSE_*` semantics changed from opt-in to kill-switch.  A variable
that is genuinely new should follow the repo's conventions: default-on if it helps (with the variable as
a *kill-switch* that restores the old behaviour), default-off only for a known-risky correctness
workaround, and a first line in the relevant block's `patches/README.md` note.
